<a id="section-1"></a>

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the production-readiness work that Pickup Lane must complete and continue to satisfy after launch. It is the authoritative list of parent passes and their required outcomes.

Each parent pass owns one production outcome. A pass may later be divided into child plans, but a child plan cannot remove, weaken, or silently defer a requirement in this document.

The [Production Launch Pass Completion Log](production-launch-pass-completion-log.md) records completed parent passes and their completion dates. Discoverable parent records under `docs/production-readiness/pass-records/` (named `p01.md` through `p21.md`) record requirement-level verification, child ownership, deferred obligations, and links to detailed evidence. Pass 03 establishes the planned requirement-to-verification mapping in `docs/production-readiness/verification-map.md`; later passes keep it consistent with their actual evidence. Individual pass plans record the engineering approach for work that remains. This document contains neither progress history nor implementation commentary.

<a id="section-2"></a>

## 2. System Boundary

The production-readiness program covers:

- the player-facing web application;
- the administrative web application;
- the backend API;
- PostgreSQL data and migrations;
- Firebase Authentication and App Check;
- Stripe payment, refund, and webhook behavior;
- durable background work;
- Cloudflare R2 venue-image storage;
- build, test, release, deployment, monitoring, backup, and recovery systems; and
- operational access, moderation, privacy, and incident procedures required to run those systems.

The program assumes this authority split:

- Firebase Authentication establishes user identity.
- PostgreSQL owns application profiles, roles, account state, resource ownership, bookings, capacity, moderation state, and application records.
- Stripe is the authority for Stripe-side payment and refund state.
- Pickup Lane is the authority for booking and capacity state.
- Cloudflare R2 stores approved venue images but does not determine who may upload, replace, or delete them.

A value copied from Firebase or Stripe must not become a second authority for identity, authorization, booking capacity, or Stripe payment state.

<a id="section-3"></a>

## 3. Requirement and Evidence Rules

### 3.1 Requirement form

Each requirement in this master must identify:

1. the application component or operation covered;
2. the behavior that must occur;
3. the result that must be observable when the requirement is satisfied; and
4. the outcomes that the implementation must prevent.

When a requirement applies only after an event or under a condition, it must name that event or condition. When a boundary or exception exists, the requirement must state what is outside its scope and why. An unconditional requirement must not invent an activating event, and a requirement with no exception must not invent one.

A child plan must preserve the meaning of every master requirement it covers. It must map each implementation step and verification step to the requirement it satisfies. Information may be stated once and referenced by multiple requirements only when the reference is direct and the shared information applies without modification to every referencing requirement.

A child plan must cite each exact upstream requirement it depends on; citing only the upstream pass is insufficient.

Terms and phrases such as “when necessary,” “as needed,” “where appropriate,” “if applicable,” “where relevant,” “reasonable,” “adequate,” “sufficient,” “properly handled,” and “appropriate safeguards” do not define an obligation by themselves. A requirement or plan that uses one of those terms must also define the condition, decision rule, or measurable result that determines what must happen.

### 3.2 What verification must prove

Verification must show what was checked, what was expected, what actually happened, and whether anything the requirement says must not happen occurred.

Each verification record must state:

1. the exact requirement and scenario being checked, including the starting state and input that matter to the result;
2. how the scenario was exercised or inspected;
3. the result that was expected and how that result could be observed;
4. the result that was actually observed, including the returned response, saved database changes, change made in Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, or another named service, or user-visible behavior that establishes the result; and
5. everything the requirement says must not happen that could occur in that scenario, how each outcome was checked, and what that check found.

A command name, test name, screenshot, checked box, or statement that something was tested does not establish completion by itself. If the required result or an outcome that must not happen cannot be observed through the recorded method, that method does not verify the requirement.

Before Pass 20, active mutation, failure, timeout, concurrency, capacity, permission, deletion, and recovery exercises must use an isolated release-configuration build of the revision being checked, with synthetic records (invented records unrelated to production people, accounts, or payments) and non-production Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL, hosting, notification, and monitoring resources. Development-stage evidence proves that revision and the configuration exercised; it does not prove the final production release candidate or an undecided production setting. Pass 20 must repeat each applicable active check using the exact production release candidate and final configuration in an isolated system with synthetic records and non-production resources. The same isolation and prohibited-effect evidence rules apply at both stages.

There are two exceptions: a numbered scenario may expressly require read-only inspection of a named production setting, or it may label an operation as a production-safe check. A production-safe check must be unable to charge a customer, exhaust or materially consume limited capacity, disclose private information, change a real user's or administrator's state, alter non-test production data, interrupt delivery or recovery, or make a real request fail.

When the final external service cannot be reproduced safely, the scenario must use the production inspection, service-documentation, and isolated-response procedure in Section 3.3.

### 3.3 Settings that cannot be chosen until required information exists

In this section, a setting means a number, deadline, interval, limit, threshold, or a rule that requires an action after a named event. The correct production setting cannot always be chosen immediately. It may depend on a production service that has not been selected, capacity that has not been configured, workload that has not been measured, or a business, privacy, or legal decision that has not been made.

These settings include:

- how many database connections the API and the parts of the application that run background jobs may keep open at the same time;
- how many background jobs may run at the same time;
- how many times a failed operation may be retried and how long the system waits between attempts;
- how long a request, database operation, or external-service request may run before it is stopped;
- how much data a request or upload may contain;
- how frequently an operation may be performed;
- the measured result and length of time that cause an alert;
- the maximum allowed completion time, computer-processing time, memory use, or downloaded data for a page, request, or background job;
- the maximum data loss and downtime allowed after a failure;
- how long records and stored files are kept; and
- how often access, recovery, and incident procedures must be reviewed or exercised.

#### Settings already fixed by a recorded decision

A setting fixed by a recorded decision remains in effect unless one of the following occurs:

- a selected service changes the maximum capacity or use it permits;
- a new business decision changes the accepted effect of failure or delay;
- a new legal or privacy requirement conflicts with the setting; or
- an incident or verification result shows that the setting cannot be enforced or allows an outcome prohibited by the requirement that governs the setting.

When one of these events occurs, the pass containing the requirement that governs the setting must review it before the affected production change is approved or, after an incident or failed verification, before the affected production operation continues. The existing setting may remain only when the review records that it does not conflict with the new information, stays within every service maximum that controls it, and still prevents every prohibited outcome named by that requirement. Otherwise, a new recorded decision must replace it.

The request-body limits in Pass 06 are already fixed. They must not be treated as undecided or recalculated through this section merely because they are numbers.

#### Requirements that must wait for missing decisions

Before implementation planning or implementation begins for a requirement governed by this section, perform a pre-plan scope review. That review must determine whether missing production information could change any of the following:

- the code or data design;
- the application component or external service that enforces the setting;
- the way the setting is supplied to the application or service;
- the deployment or operating procedure; or
- the environment, method, or expected result used to verify the requirement.

If missing information could change any item in that list, the affected requirement must remain unstarted. Its parent pass record under `docs/production-readiness/pass-records/` must identify:

1. the exact requirement that cannot start;
2. the exact production-service, capacity, workload, business, privacy, or legal information or decision that is missing; and
3. the observable condition that permits implementation planning and implementation to begin.

The requirement may start only after the information or decision named in item 2 has been recorded and satisfies the start condition named in item 3. A guessed infrastructure limit, assumed service behavior, or implementation intended to be replaced after the missing decision is made cannot be used to start the work or claim partial completion.

Work may begin before the exact production setting is known only when choosing it later will require entering the setting in configuration and changing nothing else. This is allowed only when:

- the same Pickup Lane component will enforce the same required behavior for every infrastructure option still under consideration;
- the configuration field that will receive the exact production setting is already defined; and
- entering the exact production setting will not require changes to code, database structures, deployed components, deployment steps, operating procedures, or the verification method.

Testing performed before the final choice may prove that the application reads and enforces the number or rule supplied through that configuration field. It must not claim that an undecided setting is correct for production. The setting and its requirement remain incomplete until the exact production setting is configured and verified.

A local development default may keep the application runnable, but it is not a temporary production-readiness setting, implementation evidence, or partial completion. It must not be used in production unless it is later selected and verified as the final setting under this section.

Keeping the application deployable through ordinary configuration does not authorize extra code or components whose only purpose is to support infrastructure services that have not been selected. Section 3.4 still requires an exact current requirement for every added component or interface.

#### Information required before a final setting is chosen

A setting may remain undecided only when at least one of the following could change the correct production setting:

- the documented maximums of the services selected to host the production frontend, API, background jobs, scheduled work, and PostgreSQL database, or a documented maximum imposed by Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, or another named production service;
- the greatest number of application copies, database connections, or simultaneous operations that production permits, including API copies, background-job processes, scheduled work, database changes, monitoring, administrative work, and old and new application releases running together during deployment;
- measured request volume, background-job volume, transaction volume, storage use, or simultaneous user activity;
- a recorded decision about the business effect of failure or delay;
- a recorded privacy decision; or
- a recorded legal decision.

If none of these inputs can change the setting, the child plan for that requirement must state the setting directly instead of postponing the decision. Every setting that depends on one of these inputs must be finalized before production signoff.

After the requirement is allowed to begin, its child plan must complete all of the following before recording the setting as complete:

1. state the exact setting, its unit of measurement or named event, the operation it controls, and the failure it prevents;
2. identify whether Pickup Lane, a hosting service, another external service, or a combination of them enforces it;
3. identify every place where it is enforced, which may include the service that first receives an internet request, the Pickup Lane API, code that opens PostgreSQL connections, code that runs background jobs, the browser application, or Firebase, Stripe, or Cloudflare R2 configuration;
4. identify the exact information used to choose it: a documented service limit, configured maximum number of running processes or connections, measured workload, recorded decision about the business effect of failure or delay, recorded privacy decision, or recorded legal decision;
5. record the final setting, every configuration file, deployment setting, or external-service setting that enforces it, and every development, test, staging, or production system in which it applies;
6. verify the final setting using each of the following test methods that matches the kind of setting being verified:
   - For a limit on request size, upload size, or operation frequency, use the production release build and final configuration in a separate test system. Test the largest case that must be accepted and the first case that must be rejected. Record the response and confirm that rejection does not create or change a database record, background job, upload, payment, booking, or external-service operation that the requirement prohibits.
   - For a limit on database connections or simultaneous work shared by multiple running application copies, add the greatest possible use by API copies, background-job processes, scheduled work, database changes, monitoring, administrative work, and old and new releases running together during deployment. Confirm that the total remains below the maximum permitted by the selected service. Test the planned numbers of application copies, connections, and simultaneous operations in a separate system with the same limits as production.
   - For a setting controlled by a hosting or external service, inspect the named production service and record both the configured setting and the service documentation explaining what it does. Follow the production-safety rules below for any test that reaches or exceeds the setting.
   - For a limit on page, request, or background-job completion time, computer-processing time, memory use, or downloaded data, measure the production release build using the recorded amount of data, request volume, and simultaneous user or job activity used to choose the setting.
   - For the maximum data loss or recovery time allowed after a failure, restore the affected data into a separate system that cannot serve production users. Record what data was restored, what data was missing, when restoration started, and when it finished.
   - For a record-retention or file-retention rule, inspect how the deletion or restriction date is calculated. In a separate test system, confirm that deletion or restriction occurs on that date and that records required to remain are untouched.
   - For an alert threshold, send a controlled test signal or reproduce the triggering condition in a non-production system. Record that the alert reaches the assigned destination. A production test signal must be marked as a test and must not use every available production connection, request allowance, storage allowance, or other limited capacity, or cause a user-visible failure.
7. for a maximum, minimum, timeout, or alert threshold, record what can be observed when the setting is reached or exceeded and the exact application action or human response that must follow. For a retention period, review schedule, exercise schedule, data-loss target, or recovery-time target, record how a missed date or target is detected and the exact action that must follow; and
8. list every change that requires the setting to be chosen or tested again, including a change to the selected production service or purchased capacity, a change to the maximum number of running application copies, increased measured traffic or workload, a failed limit test, a failed recovery exercise, or a new business, privacy, or legal decision.

Verification must not deliberately:

- use every database connection available to the production application;
- use up the production account's allowed requests, storage, or other limited capacity;
- create an unnecessary charge;
- change production data; or
- cause a request from a real user to fail.

If testing an exact setting against production could cause any of those outcomes, the evidence must include both:

1. inspection of the named production service to confirm the setting configured there; and
2. the result of testing the same setting in a separate non-production system configured the same way.

If the external service does not provide a non-production system where the test can be performed safely, the evidence must record that fact and include the service documentation describing what happens when the setting is exceeded. The production setting must still be confirmed by inspection. In a separate application test, the external request must be made to return the same error documented by the service. That test must confirm that Pickup Lane produces the required result without any prohibited database, job, upload, booking, payment, or external-service effect.

Production signoff is blocked when a setting covered by this section is missing its required decision information, exact production setting, enforcement location, or verification result.

### 3.4 Limits on implementation scope

Requirements define the behavior and observable outcomes Pickup Lane must produce. They do not automatically authorize a particular technical design.

A pass may add or change code, configuration, database structures, tests, or operating procedures only to satisfy or verify an exact requirement in this master.

A document does not control production-readiness work merely because it exists in the repository. Another product requirement or engineering standard may control how a master requirement is implemented only when the master or pass plan identifies its exact file and section. Before using it, the pass plan must confirm that the cited text matches the current application and does not conflict with this master. The cited document may constrain the engineering approach, but it cannot add, remove, or change required product behavior. Any such behavior change requires an amendment to this master before work begins.

The plan for the pass must identify the requirement that justifies each new:

- database table or type of stored record;
- background job;
- API route;
- administrator screen or operation;
- automated process;
- external service; or
- independently deployed application component.

A new item must not be added solely because it might support a future feature or make future work easier.

Code, stored data, or an operating procedure may be shared when two or more current operations require the same behavior or when enforcing the rule in one place is necessary to prevent conflicting results. The shared implementation must remain limited to the cases required by this master. It must not introduce additional user workflows, administrator workflows, roles, states, routes, screens, commands, or external interfaces without an exact requirement that needs them.

When existing Pickup Lane code, configuration, or an existing external service can satisfy the requirement, the pass must use or modify that path unless the plan establishes at least one of the following:

- the existing path cannot produce or verify a required result;
- retaining it would preserve a specific correctness, security, or reliability failure;
- it does not work with a dependency or production service required by this master, or with the required deployment sequence;
- it cannot be operated, recovered, or tested through the procedures required by this master;
- replacing it removes duplicated or obsolete code, data, or operating steps without adding behavior that no current requirement needs; or
- a recorded comparison shows that replacement uses fewer application components, stored records, external services, deployment steps, or recurring operating tasks while preserving every required result.

The plan must identify the existing path, the exact reason from this list, the evidence supporting that reason, and what will be removed when the replacement becomes active.

A pass must not build any of the following unless an exact requirement in this master needs it:

- common infrastructure intended to support several unrelated operations;
- a separate application that must be deployed and operated on its own;
- a system that lets people define different automated workflows; or
- a system that stores and applies configurable rules for unrelated operations.

A possible future use does not justify the additional system.

Adding a workflow, role, state, route, screen, command, external interface, independently deployed component, or external service that no current master requirement needs requires an amendment to this master before planning or implementation begins.

### 3.5 Evidence records

#### What an evidence record must identify

Every evidence record must satisfy Section 3.2.

The record must also contain the following origin details so that another person can identify where the result came from and whether it still applies:

1. the development, test, staging, or production system where the check occurred;
2. the exact release, build, or source-code revision when application code affects the result; and
3. the person who performed the check when the result was collected manually.

Evidence that does not depend on application code, such as an external-service account setting, does not need a source revision. It must still meet the external-setting requirements below.

When evidence has an expiration date or must be reviewed after a named event, its record must state that date or event. An event-based review must also state the latest calendar date by which the review must occur.

#### Evidence for settings outside the repository

A setting stored in the Pickup Lane repository proves only what Pickup Lane intends to configure. It does not prove that the setting is active in Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL hosting, application hosting, DNS, CI, monitoring, access control, alert delivery, or backup storage.

Evidence for a setting that can be changed in an external account or dashboard without changing the repository must identify:

1. the external service;
2. whether the inspected account or system is used for development, test, staging, or production;
3. the human-readable name used for that account or system in the corresponding architecture or environment record;
4. the exact setting or behavior inspected;
5. how the setting or behavior was inspected or exercised;
6. the date and time of inspection; and
7. the observed result.

Evidence from a development, test, or staging account or system establishes behavior only for that named account or system. It must not be used as proof of the production account or system's setting. Evidence used for production signoff must identify the account or system named in the production architecture record, and that record must designate it as production. Files committed to the repository must not include private account, project, tenant, customer, payment, webhook, or dashboard identifiers.

#### Information that evidence must exclude

Evidence may contain the expected result, actual result, checks for outcomes that must not occur, and the origin details required by this section. Unrelated dashboard sections, account details, user records, service settings, and other information that establishes none of those facts must be excluded or redacted.

Use fictional test users and test records whenever they can prove the requirement. When a production setting or production behavior must be inspected, capture only the fields needed to prove the result and cover or remove every unrelated field before storing the evidence.

Evidence must not contain:

- passwords, tokens, private keys, or other secrets;
- recovery codes;
- complete database connection strings;
- an unredacted real user's name, email address, phone number, postal address, authentication identifier, message text, uploaded file, or other personal content;
- a database dump or a complete copy of a database table;
- a private message or its attachments;
- an export containing records or fields outside the exact scenario being verified;
- a complete payment-card or bank-account number, security code, expiration date, or unredacted customer, payment, refund, or bank-account identifier;
- an unredacted external account, project, tenant, webhook, or dashboard identifier; or
- a signed URL that can still be used.

Redaction must preserve the external-service name, the account or system's environment and human-readable name from the architecture record, inspected setting or behavior, inspection method, inspection time, observed result, and failure information required by Sections 3.2 and 3.5. An alias recorded in the architecture record and containing none of the prohibited external identifier must replace an external identifier that this section prohibits storing.

#### When evidence stops counting

Evidence stops counting toward completion when:

- the external-service setting or production account or system it proves changes;
- the deployment configuration it proves changes;
- the application behavior it proves changes;
- an incident shows that its recorded result is no longer true;
- its recorded expiration or review point occurs; or
- the evidence is discovered to cover a different system, release, build, or production account.

Only evidence affected by the change stops counting. The requirement remains incomplete until the affected check is repeated against the changed system and a new observed result is recorded.

## 4. Parent Pass Roadmap

Follow these passes from top to bottom. Implement and test a capability as soon as its prerequisites exist; save tests requiring the actual production-equivalent build, final provider bindings, complete product flows or safe restoration for Pass 20. Pass 19 establishes the production control-plane resources, provider settings, account bindings, and configuration decisions needed before deployment; Pass 20 inspects those settings and qualifies the release without deploying application code to production. Pass 21 deploys the application, checks the deployed public host, and performs production-safe final inspection. A missing decision that changes implementation remains a blocker under Section 3.3.

| Order | Parent pass |
|---:|---|
| 01 | Architecture, Recovery Objectives, and Ownership |
| 02 | Legal, Financial, and Privacy Decisions |

<a id="section-5"></a>

## 5. Parent Pass Requirements

<a id="p01"></a>

### Pass 01 — Architecture, Recovery Objectives, and Ownership

#### Purpose

Set the approved recovery and incident-declaration objectives; name accountable roles; record the selected providers, three environment boundaries, real development resources, public request path, private dependencies, and secret-injection architecture before infrastructure-dependent work.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p01-r01"></a>**P01.R01 — Recovery-tier classification**<br>Recovery tiers must classify at least financial state, bookings and capacity, identity and roles, audit records, operational application data, and replaceable venue images. The Pass 01 incident-recovery authority must approve the recovery-tier record marking each category and system as essential or replaceable and identifying its exact records, recovery method, maximum approved data-loss interval, and maximum approved elapsed time from declaring recovery to passing the checks required for traffic resumption. A category may be marked replaceable only when the record explains how safe traffic can resume without its lost data and states its separate approved data-loss treatment.

    **Required recovery objectives.** The required launch objectives are **one hour maximum data loss** for every essential category and **eight hours from incident declaration until verified safe traffic resumption**; the ordinary recovery-point freshness aspiration (a desired age of the newest restorable data, separate from elapsed recovery time) is approximately 15 minutes when supported.

    **Data-loss measurement.** For the one-hour objective, measure the interval from the simulated failure time to the source-state time of the latest consistent, verified restorable point for each essential category; the interval must not exceed 60 minutes. Record the committed changes missing after restoration rather than treating a backup's creation or replication time as its recoverable source-state time.

   **Detection and declaration decision.** The recovery-objective decision record must name the observable failure or integrity conditions that require a restoration decision, the evidence accepted for each condition, the authority responsible for deciding, and the event that starts its numeric detection-to-declaration response boundary under Section 3.3. Pass 18 incident procedures must use these recorded conditions and authority. That authority must declare recovery or record an evidence-based decision that restoration is not required within that boundary after the triggering condition is detected.

    **Recovery-time clock and evidence.** The eight-hour recovery clock starts when the named incident-recovery or incident-command authority formally declares recovery. Record detection and declaration times separately, the triggering condition and evidence, actor, and decision. A late declaration must be recorded as a missed detection-to-declaration boundary; the declaration must not be backdated or postponed to extend the recovery target. An unknown authority, trigger, or decision rule blocks signoff.

2. <a id="p01-r02"></a>**P01.R02 — Accountable production and recovery ownership**<br>The ownership record must assign one named accountable person to each of these responsibilities, even when one person holds several: production-readiness program, platform/deployment, API, identity/application security, PostgreSQL, Stripe/finance operations, durable jobs, Cloudflare R2, frontend, release assurance, monitoring/reliability, secrets/external-service access, incident recovery, and privacy/retention. Registrar/DNS, repository/CI, Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare, PostgreSQL hosting, application hosting, monitoring, backup, and incident-command recovery must each also have a second named person or a documented vendor/escalation recovery route proposed for use when the primary owner is unavailable. For each time-critical incident or recovery action required by Pass 15 or Pass 18, the planning record must distinguish access recovery from the ability to execute the application-specific procedure: it must name a proposed qualified backup actor or escalation route, required authority, operational instructions, and availability consistent with the numeric response or recovery deadline. Pass 18 requirement 12 completes the action-specific response assignment as procedures are defined; Pass 20 verification 43 must demonstrate that each route remains usable without the primary owner or their credentials and that the qualified alternate can execute the application-specific action within its deadline. A vendor capable only of resetting a login is not sufficient evidence that it can reconcile financial state, restore PostgreSQL, revoke a compromise, or resume affected jobs. If an action has no demonstrably capable backup route, the limitation and missed objective must remain unresolved for production signoff rather than being marked covered by this planning assignment.

   For initial-launch administrator MFA recovery, name the identity/security authority and the proposed authorized assisted-recovery operator and alternate administrator-access route needed for Pass 10 requirement 6. Record how the proposed route remains available when the primary administrator or their credentials are unavailable, the qualified operator who would execute it, and the numeric response or recovery deadline it must meet. Pass 10 requirement 6 owns demonstration of the actual Firebase capability, stronger-than-email proof, qualified operator, alternate route, and deadline; those results remain incomplete there until verified, without leaving this ownership decision incomplete solely because the later MFA implementation is pending.

3. <a id="p01-r03"></a>**P01.R03 — Three primary real integration environments**<br>The architecture record must identify **three primary real integration environments: local Development, deployed Staging, and deployed Production**; ordinary automated tests and CI are additional isolated execution contexts, not substitutes for those three. Pickup Lane has no preview environment.

   Local development runs the frontend, FastAPI, worker when needed, and PostgreSQL locally, with **real dedicated development** Firebase/Identity Platform and App Check, Stripe general sandbox, R2 private/public buckets, Postmark sending server, Grafana stack, PostHog project and AWS S3 test backups whenever that integration is exercised.

   Complete App Check attestation tests need a suitable HTTPS development origin; any localhost debug token is not proof of genuine attestation.

   Staging uses separate real Render hosting/PostgreSQL, Firebase project, Stripe **staging general sandbox**, R2 buckets and tokens, Postmark server and webhook, Grafana stack/alerts, PostHog project, and dedicated AWS S3 Ohio/Oregon backup/restore resources.

   Production uses only the separate final live credentials and resources, including Stripe live mode, production Cloudflare, Postmark, Grafana, PostHog and protected S3 accounts/buckets.

   Ordinary automated unit/component tests may use dedicated PostgreSQL fixtures and simulated service responses as **additional fast tests**, but cannot prove actual external service behavior.

   Ordinary CI uses ephemeral test data and may have a **CI-only sandbox** for real integration checks; it must not have staging or production credentials in untrusted jobs.

   Production-equivalent integration and release qualification (isolated non-production checks against the relevant production build, settings, and limits) are performed in staging against real staging resources.

   Credentials, webhook destinations and data are segregated across the three environments; account-level billing administrators must still be named and restricted under Pass 10 requirement 8; Pass 19 requirement 3.

   Neither a shared vendor billing organization nor a common human administrator is proof of isolation between workload credentials.

4. <a id="p01-r04"></a>**P01.R04 — Production architecture and system inventory**<br>Before any hosting-dependent implementation begins, the production architecture and environment record must identify the frontend, API, workers, scheduler, PostgreSQL, Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, DNS or CDN, CI, notifications, monitoring, backups, every public entry point and private dependency, region, and single point of failure. The architecture record must additionally identify **Postmark, Grafana Cloud and Grafana Incident Response Management (IRM), PostHog, AWS S3 Ohio/Oregon and all real development resources**, and each approved backend outbound destination and redirect behavior, consuming component, configured endpoint, and permitted operation. Backend outbound requests must go only to the recorded service destinations for their required operations, must not use client-supplied targets, and must not follow redirects to unapproved destinations. For every named system, the record must identify the local-development resource, automated-test treatment, CI treatment, staging resource, and production resource required by Pass 01 requirement 3. It must show that no credential or resource assigned to one context can read or change another context and must state that Pickup Lane has no preview environment. Only the resource explicitly designated as production in this record may supply production evidence; renaming or temporarily using a development or demonstration resource does not make its earlier evidence production evidence.

5. <a id="p01-r05"></a>**P01.R05 — Public HTTP request path and trusted request information**<br>Before new or revised implementation planning begins for Pass 06 requirements 8–11, 15, Pass 01 requirement 4 must identify every component through which a public HTTP request travels, the component that receives the request first, the component that establishes the encrypted connection, whether the API can be reached without passing through that first component, and every component allowed to add or replace request information. The affected requirement must remain unstarted while any of those facts could change its code, configuration, deployment procedure, or verification method. Existing code may be inspected during reconciliation, but it must remain incomplete and must not be accepted as the implementation of an affected requirement until it has been compared with the recorded path, corrected where it conflicts, and verified through that path.

6. <a id="p01-r06"></a>**P01.R06 — Production secret injection mechanism**<br>The production architecture record must name the secret store or encrypted environment-injection mechanism used by each deployed component. Secrets must enter production through that mechanism and must not be committed to source control, embedded in images, exposed to the browser, stored in logs, or copied into planning evidence.

#### Verification

No original numbered scenario is assigned exclusively to this decision/setup pass. Its recorded decisions and configurations are prerequisites for the relevant implementation and staging verification scenarios.

#### Completion condition

All decisions, numeric recovery targets, accountable authority, proposed administrator MFA recovery responsibility and route, public and private paths, provider and environment assignments, and design-changing configuration inputs required by Section 3.3 are recorded. The actual MFA replacement capability, qualified alternate execution, and deadline are verified under Pass 10 requirement 6 and do not prevent this decision pass from completing. No paid staging/production deployment is implied.

---

<a id="p02"></a>

### Pass 02 — Legal, Financial, and Privacy Decisions

#### Purpose

Resolve the federal and state legal applicability, data inventory, retention and classification, public terms and participation obligations, and finance-approved credit policy that downstream features must not guess.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p02-r01"></a>**P02.R01 — Nevada online-service operator determination**<br>Before nationwide launch, the assigned Pass 01 privacy responsibility must record whether Pickup Lane is an online-service operator covered by NRS 603A.300 through 603A.360: it must operate for commercial purposes; collect and maintain information covered by NRS 603A.320 from Nevada-resident consumers seeking or acquiring, by purchase or lease, a service for personal, family, or household purposes; have the Nevada connection required by NRS 603A.330(1)(c); and not fall within an applicable operator or information exclusion in NRS 603A.330(2) or 603A.338.

    Nationwide registration alone and the absence of a current sale do not settle this determination.

    A not-covered decision must identify the missing element or exclusion and be reviewed before a change in Nevada consumer use, transactions, or information handling satisfies that element or removes the exclusion.

    The NRS 603A.340(2) notice exception applies only when the operator is located in Nevada, derives revenue primarily from a source other than online sale or lease of goods, services, or credit, and has fewer than 20,000 unique website or service visitors per year; it does not remove the NRS 603A.345 request duty. Any required Nevada notice follows Pass 16 requirement 7.

    A missing applicability decision blocks signoff when the statute applies. Pass 16 requirement 9 owns the resulting request and no-sale treatment.

2. <a id="p02-r02"></a>**P02.R02 — Data classification and controls**<br>The data inventory must classify public, internal, personal, private-message, financial, moderation, audit, authentication, and secret data. That classification must control access, display, logging, caching, retention, backup, export, and deletion behavior.

3. <a id="p02-r03"></a>**P02.R03 — Personal-data inventory fields**<br>Each personal-data category must have a recorded purpose, collection source, authoritative store, every copied or processor store, permitted reader roles, retention start and end, deletion or anonymization action in each store, backup treatment, and the Pass 01 role accountable for its retention treatment. A store is incomplete until every category it contains has all of these fields.

4. <a id="p02-r04"></a>**P02.R04 — No indefinite personal-data retention**<br>No personal-data category may default to indefinite retention. Each category must have a numeric duration or an event-based end condition recorded by the person assigned the Pass 01 privacy or retention responsibility. The inventory entry for a payment, refund, dispute, or tax category must also cite the finance decision that sets its retention, and a category retained for a legal obligation must cite the legal decision that states its authority and end condition. A Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, analytics, error-monitoring, or backup limitation must be recorded for the category it affects rather than treated as approval of the retention period.

5. <a id="p02-r05"></a>**P02.R05 — Terminal treatment for personal-data stores**<br>Each database table, object, cache, telemetry store, and processor record containing personal data must assign one terminal treatment: hard deletion, irreversible anonymization, restricted retention for a stated obligation, or soft deletion followed by a separately recorded terminal treatment and deadline. Soft deletion alone is not a terminal result.

6. <a id="p02-r06"></a>**P02.R06 — Legally approved versioned Terms of Service**<br>Qualified legal review must approve the final, versioned Terms of Service text before the account, hosting, participation, and payment flows that depend on it are finalized. The review must decide whether the participation provisions may be in the same document or need a separate document or acknowledgment. Pass 17 requirement 22 owns public presentation and comparison with implemented behavior; Pass 21 requirement 4 owns production publication.

    For nationwide adult registration and community games and Illinois official games, qualified legal review must approve the actual text and record the jurisdictions and game types considered, the reviewer and decision date, and which provisions answer an applicable legal or contractual duty versus which are proposed liability protections.

    The terms must accurately identify Pickup Lane's role for official games and the independent community organizer's role; state account eligibility, user conduct, game participation, cancellation, refund, and dispute rules; describe soccer participation and injury risks; and state counsel-approved governing-law, dispute, and injury-related liability-allocation provisions.

    The review must decide whether an assumption-of-risk acknowledgment, participant waiver, liability limit, or indemnification provision is justified for each affected activity and jurisdiction, and approve its scope and acceptance method before it is included or relied upon.

    No provision may claim that a waiver, liability limit, governing-law selection, or indemnity defeats rights that applicable law preserves.

    An unresolved material legal question must block production approval. Revisions must receive the same review before publication.

7. <a id="p02-r07"></a>**P02.R07 — Eligible source and customer-paid base**<br>For a validated eligible source booking or payment, the customer-paid base is the gross settled amount actually paid by that customer in the source currency before refunds or chargebacks; exclude any amount funded by previously issued application credit.

    **Maximum additional administrative credit (source currency):**

    `max(0, gross settled customer-paid base − cumulative distinct refunds attributable to the source − finalized chargeback losses attributable to the source − every credit already issued against the source)`

    **Deduction and reversal safeguards.** Count each returned or lost amount only once across overlapping provider records and repeated events; a refund or loss already included in one deduction must not be deducted again. Spent credit still consumes the issuance cap. A reversal restores capacity only when the related liability has actually been extinguished and that fact is recorded in the immutable ledger links.

    **Finance approval.** The named Stripe/finance responsibility must approve source eligibility, this calculation, later refund and chargeback treatment, and reversal accounting before implementation or signoff; an undecided financial rule cannot be guessed.

#### Verification

No original numbered scenario is assigned exclusively to this decision/setup pass. Its recorded decisions and configurations are prerequisites for the relevant implementation and staging verification scenarios.

#### Completion condition

The Nevada applicability decision, qualified legal approval of the versioned terms, finance-approved credit policy, and the data and retention inventory for planned production categories are recorded. A missing required decision or approval blocks completion.

---

<a id="p03"></a>

### Pass 03 — Repository, CI, and Security Foundations

#### Purpose

Establish secure repository governance, CI/test ownership and isolation, supply-chain controls, permission and secret inventories, and blocking development checks before implementing application changes.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p03-r01"></a>**P03.R01 — Secret and sensitive configuration inventory**<br>The secret inventory must identify each secret or sensitive configuration name, named external system, purpose, consumers, classification, storage/injection path, environment boundary, scope, rotation trigger, revocation effect, compromise response, offboarding dependency, and recovery procedure without recording its value. For every Pickup Lane-generated secret, one-use recovery capability, or other value whose security relies on unpredictability, the issuing operation must use a cryptographically secure random generator and at least 128 bits of unpredictability; ordinary non-secret record IDs are not covered by this rule. The inventory must name the algorithm and key or certificate owner for each Pickup Lane-managed cryptographic operation and the provider responsible for each provider-managed operation, without collecting or exposing provider key material.

2. <a id="p03-r02"></a>**P03.R02 — Browser-public configuration boundaries**<br>Browser-public configuration, including Firebase web identifiers and Stripe publishable keys, must be classified separately from server-private credentials. A public key or identifier still requires the correct environment binding and any Firebase, Stripe, or domain restriction supported for that exact key type.

3. <a id="p03-r03"></a>**P03.R03 — Least-privilege service and human roles**<br>The API, worker, migration, backup, monitoring, and human-administration roles must each have an inventory of the exact operations and resources they require. Two roles may share a service credential only when those inventories are identical; roles with any different required permission must use different credentials. A role must not receive an extra permission merely to make its credential match another role.

4. <a id="p03-r04"></a>**P03.R04 — Shared branch and review gate**<br>Default `main` and active `develop` branches must require pull requests, a recorded pre-merge review of affected behavior and security risk, and the mandatory checks assigned in Pass 03 requirement 14.

    **High-risk classification.** Before review, the Pass 01 identity/application-security responsibility must classify a change as high-risk when it affects identity, authorization, public ingress, sensitive data, financial or capacity state, external-service trust, production credentials, recovery, or release authority, or when the recorded trust-boundary review in Pass 03 requirement 9 identifies a comparable prohibited effect. Record the affected boundary, misuse, and classification.

    **Qualified independent reviewer available.** When a qualified independent reviewer is available for a high-risk change, request that review and record the result.

    **Solo-maintainer path.** With one maintainer and no independent reviewer, use a **documented self-review**, tests selected to exercise each identified misuse and prohibited effect in addition to the mandatory checks, and explicit production authorization; record the selected tests, their results, and any uncovered effect. Do not invent a second approver or enable an unfulfillable GitHub approval count.

    **Requirements common to both paths.** GitHub must block merge on failed or missing mandatory checks, and no account or role may bypass those enforced checks. A self-review record is an accountable process control, **not technically equivalent to an independent GitHub approval**. Workflow changes must be assigned to the named Pass 01 repository or CI responsibility, and a workflow triggered from an untrusted fork must receive neither protected secrets nor deployment authority.

   **Reviewer availability and gate evidence.** Before the gate, record whether a person independent of the author has expertise in the changed behavior or security boundary and can review before the release gate, the review request and result when one is available, or the reason none is available. The solo-maintainer path must record self-review, heightened checks, and explicit production authorization. No second GitHub approval is required solely by this requirement.

   **Branch-protection evidence.** Inspect the actual `main` and active `develop` branch rules, roles that can change those rules or merge, required checks, administrator treatment, and available real pull-request gate results read-only. Exercise failed checks, bypass attempts, and untrusted workflows in an isolated repository configured with the same recorded rules and permission classes; that exercise does not prove the active branches' settings by itself. Compare the actual and isolated rules, permissions, mandatory checks, and enforcement evidence before this gate is complete. A mismatch or an unverified actual setting remains unresolved until corrected and checked again.

5. <a id="p03-r05"></a>**P03.R05 — Least-privilege CI workflows**<br>CI workflows must use least-privilege permissions, event triggers that do not expose secrets to untrusted changes, no direct interpolation of untrusted values into shell code, third-party actions pinned to reviewed immutable revisions, and protected deployment environments. Deployment authentication must use short-lived OpenID Connect (OIDC) credentials when the final hosting or cloud account exposes that method for the required deployment operation; any long-lived deployment key follows Pass 19.

6. <a id="p03-r06"></a>**P03.R06 — Pinned dependency installation**<br>Dependency installation must use committed lock data that fixes the resolved direct and transitive versions used by the release build. The release installation must obtain each Python and Node package from a recorded approved package source or an individually approved direct source over an authenticated connection, and must use the existing package manager to verify the package-integrity value recorded for each downloadable artifact. A missing or mismatched integrity value, unapproved source, or source substitution must fail the release installation; a version pin alone does not prove package integrity. A shipped dependency with a known vulnerability that is exploitable in the deployed configuration must block release until the vulnerable dependency or deployed configuration is corrected.

   Before a package source is placed in the allowlist or a lockfile/release install uses it, the named Pass 01 release-assurance person must approve its URL, scope, reason, approval time, and integrity mechanism. Each direct-source exception needs its own approval and recorded artifact identity. A missing approval blocks installation; this uses the existing package manager and release record.

7. <a id="p03-r07"></a>**P03.R07 — Pinned CI runtime and tool versions**<br>Continuous integration must pin the Node, npm, Python, and tool versions used for release checks. A lockfile or manifest mismatch must fail rather than silently resolve a different dependency set.

8. <a id="p03-r08"></a>**P03.R08 — Static security analyzer gate**<br>Source code must run the repository's named static security analyzer; source and browser bundles must be scanned for committed or embedded secrets; and direct and transitive dependencies, runtime images, and operating-system packages shipped with the release must be scanned for known vulnerabilities. Each scan must record the tool and version, rule or advisory-data version, source revision or artifact, and findings. A required rule must not be disabled, and a shipped component must not be excluded from scanning. Discovering an exposed secret requires revocation under Pass 18; deleting it from the latest revision is not remediation.

    Before launch, the Pass 01 identity/application-security responsibility must name the vendor and public advisory sources used for the components in the Pass 20 requirement 7 deployed inventory, how new reports reach that responsibility, the review interval, maximum time from report to applicability decision, and correction deadlines by risk class under Section 3.3. The classes must account for active exploitation, internet exposure, possible user or financial harm, and available fixes or mitigations.

    After launch, newly disclosed findings must be compared with the currently deployed component versions at that interval and when a named source reports a potentially affected component or active exploitation.

    Each review must record the source, affected deployed component and exposure, exploitability in Pickup Lane's configuration, accountable response role, risk class, and resulting deadline.

    An applicable exploitable finding must remain visible until a corrected release or verified containment removes the exposure; a containment must identify its remaining risk and deadline for a permanent fix.

    An actively exploited exposure must reach the incident-recovery responsibility within the recorded escalation time, and a missed correction deadline must alert the assigned role under Pass 18 requirements 1, 6.

    A finding may be closed as inapplicable only with evidence about the deployed version or configuration, and correction or containment must be retested against the affected behavior and deployed artifact. Waiting for the next ordinary release without meeting the recorded deadline must not close the finding.

    The same accountable role must maintain the deployed inventory of Python, Node, PostgreSQL, supported runtimes, authentication/payment/storage SDKs, and external provider APIs with their documented support end dates and announced breaking changes.

    Review vendor support and compatibility notices at a numeric recurring interval under Section 3.3 and on a provider deprecation or announced breaking-change notice; record the affected deployed version, change deadline, owner, migration or replacement action, and compatibility tests.

    An unsupported critical component or unaddressed provider change that violates a required security or functional outcome must block the affected deployment or continued acceptance until a supported compatible state or verified risk-limiting mitigation is established. This review uses existing release checks and does not mandate another product.

9. <a id="p03-r09"></a>**P03.R09 — Requirement-to-scenario mapping**<br>Each numbered production requirement must map to one or more numbered verification scenarios, a manual verification procedure, or a named production-setting check. Pass 03 must establish the complete planned mapping for Passes 01–21 in `docs/production-readiness/verification-map.md`; later passes must keep it consistent with their pass-specific records. The mapping must identify the test layer, environment, expected observable result, prohibited effects checked, and evidence record. The linked pass records distinguish planned coverage from checks performed successfully, checks not yet performed, and evidence invalidated by later changes; a planned method is not completed evidence. A requirement is uncovered when no mapped check proves all of its required outcomes and every prohibited effect that could occur in that scenario. Before selecting an implementation for a requirement involving identity, authorization, public ingress, sensitive data, financial or capacity changes, external-service trust, or release authority, its pass plan must identify the relevant trust boundary (where less-trusted input or a caller reaches a protected operation), a plausible misuse of that boundary, the prohibited effect, and the existing requirement and check that prevent it. A newly identified misuse requires an amendment to this master before that design begins when it could cause unauthorized access, sensitive-data disclosure, financial or capacity error, lost durable or audit state, unsafe recovery, or another failure of a required production outcome, and no existing master requirement governs that effect. Record the effect, the search for a governing requirement, and the amendment decision; this review does not create a separate threat-modeling system.

10. <a id="p03-r10"></a>**P03.R10 — Unit tests must own isolated decision logic**<br>Unit tests must own isolated decision logic; PostgreSQL tests must own constraints, transactions, locking, and query behavior; API tests must own authentication, authorization, validation, and response contracts; integration tests must own Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, worker, and multi-component boundaries; manual checks must own human-perception behavior that automation cannot establish.

11. <a id="p03-r11"></a>**P03.R11 — Duplicate-effect and isolation test coverage**<br>A scenario whose failure can cause a second charge, second booking, unauthorized read, duplicate job effect, negative capacity, partial transaction, leaked secret, or lost audit record must test both the required result and that prohibited side effect.

12. <a id="p03-r12"></a>**P03.R12 — PostgreSQL-backed database tests**<br>Tests that exercise database behavior must run against the supported PostgreSQL behavior rather than substituting a database with different constraint, locking, or transaction semantics.

13. <a id="p03-r13"></a>**P03.R13 — External-service test doubles**<br>A Firebase emulator, Stripe mock, R2 fake, or other test double may verify application decisions, but it cannot prove Firebase project settings, App Check behavior, Stripe signatures and event delivery, R2 permissions and object behavior, PostgreSQL-host limits, hosting limits, or production credentials. Each such claim requires the named real development/staging Firebase project, independent Stripe general sandbox, R2 bucket, final PostgreSQL service, final hosting environment, or production-safe check capable of demonstrating it; unit test doubles are supplementary, not substitutes for real development or staging integrations.

14. <a id="p03-r14"></a>**P03.R14 — Release-check ownership and records**<br>The release-check record must assign formatting, static analysis, type checks, backend tests, frontend non-Playwright tests, migration checks, API-description checks, dependency checks, secret checks, and production-build checks to the merge gate, release gate, or both. Continuous integration must block the assigned action when any mandatory check fails, is cancelled, does not run, or produces no result.

15. <a id="p03-r15"></a>**P03.R15 — Mandatory check enforcement**<br>Every required check must run and must block its assigned action when it fails. Its file and change triggers must include every source, configuration, migration, lock, workflow, test-support, and build file that can affect the checked behavior. If no trigger set can include every affecting file, the check must run for every change at its assigned gate.

16. <a id="p03-r16"></a>**P03.R16 — Flaky test ownership and resolution**<br>A flaky test must have one accountable person, recorded first-run failure evidence, a correction deadline, and the requirements and release gates it affects. A retry may collect diagnostic information, but a later passing attempt must not replace the failed mandatory result or approve the affected merge or release.

17. <a id="p03-r17"></a>**P03.R17 — Production invariant test fixtures**<br>Test fixtures and support helpers must preserve the production invariant being tested. A helper must not bypass authorization, PostgreSQL constraints or transaction behavior, Firebase ID-token and App Check verification, Stripe signature/state verification, or Cloudflare R2 permission checks in a scenario that claims to prove those controls.

18. <a id="p03-r18"></a>**P03.R18 — Evidence type and system identity**<br>The evidence record must distinguish unit, frontend component, backend service, API, PostgreSQL, mocked-browser, full-stack-browser, Firebase emulator/test-project, Stripe dedicated general-sandbox, Cloudflare R2 test-bucket, migration, concurrency, security, recovery, load, and production-safe post-deploy results. A result must not claim a stronger boundary than the environment it exercised.

19. <a id="p03-r19"></a>**P03.R19 — Synthetic test data**<br>Tests must use synthetic (invented and unrelated to production people, accounts, or payments), non-production data and isolated database, browser, Firebase, Stripe, R2, time, environment, worker, and scheduler state. They must remain order-independent and clean up after failure without sharing production credentials or live-charge authority.

20. <a id="p03-r20"></a>**P03.R20 — Stable browser-test element selection**<br>A browser test included in a release gate must locate the control by user-visible role, label, or stable test contract; wait for an observable application result rather than a fixed delay; isolate user and browser state; and sanitize failure artifacts. A CSS class or document position alone must not be the proof that the intended control was exercised.

21. <a id="p03-r21"></a>**P03.R21 — Sanitized CI failure artifacts**<br>Test and CI failure artifacts must be sanitized, access-controlled, linked to the failing revision, and retained for the period recorded under Section 3.3. They must not contain tokens, private messages, payment details, unrestricted personal data, Firebase/Stripe/R2/PostgreSQL/hosting credentials, or reusable signed URLs.

22. <a id="p03-r22"></a>**P03.R22 — Required suite triggers and gates**<br>Each required suite must state the event that starts it and the gate it controls. A Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, full-stack browser, load, restore, or production-safe post-deploy check that is not run on every pull request must name its pre-release or deployment stage and must remain an incomplete release blocker until it passes for the exact release candidate identified by Pass 20 requirement 1. All required pre-deployment suites must pass before authorization to deploy. A production-safe check that requires the deployed production release remains explicitly pending at authorization to deploy; it blocks final production acceptance if incomplete or failed, but does not block the deployment needed to run it.

#### Integration boundaries

- This pass does not require duplicate tests at every layer.
- Playwright or another browser end-to-end tool is used only for scenarios that require an actual browser boundary; it is not the default proof for backend rules.
- Pass 03 requirements 9–13, 17–22; Pass 20 requirement 2 consume the exact scenarios and environments defined by Passes 01 through 21. Each numbered requirement must state its observable required and prohibited outcomes; Pass 03 selects the smallest test boundary that can prove them.
- Pass 01 requirements 3, 6; Pass 03 requirements 1–3; Pass 10 requirement 8; Pass 19 requirements 3 and 4 supply CI, test-service, and deployment credentials. Pass 20 requirements 6–11, 14 and 15; Pass 21 requirement 1 consume the release gates, artifact identities, and evidence created here.
- A check requiring a final external account, production service, deployment artifact, projected volume, or production-safe stage remains incomplete under Section 3.3 until that input exists. A mock or earlier artifact must not be used to close it.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p03-v01"></a>**P03.V01 — Requirement-to-scenario mapping inspection**<br>For Pass 03 requirements 9–11, produce the complete requirement-to-scenario mapping in `docs/production-readiness/verification-map.md` and inspect every numbered requirement and verification scenario in Passes 01 through 21. For each mapping, inspect its layer, environment, expected result, prohibited effects, and evidence location; distinguish planned checks from observed results. For each security-sensitive design named in Pass 03 requirement 9, inspect the trust-boundary and misuse review recorded before implementation selection and its links to the governing requirement and check; present one material misuse with no coverage and confirm that design cannot proceed until the master is amended. Confirm that every requirement is fully covered once or at the smallest set of layers needed and that every duplicate charge or booking, unauthorized read, duplicate job effect, negative capacity, partial transaction, secret leak, and lost audit risk has an explicit absence check.

2. <a id="p03-v02"></a>**P03.V02 — Mapped test-layer checks**<br>For Pass 03 requirements 10, 12 and 13, 17 and 18, run one mapped check from each unit, PostgreSQL, API, integration, manual, emulator or fake, test-service, and production-setting layer or environment used by the release. Replace PostgreSQL with a database having different transaction behavior and attempt to use a Firebase emulator, Stripe mock, or R2 fake as production-setting evidence. Record the claimed boundary and observed result. Confirm correct layer assignment, rejection of the substituted database claim, and evidence labels that never claim a stronger boundary than the environment exercised.

3. <a id="p03-v03"></a>**P03.V03 — Mandatory check failure cases**<br>For Pass 03 requirements 14–16, 22, force every mandatory check to fail, cancel, produce no result, and skip a file that affects its behavior. Create a controlled first-run flaky failure followed by a passing retry and inspect every suite trigger and deferred pre-release item. Confirm that the assigned merge or release decision remains blocked, the retry does not replace the failure, every trigger covers its affecting files, and a deferred suite closes only for the exact release candidate. Confirm that an incomplete or failed pre-deployment check blocks authorization to deploy, while a production-safe check requiring the deployed release remains pending at that decision and blocks final production acceptance until it passes.

4. <a id="p03-v04"></a>**P03.V04 — Vulnerability and secret scan checks**<br>For Pass 03 requirements 6, 8, change a locked direct and transitive dependency, introduce a controlled known vulnerable component, inject a detectable fake secret, disable a required analyzer rule, and omit a shipped runtime or operating-system component from scanning in an isolated branch. Separately redirect one Python and one Node package to an unapproved source and supply a missing or mismatched package-integrity value in a controlled release installation. Record the approved source, actual source, expected and observed integrity result, tool, tool version, rule or advisory-data version, revision or artifact, findings, gate result, and secret-revocation result. Confirm deterministic installation, rejection of source substitution and missing or mismatched integrity, and failure of each required gate until the dependency, deployed configuration, analyzer rule, scan coverage, or exposed secret is corrected.

   Remove a source approval and add an unapproved direct source in separate install attempts. Confirm each blocks before install, then inspect the named approval, scope, integrity result, and exact lockfile artifact for a permitted attempt.

5. <a id="p03-v05"></a>**P03.V05 — Shuffled and parallel test isolation**<br>For Pass 03 requirements 19–21, run tests in shuffled order and in parallel with isolated users and resources, fail cleanup separately for PostgreSQL data, browser state, R2 objects, Stripe test objects, Firebase test identities, worker jobs, and scheduler occurrences, and produce a browser-test failure artifact containing controlled sensitive markers. Confirm order independence, isolation from production, cleanup visibility, observable waits and stable locators, sanitization, access restriction, revision linkage, and retention at the period chosen under Section 3.3.

6. <a id="p03-v06"></a>**P03.V06 — Active branch protection inspection**<br>For Pass 03 requirements 4 and 5, inspect the actual `main` and active `develop` rules, required checks, administrator treatment, available real pull-request gate results, and the permissions of every account or role that can change repository settings or merge, without changing those protections. In an isolated repository with the recorded equivalent rules and permission classes, attempt an untrusted-fork workflow, excessive workflow permission, direct interpolation of controlled shell input, an unpinned third-party action, protected-environment access, secret access, deployment authority, and protected-branch bypass through every relevant permission class. Record the actual settings and available gate results, isolated setup, attempts, and results; compare the actual and isolated rules, permissions, mandatory checks, and enforcement evidence. Confirm least privilege, immutable action revisions, no protected capability for untrusted changes, enforcement of pull requests and mandatory status checks on the actual branches as configured, and complete documented self-review or genuine independent-review evidence for each change. An isolated success cannot establish an actual branch setting; any mismatch or unverified enforcement remains unresolved until corrected and verified. A PR author's self-review must not be represented as another person's approval.

   Exercise a high-risk change with an available relevant reviewer and one without. Inspect the recorded trust boundary, misuse, prohibited effect, high-risk classification, availability decision, request and result or solo self-review evidence, additional scenarios selected for each identified effect, their results, and production authorization; confirm no mandatory second GitHub approval was inferred. Present a newly identified uncovered misuse that meets Pass 03 requirement 9's amendment condition and confirm that design stops until the master is amended.

7. <a id="p03-v07"></a>**P03.V07 — New dependency advisory response**<br>For Pass 03 requirement 8, after a simulated release, introduce a new advisory for an affected deployed component, one for an unaffected version, and one describing active exploitation. Advance a controlled clock through the recorded review and correction deadlines, then apply a corrected artifact or containment and retest the affected behavior. Inspect the deployed inventory, advisory source, applicability decision, exposure and risk classification, assigned role, incident escalation, alert on a missed deadline, remaining risk, and closure evidence. Confirm that an unaffected finding closes only with version or configuration evidence, an exploitable finding remains visible until a verified fix or containment, active exploitation reaches incident recovery, and no expired finding is treated as resolved merely because a future release is planned. Separately simulate an impending Python or Node end-of-support date, PostgreSQL/SDK compatibility withdrawal, and a Firebase or Stripe breaking API notice. Advance beyond the recorded review and provider deadlines; inspect affected-version detection, ownership, migration plan, release regression tests, and the gate or escalation for any unresolved incompatible component. Confirm that advisory-only scanning does not substitute for support-lifecycle review.

#### Completion condition

Development PR gates, branch protections, access/secret classification and test-layer contracts function on the active repository branches. Later production-build evidence remains with Pass 20.

---

<a id="p04"></a>

### Pass 04 — Local Development Environment

#### Purpose

Implement local development and dedicated development-provider configuration. Keep personal production data and cross-environment credentials out of this environment; activate no unnecessary paid staging or production services.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p04-r01"></a>**P04.R01 — No production personal data in development**<br>Production personal data must not be copied to development, test, or a separate diagnostic dataset. Development, testing, and reproduction of a production problem must use synthetic data or data that has been irreversibly anonymized before it leaves the production environment so that no person or production account can be identified or reconstructed.

   Before exporting purportedly anonymized data, the named Pass 01 privacy responsibility and qualified legal/privacy reviewer must assess reconstruction against information already retained by the destination, publicly available information, and data available to other authorized recipients. Inspect direct identifiers, stable pseudonyms, rare combinations, and lookup keys. If irreversible nonreconstruction cannot be demonstrated for the proposed recipient and purpose, use synthetic data; the release remains blocked until the review and result are recorded.

2. <a id="p04-r02"></a>**P04.R02 — Fail-safe API startup validation**<br>A production API process must stop before opening its HTTP listener, and a production worker or scheduled process must stop before claiming or starting work, when a required startup setting is absent, has an invalid format, retains a development default, or identifies a development, test, preview, or demonstration resource.

3. <a id="p04-r03"></a>**P04.R03 — Database startup settings validation**<br>Startup validation must cover the PostgreSQL database and role; Firebase project and application; Stripe account mode, sandbox/live account, keys, and webhook configuration; Cloudflare R2 account, private/public buckets, and credentials; Postmark server and webhook credentials; Grafana/IRM monitoring destination and credentials; AWS S3 backup destination and role; browser and API addresses; allowed request hosts; cookie and session behavior; external-service addresses; and every private credential the process uses. The child plan must list the exact setting names used by each process. A production process must reject any listed setting that points to a non-production resource.

4. <a id="p04-r04"></a>**P04.R04 — Production runtime safeguards**<br>Production must disable automatic code reload, debug output, test-only routes, and development or preview servers. API documentation and the generated API description must be disabled publicly. If either is exposed in production, the request must pass the same server-side administrator authentication and authorization used by the current administrative API.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p04-v01"></a>**P04.V01 — Synthetic personal-data boundary check**<br>For Pass 04 requirement 1, use a synthetic marker that represents production personal data to attempt every path that could copy data into development, test, or a separate diagnostic dataset. Separately, in an isolated system, process representative synthetic records through the recorded anonymization method and attempt to reconstruct the synthetic people or accounts from its result; record which non-identifying production data characteristics the synthetic records represent. Confirm that each prohibited copy is rejected, ordinary tests use synthetic data, and data can leave production only after irreversible anonymization is verified.

   Attempt linkage of the synthetic result with representative destination-held, public, and other authorized-recipient data, including stable pseudonyms, rare combinations, and lookup keys. Before any real production-data export, separately review the actual production data categories and available linkage information under Pass 04 requirement 1 without copying identifiable production records into the isolated test system. Record the qualified review and observed reconstruction result; confirm synthetic data is used when irreversible nonreconstruction of real data for the proposed recipient and purpose is unproven.

**Development-stage manual verification:** For Pass 04 requirements 2–4, use the Section 3.2 development-stage build and isolated resources. Start the API, worker, and scheduler with complete valid development settings and record successful startup and shutdown.
