For each setting named for a process under Pass 04 requirement 3, exercise production-mode startup validation with that setting missing, malformed, retaining a development default, or bound to a development, test, preview, or demonstration resource; use synthetic setting values and stop before any production connection. Record the rejected setting class and process result, and confirm that the API opens no listener, the worker and scheduler claim or start no work, and no failed startup contacts or changes an external resource. In an isolated deployment using the production response and process policy, inspect reload, debug output, test-only routes, development servers, API documentation, and the generated description; confirm they are disabled publicly or, for documentation permitted by Pass 04 requirement 4, require the current administrator gate. These checks do not establish final production bindings; Pass 21 verification 3 inspects them after deployment.

#### Completion condition

The local stack and separately scoped real development integrations can start, fail safely, and shut down without accessing production resources; startup validation and synthetic-data controls have passing Pass 04 verification 1 and development-stage manual evidence for requirements 2–4. Pass 19 binds final production settings, and Pass 21 checks the deployed startup result.

---

<a id="p05"></a>

### Pass 05 — PostgreSQL Foundations

#### Purpose

Implement data integrity, transactional concurrency, PostgreSQL roles, the connection-budget calculation and configuration checks, schema migrations, sessions, query controls, and error handling before dependent application features. Final process values and the resulting connection budget are selected together in Pass 19.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p05-r01"></a>**P05.R01 — PostgreSQL integrity constraints**<br>PostgreSQL constraints must enforce required foreign-key relationships, unique Firebase-account links, unique memberships, unique active records where the domain permits only one, idempotency-key uniqueness within its operation scope, non-null required fields, valid enumerated states, and nonnegative monetary and capacity values. Booking-capacity, refund/credit-value, and job-claim invariants that cannot be expressed by one constraint must use the transaction and serialization rules below.

2. <a id="p05-r02"></a>**P05.R02 — Atomic business transactions**<br>A transaction must cover every group of database changes that represents one application outcome. A failure before commit must leave none of that outcome's partial state.

3. <a id="p05-r03"></a>**P05.R03 — Concurrent capacity and uniqueness claims**<br>Concurrent attempts to claim the final game spot, create the same membership, create the same account mapping, consume the same credit, or apply the same idempotent mutation must produce one valid outcome and no duplicate record or negative capacity.

4. <a id="p05-r04"></a>**P05.R04 — Capacity-limited operation concurrency**<br>For each operation that adds, removes, or transfers a capacity-limited membership, the child plan must identify the database record that determines whether capacity remains and the exact transaction that changes it. If both a stored counter and membership rows represent the same capacity, that transaction must lock or conditionally update the required rows, verify their relationship before commit, and reject or repair a mismatch without assigning the same place twice.

5. <a id="p05-r05"></a>**P05.R05 — Exact monetary storage**<br>Monetary values must use exact decimal or integer-minor-unit storage. Binary floating-point values must not be used for persisted prices, charges, refunds, credits, or balances.

6. <a id="p05-r06"></a>**P05.R06 — Timestamp storage and comparison**<br>PostgreSQL must store and compare timestamps that determine ordering, expiry, or financial state as UTC instants. The server or database must assign authoritative creation and processing times. A client-supplied date or time must be validated and converted to the required UTC instant before it affects stored state, and a client clock must not determine when the server records that an operation occurred.

7. <a id="p05-r07"></a>**P05.R07 — Parameterized SQL values**<br>SQL values must be bound as parameters. Dynamic identifiers or ordering choices must be selected from an allowlist before query construction.

8. <a id="p05-r08"></a>**P05.R08 — Relationship deletion decisions**<br>The child plan must inventory every relationship affected by the pass and assign one deletion behavior to it: reject deletion, delete the dependent record, set the reference to null, anonymize the record, or retain it under a cited Pass 16 requirement.

   **Decision source.** For each choice, cite the governing business requirement and the approved data-category treatment under Pass 02 requirements 3–5 and Pass 16; the database choice must satisfy both. If those sources do not determine one behavior, the accountable business and privacy/retention responsibilities must approve the missing decision before implementation of that relationship begins. A decision that changes required product behavior must amend this master under Section 3.4 before implementation.

   **Enforcement.** The database constraint and application behavior must implement the recorded choice. An undeclared database default is not an accepted deletion policy.

9. <a id="p05-r09"></a>**P05.R09 — Stable database error mapping**<br>Database errors returned to clients must map to stable application errors and must not expose SQL, table structure, credentials, or raw PostgreSQL-host diagnostics.

10. <a id="p05-r10"></a>**P05.R10 — Monetary field currency and storage**<br>Each monetary field must identify its currency field, storage unit, permitted precision, and rounding rule. Database defaults, SQLAlchemy defaults, and migration definitions for money, time, state, and identifiers must describe the same behavior and must not assign conflicting defaults.

11. <a id="p05-r11"></a>**P05.R11 — Critical business invariants**<br>Capacity, one-active-record, duplicate webhook, credit/refund, notice delivery, job claim, and concurrent administrative invariants must use a database serialization point (the database constraint or operation that decides which competing change succeeds) such as a constraint, atomic conditional write, locked row, or version check. A process-local lock cannot be the only serialization control across instances.

12. <a id="p05-r12"></a>**P05.R12 — Database failure classification**<br>The application must distinguish a deadlock, serialization failure, lock timeout, and response loss or connection failure that leaves commit success unknown. It may retry the entire transaction only when a database constraint, idempotency identity, or authoritative current-state check guarantees that repeating every contained database and external operation cannot create a second record, external effect, or state transition. Otherwise it must record or return an operation identity and an unresolved outcome before another attempt. Reconciliation must read the authoritative records for that operation and mark it succeeded, failed, or still unresolved without creating a duplicate effect.

13. <a id="p05-r13"></a>**P05.R13 — PostgreSQL connection budget**<br>The PostgreSQL connection-budget design must define how to add the largest possible simultaneous use by API, worker, and scheduler instances; each process's reusable and additional connections; migrations; monitoring; backups; administrative maintenance; autoscaling; and simultaneous current and incoming instances during deployment. It must identify the process, instance, concurrency, service-limit, and reserve inputs that the calculation will use. Pass 19 requirement 5 owns the final selected values and calculation.

14. <a id="p05-r14"></a>**P05.R14 — Pool and process configuration validation**<br>PostgreSQL pool and process configuration validation must reject a missing emergency and maintenance reserve or a calculated connection total that exceeds the supplied service limit. It must use the calculation method in Pass 05 requirement 13. Verification here may use test values; Pass 19 requirement 5 applies the selected production values and the selected service's enforced limit.

15. <a id="p05-r15"></a>**P05.R15 — PostgreSQL connection wait limits**<br>Each request and job type must accept a PostgreSQL connection-wait deadline and maximum attempt count through defined configuration fields. When an attempt cannot obtain a connection by the supplied deadline, it must return or record that operation's database-unavailable error, roll back and close its session, create no partial business effect, and stop after the supplied attempt count. Pass 19 requirement 9 chooses and applies the exact production values.

16. <a id="p05-r16"></a>**P05.R16 — Query inventory**<br>The child plan must list the queries used by high-traffic requests, administrative searches, cleanup tasks, and background jobs and identify how their execution plan, elapsed time, returned-row count, and plan-reported scan-row total will be measured.

    **Scan-row calculation.** Use `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, FORMAT JSON)` and sum the following expression over each included base-relation scan node:

    `Actual Loops × (Actual Rows + Rows Removed by Filter + Rows Removed by Index Recheck)`

    Included scan nodes are Seq Scan, Index Scan, Index Only Scan, Bitmap Heap Scan, Tid Scan, and Tid Range Scan. Treat an absent removal field as zero.

    **Exclusions and retained plan evidence.** Exclude child Bitmap Index Scan nodes so candidate rows are not counted twice; identify and include any other base-relation scan node under the same rule. Record the included nodes and fields with the plan. Each later flow must add its listed queries before that flow is accepted.

    **Final measurements and limits.** Pass 19 requirement 10 owns the measurements, final limits, and corrections at the projected record volume and planned peak workload.

17. <a id="p05-r17"></a>**P05.R17 — Indexed list endpoints**<br>Every endpoint that returns a list must define an indexed, deterministic ordering that includes a unique tie-breaker and must enforce a maximum page size chosen under Section 3.3. The endpoint must reject or cap a larger request according to its API contract and must not return an unbounded table or load an entire production dataset into application memory.

18. <a id="p05-r18"></a>**P05.R18 — Alembic migration and rollback plan**<br>Every schema change must have an Alembic migration, a forward execution check, and a defined rollback or forward-fix procedure (a tested new change that restores a compatible state when reversing the original change is unsafe). Application code deployed during a rolling release must tolerate the schema state shared with the adjacent release.

19. <a id="p05-r19"></a>**P05.R19 — Large-table migration safety**<br>A migration that rewrites, locks, backfills, or validates a table must identify its affected records, lock behavior, interruption points, and method for exercising it at the record volume projected from Pass 16. Pass 19 requirement 11 owns the service-specific lock and runtime limits and their measured result before release.

20. <a id="p05-r20"></a>**P05.R20 — Migration environment safeguards**<br>Every migration and maintenance command must require the intended environment and database identity as explicit input and must compare them with the connected database before its first change. A command intended for production must stop without changing data when the connected service, database, or environment does not match the production architecture record.

21. <a id="p05-r21"></a>**P05.R21 — Database role separation**<br>The API, worker, migration, backup, monitoring, read-only diagnostic, and human-administration uses of PostgreSQL must each have a recorded grant set. Two uses may share one database credential only when those recorded grant sets are identical; uses with different grant sets must use different credentials. No application credential may gain a broader grant merely to avoid creating the required separate role.

22. <a id="p05-r22"></a>**P05.R22 — Per-process SQLAlchemy engine**<br>Each process must create its own SQLAlchemy database engine and its reusable PostgreSQL connections after process start and must close those connections during shutdown. A live connection must not be inherited across a process fork.

23. <a id="p05-r23"></a>**P05.R23 — Database session lifecycle**<br>Each request, job attempt, webhook attempt, migration, and administrative operation must use its own database session. A failed flush or operation must roll back, and every session must close before that operation finishes. A Firebase, Stripe, or Cloudflare R2 call or response stream must occur outside an open database transaction unless the child plan cites the exact parent requirement that requires the transaction to remain open, identifies the database rows held during the call or stream, and verifies that timeout or failure releases those rows and rolls back within the lock-wait limit chosen under Section 3.3.

24. <a id="p05-r24"></a>**P05.R24 — Query-backed PostgreSQL indexes**<br>Indexes must correspond to actual filters, joins, authorization predicates, and ordering. The review for every query listed in Pass 05 requirement 16 must examine foreign-key indexes, composite and partial indexes, repeated per-record loading, PostgreSQL autovacuum, including automatic `ANALYZE` statistics updates, redundant indexes, and the added write cost of each retained index at the projected end-of-retention record volume.

25. <a id="p05-r25"></a>**P05.R25 — Restricted production application role**<br>The production application role must not own the database or act as superuser. The application, migration, worker, read-only/diagnostic, and backup role design must assign only the grants required by Pass 05 requirement 21 and identify schema ownership, `search_path` (the ordered schemas searched for an unqualified name), default privileges (grants applied to future objects, distinct from grants on existing objects), and direct human access. Pass 19 requirement 12 owns configuration on the selected PostgreSQL service; Pass 20 verifies its actual grants.

26. <a id="p05-r26"></a>**P05.R26 — Single intended Alembic head**<br>The repository must maintain one Alembic migration graph with one intended head; concurrent branches must be joined by a reviewed merge migration before release.

    The graph must identify which migration creates each required PostgreSQL extension and must include model/schema drift checks (comparing the SQLAlchemy model expected by the application with the Alembic-migrated PostgreSQL schema).

    A rolling schema change must first add structures that both adjacent application versions can use, deploy compatible code, and remove the old structures only after the old code no longer runs.

    A data change that cannot finish within the migration transaction and lock-duration limits chosen under Section 3.3 must divide work into bounded batches, record completed progress, resume after interruption without repeating a completed change, expose failure and remaining work, and support a documented recovery action for a failed batch.

27. <a id="p05-r27"></a>**P05.R27 — Protected pagination cursors**<br>A pagination cursor must be protected against modification and bound to the endpoint, sort direction, filter values, and authorization context that created it. Before implementation of each cursor endpoint begins, its contract must state its deterministic ordering and whether a row inserted, updated, or deleted after one page can appear on a later page, including how the cursor and current authorization context affect that result. Tests must perform each change between page requests and confirm the stated result, deterministic ordering, no repeated row, and no row that the caller is unauthorized to read.

28. <a id="p05-r28"></a>**P05.R28 — Reusable PostgreSQL engine**<br>Each application process must create one reusable PostgreSQL engine for that process. Firebase, Stripe, and Cloudflare R2 clients that keep network connections or other resources open must also be reused within the process and must release those resources during graceful shutdown using the cleanup operation supported by their library. Importing an application module must not contact a network service, run a database migration, or change production data.

#### Integration boundaries

- Pass 06 requirements 2–5, 15 supply the common error, input, response, data-model, timeout, and cancellation rules used by database-backed operations. Pass 05 requirement 12 supplies the PostgreSQL unknown-outcome rule referenced by Pass 06 requirement 15.
- This pass owns logical data correctness and the database serialization point for shared state. Pass 05 owns connection limits, query operation, database roles, and execution of schema changes. Pass 13 owns comparison with Stripe and reconciliation of financial outcomes. Pass 16 owns the purpose, retention end date or event, and final deletion or anonymization treatment for personal data.
- Pass 12 requirements 5–10, Pass 05 requirements 15, 18 and 19, 23, 26 and 27, Pass 13 requirements 1–12, 16–18, 20–24, Pass 11 requirements 1–7, Pass 14 requirements 4, 8 and 9, 11, 13 and 14, Pass 09 requirements 8–14, and Pass 15 requirement 6; Pass 16 requirements 1, 4 and 5 consume one or more of the constraints, transactions, concurrency controls, and unknown-outcome rules defined here. Each child plan must cite the exact Pass 05 requirement used by its operation.
- The Pass 05 child plan must cite the exact upstream requirement and downstream operation for each invariant. Completion of an entire parent pass is not a substitute for proving the individual database rule that an operation uses.

- Pass 05 requirements 13–16, 19, and 25 define the calculation, configuration fields, query and migration inventories, and role design that can be implemented before final production values only under Section 3.3's configuration-only rule. Pass 19 requirements 5 and 9–12 apply the selected service, process, workload, and volume inputs to those contracts. Pass 20 requirement 5 starts as soon as a production-only PostgreSQL check is identified and remains incomplete until evidence from the exact production service and setting closes it. If a missing fact could change the Pass 05 design or verification method rather than only a defined configuration value, that affected requirement remains unstarted under Section 3.3.
- Pass 05 requirements 1–12; Pass 20 requirement 4 supply the constraints, transactions, serialization, and unknown-outcome handling used during query and migration work. Pass 02 requirements 3 and 4 and the Section 3.3 workload record supply the initial end-of-retention volume projection for Pass 05 requirement 24; Pass 19 requirements 10 and 11 use the updated projection after Pass 16 integrates the implemented stores. Pass 01 requirements 3, 6; Pass 03 requirements 1–3; Pass 10 requirement 8; Pass 19 requirements 3 and 4 supply production credential storage, separation, and human-access controls consumed by Pass 05 requirements 20, 21, and 25 and Pass 19 requirement 12.
- Pass 11 consumes the session and query rules in Pass 05 requirements 15 and 16, 22–24. Pass 20 consumes the final connection calculation in Pass 19 requirement 5 and the migration compatibility results in Pass 05 requirements 18, 19, and 26 and Pass 19 requirement 11. Pass 15 owns backup restoration and consumes the PostgreSQL roles and migration state in Pass 05 requirements 21, 25, and 26 and Pass 19 requirement 12.
- The Pass 05 child plan must cite each exact upstream input and downstream operation. Completion of Pass 05, Pass 16, Pass 01, or Pass 20 as a whole is not required when the individual cited requirement is complete and no undecided infrastructure fact can change the work.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p05-v01"></a>**P05.V01 — Process import, startup, and shutdown**<br>For Pass 05 requirement 28, import each application module without running startup, then start and gracefully stop every process type. Record that import performs no network call, migration, or production change; that each process creates only its intended reusable clients; and that shutdown releases every resource whose library requires cleanup.

2. <a id="p05-v02"></a>**P05.V02 — Monetary storage and migration inspection**<br>For Pass 05 requirements 5–7, 10, inspect the PostgreSQL and SQLAlchemy types, defaults, migrations, currency fields, precision, rounding operations, timestamp creation, client-date conversion, parameter binding, and every allowlisted dynamic identifier or ordering choice. Exercise fractional and boundary monetary inputs, a misleading client clock, an injection value, and an unlisted sort or identifier. Confirm exact monetary results, UTC ordering, server-controlled operation times, parameterized values, and rejection of every unlisted query choice.

3. <a id="p05-v03"></a>**P05.V03 — Database retry and unknown-outcome cases**<br>For Pass 05 requirement 12, produce a deadlock, serialization failure, lock timeout, lost response before commit, and lost response after commit for one operation that meets Pass 05 requirement 12's retry precondition and one that does not. Record the transaction attempts, operation identity, authoritative database result, reconciliation state, and final outcome. Confirm that only the first operation retries the whole transaction, the second and every unknown outcome remain unrepeated until reconciliation, and no scenario creates a duplicate effect.

4. <a id="p05-v04"></a>**P05.V04 — Migration command environment checks**<br>For Pass 05 requirement 20, run each migration and maintenance command once against its intended isolated database and once with a mismatched service, database, or environment identity. Inspect the target before and after. Confirm that the correct target changes only as intended and every mismatch stops before the first change.

5. <a id="p05-v05"></a>**P05.V05 — Per-process engine and pool checks**<br>For Pass 05 requirements 22 and 23, start multiple API and worker processes, verify that each creates its reusable PostgreSQL connection pool after process start, and shut each down. Cause a failed flush, operation failure, external-service timeout, response-stream cancellation, and process shutdown during database work. Inspect sessions, transactions, locks, and inherited file descriptors or connections. Confirm deterministic rollback and closure, no live connection crosses a process fork, and no external call or stream holds a transaction unless its recorded exception and failure result satisfy Pass 05 requirement 23.

**Development-stage manual verification:** For Pass 05 requirements 1–4, 11, 14 and 15, 18, 19 and 26, use the Section 3.2 development-stage build and isolated PostgreSQL. Exercise representative implemented foreign-key, uniqueness, required-field, valid-state, and nonnegative-value constraints; interrupt a multi-write outcome before commit; and race two processes for one uniquely constrained record and, when its path exists, one capacity-limited record. Inspect every affected row, counter, constraint result, and caller error; confirm one valid concurrent outcome, agreement of capacity representations where both exist, and no duplicate, negative, or partial state. Apply an Alembic migration forward, exercise its defined rollback or forward fix and any interruption point applicable to that migration, and inspect the graph head, affected records, and adjacent-code compatibility that can be checked at this stage. Using recorded test values rather than assumed production limits, reject a missing reserve and a calculated connection total above the supplied service limit; exhaust an isolated pool for a representative request and job operation or their test harnesses and confirm their configured connection-wait deadlines and attempt counts stop with the database-unavailable result, rollback, closed sessions, and no partial business or external effect. Record inputs, expected and actual responses, database changes, and prohibited-effect checks for each exercise. Each later request and job type must verify its configured database-unavailable behavior, and each later feature must verify its own database invariant, before that feature completes. The projected-volume migration exercise and service-specific limits remain with Passes 19 and 20; Pass 20 verifications 11–17 cover all released write paths, final limits, and migration overlap.

#### Completion condition

Shared database invariants, schema and migration mechanics, connection calculation and configuration-rejection behavior, configurable wait/attempt handling, query and migration inventories, and role design exist, with passing development-stage manual verification and Pass 05 verification 1–5 clauses that exercise operations implemented by this pass. Record each clause requiring a later request, job, or business writer and its implementation trigger as incomplete; verify it before that writer's owning feature completes. Pass 19 selects and applies the service-specific values, measurements, and grants. Cross-feature write-path coverage is checked again in Pass 20 once all writers exist.

---

<a id="p06"></a>

### Pass 06 — Backend API and HTTP Foundations

#### Purpose

Implement the API, request validation, HTTP policy, routing, shared durable state, response contracts, safe methods, lifecycle endpoints, and bounded time budgets; reserve browser-wide CSP verification for the implemented frontend.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p06-r01"></a>**P06.R01 — Stateless API process state**<br>API instances must keep authoritative sessions, jobs, locks, uploads, and user data in shared durable systems rather than process memory. Frontend, API, worker, and scheduler responsibilities must have explicit deployment and shutdown behavior.

2. <a id="p06-r02"></a>**P06.R02 — Stable JSON error responses**<br>Every JSON error response must contain a stable error identifier for program logic, a message safe to show the caller, the HTTP status, and the request identifier used to find related server records. Validation errors, malformed JSON, Firebase failures, Stripe failures, Cloudflare R2 failures, PostgreSQL failures, hosting failures, timeouts, and unexpected exceptions must not return a stack trace, credential, private key, service secret, SQL statement, internal file path, or raw request content.

3. <a id="p06-r03"></a>**P06.R03 — Request-field validation**<br>Before using request data, the backend must validate every accepted field's name, data type, format, length, numeric or date range, and permitted choices. An idempotency key identifies one business attempt so repeating that attempt cannot create a second effect; reusing the key with different input conflicts. For a field the route contract treats as singular and security-sensitive, including authentication, authorization, target identity, price, capacity, idempotency, and redirect fields, the request must be rejected before authorization or business work if the field appears more than once in one input source, appears in conflicting sources, or has malformed or inconsistently decoded names or values; request handling and business logic must use the same parsed value. An encoded input must be decoded to its single canonical representation (one decoded value that validation and business logic both use) before validation and must not be decoded again or reinterpreted after validation; intermediary and API parsing must not produce different authorized values. An operation that can change ownership, role, price, capacity, payment state, moderation state, or administrator authority must reject an unrecognized field rather than ignore it.

4. <a id="p06-r04"></a>**P06.R04 — Data model separation**<br>Public, authenticated-user, administrator, request-input, and internal data models must be separate whenever their allowed fields differ. Response construction must prevent internal fields, Firebase-only fields, Stripe-only fields, Cloudflare-R2-only fields, administrator-only fields, and fields belonging to another user from leaving the backend.

5. <a id="p06-r05"></a>**P06.R05 — Safe GET, HEAD, and OPTIONS methods**<br>`GET`, `HEAD`, and `OPTIONS` requests must not change business records or initiate a payment, booking, message, upload, deletion, or background job. An unsupported method must return `405` and identify the methods the route accepts. A request using an unsupported content type must return `415`. A missing or invalid user-authentication credential must return `401`; a valid user identity without permission must return `403`, except where the affected endpoint family records a concealed `404` under Pass 08 requirement 10. A `204` response must have no body. API timestamps must include a time-zone offset, identifiers represented as UUIDs must use the canonical hyphenated form, and a monetary amount must use a whole number of cents together with its three-letter currency code. Each request and response schema must distinguish an omitted field from a field whose explicit value is `null` whenever the two inputs cause different behavior. A `429` or temporary `503` response may invite an automatic retry only by returning the delay in a `Retry-After` header.

   **Response precedence when more than one fault applies.** The public request path must use the first applicable result in this order:

    1. **TLS:** A TLS failure ends the connection.
    2. **Host:** The first component that validates the requested host rejects an unlisted host without deriving a redirect target from it.
    3. **HTTPS redirect:** For a listed host, an HTTP request follows the recorded HTTPS redirect before API method, content-type, authentication, App Check, or authorization evaluation and performs no protected operation.
    4. **CORS preflight:** On HTTPS, a browser CORS preflight follows Pass 06 requirement 10 before user authentication or App Check; it does not execute the requested route.
    5. **Method:** For other HTTPS requests, an unsupported method returns `405`.
    6. **Content type:** An unsupported content type then returns `415`.
    7. **Authentication:** A missing or invalid required user credential then returns `401`.
    8. **App Check:** Classified browser requests then follow the App Check `403` or `503` result in Pass 10 requirement 4.
    9. **Authorization:** Insufficient authorization then returns `403` or the recorded concealed `404`.

    **Exceptions and boundaries:** A route that does not require user authentication skips that check; Stripe webhooks, non-browser callbacks, and direct signed R2 requests keep their own credential and signature rules and do not acquire App Check through this ordering. An unlisted actual browser origin never receives headers authorizing credentialed response access. The response policy must record the host and preflight rejection responses so a request with two faults has one predictable result. No earlier rejection may disclose protected content or execute later route or business work.

6. <a id="p06-r06"></a>**P06.R06 — API description request and response contracts**<br>The generated API description must list the request content types, accepted fields, response fields, status codes, and error forms implemented by each released route. Every released Pickup Lane API response with a body must send a `Content-Type` that matches its actual media type; textual responses must declare a safe character encoding when that media type defines or permits a charset parameter, and JSON response bodies must be valid UTF-8 rather than being mislabeled as another content type. Continuous integration must compare that description with route-level request and response tests. Generating the description from the same application code does not by itself prove that the route behaves as described. A changed route must be rejected when a currently deployed or incoming version of any caller that can remain active during deployment fails its contract test against the other backend version. The caller list must include the player frontend, administrator frontend, any worker or scheduled process that calls the API, and every independently deployed external client that exists at the time of the release.

7. <a id="p06-r07"></a>**P06.R07 — Independent client release compatibility**<br>A public API version is required before a client released independently from Pickup Lane depends on the API. Until that occurs, each deployment must keep every HTTP caller listed in Pass 06 requirement 6 compatible with both the currently deployed and incoming backend for the period in which either backend can receive its requests. Compatibility among adjacent worker, scheduler, and database-schema versions is verified with the same release under Pass 20 requirement 10.

8. <a id="p06-r08"></a>**P06.R08 — Application liveness endpoint**<br>The public `/live` endpoint must return success only after application startup has completed; it must not test PostgreSQL, Firebase, Stripe, or Cloudflare R2. The public `/ready` endpoint must return success only after startup has completed and the application can open a PostgreSQL connection. A new API instance must not receive traffic before `/ready` succeeds. If `/ready` fails on an already-serving instance, PostgreSQL-dependent requests that arrive before Render withdraws traffic under P20.R09 must fail safely without unauthorized booking, payment, capacity, job, or external-service mutations. Both responses must disable caching and contain only their state and the deployed release identity. A separate dependency-diagnostic endpoint, including `/db-health` if retained, must be disabled publicly or require administrator authentication and authorization.

9. <a id="p06-r09"></a>**P06.R09 — Policy and headers**<br>After the public request path is recorded, one named component must own each redirect from HTTP to HTTPS and each transport or response-security setting, including `Content-Security-Policy`. The policy must state the exact effective `Strict-Transport-Security`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, frame-embedding restriction, `Permissions-Policy`, and `Cache-Control` header for application HTML, static files, API responses, downloads, and Pickup Lane-hosted return pages and redirects reached after Stripe or Firebase sends the browser back. Pickup Lane's header policy does not claim control of HTTP responses served by Stripe or Firebase; the application-owned return response received by the caller must match the policy and must not contain conflicting header fields or enter a redirect loop. Public frontend and API responses must include `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. Pass 17 requirement 2 determines the exact effective `Content-Security-Policy` after its browser-destination inventory is complete.

   **HSTS:** For a production hostname under Pickup Lane's control, HTTPS responses must carry `Strict-Transport-Security` with a `max-age` of at least `31536000` seconds. `includeSubDomains` may be enabled on a Pickup Lane-controlled domain only after every affected subdomain is inventoried and confirmed capable of maintaining valid HTTPS throughout that period. If this cannot be established, the policy must protect each production hostname without `includeSubDomains` and record the affected subdomain and the resulting OWASP Application Security Verification Standard (ASVS) Level 2 exception; it must not impose a policy that makes an existing subdomain inaccessible. A shared provider-owned hostname must retain the hosting provider's HSTS policy rather than impose a policy on its parent domain.

   **Security-sensitive cookies:** If Pickup Lane sets a cookie containing a credential or other security-sensitive value, it must use `Secure`, an explicit `SameSite` policy, and `HttpOnly` unless browser code must read that exact value; a cross-site `SameSite=None` exception must identify the required flow and retain `Secure`. This condition does not authorize cookie-based authentication. A security-sensitive cookie that is intended for one host must use the `__Host-` prefix, omit the `Domain` attribute, and use `Path=/`; a cookie intentionally shared across hosts must use `__Secure-` and have the exact host-sharing purpose and domain recorded in the response policy.

   **Transport:** Every public frontend and API hostname, including an alternate provider hostname that serves Pickup Lane, must serve protected content only over HTTPS with a currently valid, trusted certificate for that hostname. An HTTP request must be redirected without processing an authenticated, administrative, booking, payment, or webhook operation.

10. <a id="p06-r10"></a>**P06.R10 — Credentialed browser request CORS**<br>For a browser request carrying a user credential, the Cross-Origin Resource Sharing policy must return an allowed-origin header only when the request's exact origin is on the production origin list. It must never use `*` for a credentialed request. The policy must list the allowed methods and request headers. A request or browser preflight (the separate `OPTIONS` request a browser sends before certain cross-origin requests) from any other origin must not receive headers that authorize the browser to read the response with credentials.

    A valid preflight for a classified browser API route must complete without a user credential or App Check proof after the exact origin, requested method, and requested headers have been checked against the recorded route and CORS policy. A preflight with an unlisted origin, method, or header must not receive authorizing CORS headers or execute the requested route. Handling a preflight this way is a protocol check, not an exemption of the subsequent browser API request: when App Check is enforced, that actual classified request must still provide valid proof and pass its own authentication and authorization checks. A non-preflight `OPTIONS` request follows the method contract in Pass 06 requirement 5.

11. <a id="p06-r11"></a>**P06.R11 — Public request path trust**<br>After the public request path is recorded, the application must accept only the production hostnames and canonical hostname recorded for that path. It may trust information added by a proxy about the original scheme, hostname, client address, or port only when the request came through the recorded proxy path. A caller that sends those headers directly must not change routing, authorization, redirect destinations, security decisions, or the client address used by an abuse control.

12. <a id="p06-r12"></a>**P06.R12 — Public response cache policy**<br>The recorded response policy must state which public responses may be cached, for how long, and what event makes the cached response stale. Responses containing private messages, account information, bookings, sensitive financial information, or administrative information must use `Cache-Control: no-store` so neither a browser nor an intermediary HTTP cache retains them. Other authenticated responses and downloads containing user-specific information must instruct shared caches not to store them. A cache must not return one user's or administrator's information to another caller.

13. <a id="p06-r13"></a>**P06.R13 — Request and upload size limits**<br>Each request-size, request-header-size, URL-size, multipart-part-count, multipart-part-header-size, uploaded-file-size, decoded-file-size, query, pagination, search, export, bulk-operation, execution-time, and simultaneous-work limit must identify what is counted, the exact point where the application or selected production service rejects excess input or work, the returned error, and the effects that rejection must prevent. Raw request-byte limits must run before body parsing. Multipart part counts, part headers, and encoded file bytes must be checked while the upload is read and before the file is stored or processed. A limit that can be known only after safe decoding, including decoded image dimensions or pixel count, must run immediately after decoding and before publication or another business change. Field-count, item-count, page-size, search, export, and bulk-operation limits must run after the information needed to evaluate them is decoded but before work exceeding the limit begins. Time and simultaneous-work limits must be enforced at the operation they protect. Settings not fixed by Pass 06 requirement 14 must be chosen under Section 3.3.

14. <a id="p06-r14"></a>**P06.R14 — JSON request body limit**<br>The application must reject an ordinary JSON request after `65,536` received bytes, `POST /admin/platform-notices` after `163,840` received bytes, and signed `POST /stripe/webhook` after `65,536` received bytes. The count applies to the bytes actually received, regardless of a missing or incorrect length header, and must occur before JSON parsing, Stripe event construction, database mutation, or another business change. Route classification must keep the ordinary route, platform-notice route, and Stripe-webhook route separate. Changing one of these three limits requires an amendment to this master.

15. <a id="p06-r15"></a>**P06.R15 — End-to-end timeout behavior**<br>After the final request path and hosting services are selected, choose the following time limits under Section 3.3:

    - Pass 06: request-body-read, total-request, and public-proxy limits.
    - Pass 05: PostgreSQL connection, statement, and lock-wait limits.
    - Pass 07: Firebase identity-operation limits.
    - Pass 16: Firebase account-deletion limits.
    - Pass 13: Stripe limits.
    - Pass 14: Cloudflare R2 limits.
    - Pass 17: the supported-client waiting limit.

    For work performed while answering an HTTP request, each database or external-service operation must finish or stop before the total application-request limit, the application limit must expire before the public proxy stops waiting, and the public-proxy limit must expire before the supported client's configured waiting limit. The recorded timeout budget must assign a numeric difference between each adjacent deadline, and verification must show that the inner layer's recorded error reaches the outer layer before the outer deadline.

    A user closing a page or deliberately cancelling a request is a cancellation event rather than a configured client-timeout failure and must follow the same cleanup rules. Background-job time limits and retries follow Pass 11 requirement 5 instead of the HTTP-request ordering.

    A timeout or cancellation must close the request's database session, roll back an uncommitted transaction, release an upload stream, and release any other resource held for that request.

    An operation whose external or database change may have succeeded despite the timeout must not be repeated blindly. PostgreSQL outcomes follow Pass 05 requirement 12; Firebase identity outcomes follow Pass 07 requirement 13; Firebase account-deletion outcomes follow Pass 16 requirement 5; Stripe and booking outcomes follow Pass 13 requirements 4, 9, 11, 16, 18, 24; durable-job outcomes follow Pass 11 requirement 5; and Cloudflare R2 outcomes follow Pass 14 requirements 9, 11.

   For each HTTP caller class, record its actual outer waiting deadline: the supported browser, each Stripe or other provider webhook/callback documented by that provider, and every other independently deployed caller under its application-controlled contract. Set the inner request, proxy, and caller ordering with numeric margins under Section 3.3 for that class. If an external caller deadline is not documented or verified, do not claim that its response is delivered before that deadline; keep the affected delivery result incomplete.

#### Integration boundaries

- This pass owns production startup validation and behavior shared by HTTP requests. Pass 18 requirement 18 assigns responsibility for repeated-request controls. The pass that owns any other booking, payment, message, identity, image, or administrative behavior owns that operation's business rule and prohibited effects.
- Pass 06 requirements 5 and 10 define the HTTP response order and preflight contract that Pass 10 requirements 4 and 5 later consume. Pass 06's HTTP work does not wait for App Check implementation; the combined behavior remains unverified until the Pass 20 release exercise checks both.
- Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL-hosting, and application-hosting permissions belong to Pass 01. Final verification that production uses the correct accounts and resources depends on Pass 01 requirements 3, 6; Pass 03 requirements 1 and 2.
- The fixed body limits in Pass 06 requirement 14 do not require another decision under Section 3.3. Every other numeric request, header, execution-time, or simultaneous-work setting follows Section 3.3 unless an existing recorded requirement or decision fixes it.
- Pass 17 requirement 1 supplies the browser-destination inventory consumed by Pass 17 requirement 2. Pass 17 requirement 2 must not begin before that inventory exists, and Pass 17 requirement 1 does not depend on Pass 17 requirement 2.
- Pass 06 requirements 6 and 7 define the HTTP contracts that Pass 20 requirement 10 consumes. Their final adjacent-release evidence is produced jointly with the Pass 20 rolling-deployment exercise; defining and testing each individual HTTP contract does not depend on that exercise.
- Pass 01 requirement 4 supplies the production architecture and public request path consumed by Pass 01 requirement 5; Pass 06 requirements 8–11, 15. Those requirements must not begin while that information remains undecided. Pass 04 requirements 2–4; Pass 06 requirements 2–7, 12–14; Pass 07 requirement 15; Pass 18 requirement 18 except Pass 18 requirement 18's workflow-specific results, and Pass 05 requirement 28 may proceed earlier only when their implementation and verification do not depend on the missing architecture.
- Passes 06, 07, 08, 17, 18, and 20 consume individual requirements from this pass. Their child plans must name those requirements rather than treating completion of the entire parent pass as one prerequisite.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p06-v01"></a>**P06.V01 — Error and rejection response cases**<br>For Pass 06 requirements 2, 12, produce each defined validation, authentication, authorization, malformed-request, dependency, timeout, and unexpected error and inspect the returned fields and headers. Confirm that each response has the documented safe form, that private system information is absent, that shared caches cannot store authenticated or administrative content, and that one caller cannot receive another caller's cached information. With normal browser caching enabled, request a private message, account record, booking, sensitive financial response, and administrative response, then log out and repeat each request without authorization; confirm `no-store` on each sensitive response and that neither the browser HTTP cache nor an intermediary HTTP cache serves its protected content.

2. <a id="p06-v02"></a>**P06.V02 — Released route request coverage**<br>For Pass 06 requirements 6 and 7, exercise every released route with the accepted request and each documented error case, then compare the observed content types, fields, status codes, and error forms with the generated API description. For every response with a body, compare its actual media type and bytes with the advertised `Content-Type`, inspect a declared charset for text-based formats when that parameter is defined or permitted, and confirm that JSON responses use UTF-8 and no response is misrepresented as executable HTML. Record a missing or mismatched media type or required charset as a failed contract check. For every changed route, test every HTTP caller listed in Pass 06 requirement 6 in both directions: the currently deployed caller against the incoming backend and the incoming caller against the currently deployed backend. During the adjacent-release exercise in Pass 20 requirement 10, also run the current and incoming worker, scheduler, and database-schema combinations that can coexist. Record that an incompatible combination blocks release until the deployment sequence removes the overlap or restores compatibility.

3. <a id="p06-v03"></a>**P06.V03 — Safe HTTP method verification**<br>For Pass 06 requirement 5, exercise `GET`, `HEAD`, and `OPTIONS` on routes where another method changes state; every unsupported method and content type; missing, invalid, and insufficient credentials; and every response contract for `204`, timestamps, UUIDs, money, omitted fields, explicit `null`, `429`, and temporary `503`. Record the exact status, headers, body, and stored or external state. Confirm that read-only methods cause no business change, `204` has no body, each serialized field uses the required form, omitted and `null` inputs produce their defined results, and a retryable response contains `Retry-After`.

   For the response order in Pass 06 requirement 5, submit requests with two simultaneous faults: an unlisted host and an HTTP scheme, an allowed HTTP host and an unsupported method, an unlisted preflight origin and a missing App Check proof, an allowed preflight with an unlisted requested method or header, an unsupported method and missing user credential, an unsupported content type and missing user credential, missing user credential and missing App Check proof, and valid user authentication with missing App Check proof and insufficient authorization. Include a valid preflight followed by its actual classified request with valid and invalid proof. Record the response at the first enforcing component, response status and CORS headers, and whether later authentication, App Check, authorization, route, database, job, or external-service work ran. Confirm the first applicable result in the recorded policy wins, an invalid preflight never authorizes credentialed response access, a valid preflight does not bypass the later proof or permission check, and no rejected request creates a prohibited effect or discloses protected content.

4. <a id="p06-v04"></a>**P06.V04 — Production startup and health endpoints**<br>For Pass 06 requirement 8, start the production release normally and record successful `/live` and `/ready` responses. In an isolated application-level test, set the application lifecycle to not started and record that both endpoints return failure; this test does not claim that an HTTP listener exists before real startup completes. With the running process unchanged, make PostgreSQL unavailable and record that `/live` continues to succeed while `/ready` fails. In staging with Render's configured `/ready` health check, send controlled PostgreSQL-dependent requests during the failure interval and record any that arrive before an already-serving instance is withdrawn after 15 consecutive seconds of failed checks; confirm they create no unauthorized booking, payment, capacity, job, or external-service mutation. Observe Render's restart after 60 consecutive seconds of failed checks even while `/live` succeeds, and record recovery after PostgreSQL returns. Call each retained dependency-diagnostic endpoint without and with administrator access. Record every status, body, cache header, and traffic decision, and confirm that a public response contains only state and release identity.

**Development-stage manual verification:** For Pass 06 requirements 9–11, 13 and 14, use the Section 3.2 development-stage build and the recorded development request path after Pass 01 requirements 4 and 5 have fixed its applicable components. Send the response kinds named in Pass 06 requirement 9 and record the effective non-CSP security, cache, and security-sensitive cookie headers, HTTPS behavior, and HTTP redirect result; confirm the recorded component owns each setting and no redirect executes protected work. Exercise a listed and unlisted host, a directly supplied proxy header and one supplied through the trusted path, and allowed and disallowed browser origins, preflight methods, and headers. Record the response and authorizing CORS headers; confirm that direct or unlisted input cannot change routing, authorization, redirect destinations, or client-address decisions or authorize credentialed response access. For ordinary JSON, `POST /admin/platform-notices`, and signed `POST /stripe/webhook`, exercise the body-read and route-classification boundary using the actual route when implemented or an isolated handler harness before its downstream feature exists; the later feature must repeat the check on its actual route before that feature completes. Send the largest contract-valid request at or below the fixed received-byte limit and a correctly framed request one byte above that limit through the applicable development ingress, including a missing or incorrect length header in separate attempts. If a request of exactly the fixed maximum is not contract-valid for another reason, send one at that size as well and distinguish its later validation result from a size rejection. Record the bytes received, first rejection point, response, and database, job, payment, and external-service state. Confirm the largest contract-valid request is accepted, a request at the fixed maximum is not rejected for size alone, and the first excessive request is rejected before parsing or business work without a prohibited effect. This development-path evidence does not establish the final provider settings or deployed public-path behavior; Pass 20 verifications 1 and 2 and Pass 21 verification 2 check those final paths. Pass 17 requirement 2 supplies the later exact CSP check.

#### Completion condition

Backend request, response, lifecycle, error, redirect, and non-CSP header contracts are implemented and development-tested through the development-stage manual verification for requirements 9–11 and the fixed-limit portion of requirements 13 and 14, plus Pass 06 verification 1–4 clauses that exercise routes and controls implemented by this pass. Record each clause requiring App Check or a route owned by a later feature and its implementation trigger as incomplete; verify it before that feature completes. The exact browser-derived CSP policy belongs to Pass 17 requirement 2; final request-path, other Section 3.3 limits, and capacity evidence belong to Pass 20.

---

<a id="p07"></a>

### Pass 07 — Firebase Identity and Account Foundations

#### Purpose

Implement Firebase identity verification, account linkage, age eligibility, email gates, account onboarding, session rules, non-enumerating recovery, and terms acceptance on top of the approved legal decision.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p07-r01"></a>**P07.R01 — Firebase identifier validation**<br>Before using a Firebase user identifier, the backend must verify the Firebase ID token's signature, Firebase project, intended audience, expiry, revocation state, and required claims through Firebase Admin verification. An expired, malformed, revoked, wrong-project, wrong-audience, unsigned, or incorrectly signed token must not authenticate the request or establish the caller's Pickup Lane identity.

2. <a id="p07-r02"></a>**P07.R02 — Firebase ID token handling**<br>When sent to the Pickup Lane API, Firebase ID tokens must travel only in the HTTPS `Authorization` header. Pickup Lane must not accept them from URLs or forms, forward them to unrelated services, or include them in logs or telemetry. Google sign-in must use the supported Firebase browser SDK provider flow and the production Firebase and Google authorized-domain and redirect configuration; a callback, return destination, or provider result outside that configuration or unrelated to the initiating sign-in transaction must not establish Pickup Lane identity or redirect the browser to an untrusted origin. Pickup Lane must not implement or accept a separate client-supplied OAuth result. Firebase SDK-managed authentication persistence under Pass 07 requirement 9 is permitted; Pickup Lane application code must not independently copy Firebase ID tokens, OAuth access tokens, or Firebase refresh tokens into browser storage outside that SDK-managed persistence.

3. <a id="p07-r03"></a>**P07.R03 — Firebase identity authority**<br>Firebase owns authentication identity, email verification, and other Firebase authentication facts. PostgreSQL owns application profile, role, account state, permissions, and ownership. PostgreSQL copies of Firebase facts are snapshots and must not become independent authorization sources. Ordinary users must not write UID, verification, role, account-state, or administrator-controlled fields.

   The automatic Firebase-to-PostgreSQL copy is limited to the stable UID link and current email and email-verification display snapshots obtained from a verified read of that UID. Synchronization must not replace PostgreSQL profile, role, account state, permission, ownership, or other application-owned fields. A snapshot cannot authorize an operation; current verification follows Pass 07 requirements 1, 7 and 8.

4. <a id="p07-r04"></a>**P07.R04 — Stable Firebase account linking**<br>PostgreSQL must link each application account to one stable Firebase user identifier. Concurrent first requests for the same Firebase identity must create or return one application account. Repeated requests from that UID may synchronize permitted Firebase values and repair missing account-context rows without creating a second profile. A different UID must not take over the account because it presents the same email address.

   Automatic synchronization may create only a UID-linked provisional account/profile with the minimum age, verification, recovery, and terms state required to complete access setup. It may repair that context only for the same verified UID and must not create roles, memberships, bookings, payments, or active privileges for an unverified or otherwise ineligible account. It may update only the verified same-UID Firebase email and email-verification display snapshots described in Pass 07 requirement 3; a matching email from another UID cannot link accounts.

5. <a id="p07-r05"></a>**P07.R05 — Adult registration and validated birth date**<br>Registered Pickup Lane accounts and participation in every game type are limited to people at least 18 years old. Before implementing the age gate, the assigned Pass 01 identity and privacy responsibilities must approve and record one calendar time zone for the authoritative server date and one rule for when a February 29 birthday reaches age 18 in a non-leap year. Email signup, Google sign-in, Firebase identity synchronization, profile completion or change, every direct backend or API account-creation path, and later age revalidation must use that same date and birthday rule to enforce a valid date of birth establishing age 18 or older before an application account becomes active or any registered-user or game operation is allowed. A Firebase identity or provisional PostgreSQL record created before age is established must not grant that access; a missing, invalid, or under-18 birth date and a restored session must not bypass the gate.

    **Restriction and report threshold.** When a validated birth date or a reviewed credible-evidence determination establishes that an existing user is under 18, the next protected backend operation must deny access and the account must remain inactive while its information is treated. Before launch, the assigned identity/privacy role must record the sources and minimum account-linked evidence that make an underage report credible, the person who applies temporary restriction, the numeric maximum time to review a restricted report under Section 3.3, and the action when that review deadline is missed. An unsupported allegation may be investigated but does not by itself meet the restriction threshold. A report meeting the recorded threshold requires temporary restriction and investigation, not a confirmed-age finding.

    **Investigation outcomes.** A validated date of birth establishing an age below 18 is an underage determination. A documented report or other evidence meeting the recorded credibility threshold must cause temporary restriction while the assigned identity/privacy role reviews it; the report alone must not be recorded as a confirmed age finding. The review must record the evidence, actor, time, and one of three outcomes: confirmed underage, disproved underage, or unresolved. A confirmed determination keeps the account inactive and invokes Pass 16 treatment; a disproved report ends the report-based restriction without changing any independent account restriction; an unresolved result keeps the restriction and an assigned next review or escalation visible without treating age as confirmed.

    **Stored information and terminal treatment.** For an under-18 person whose information reached Firebase, PostgreSQL, or another inventoried store before rejection or discovery, the Pass 16 inventory must record each collected category and store and its terminal treatment: delete the information, or restrict only the exact fields, access, and duration required by a cited legal obligation. Deletion and unknown processor results must follow Pass 15 requirement 6; Pass 16 requirements 2–5; an unfinished result must not reactivate the account.

    **Separate under-13 legal determination.** Before signoff, the assigned Pass 01 privacy responsibility must determine whether the deployed service is directed to children under 13 under the Children's Online Privacy Protection Rule (16 C.F.R. Part 312); the 18-and-older policy alone does not establish that it is not child-directed.

    For a general-audience service that gains actual knowledge of a child under 13, further collection, use, or disclosure for registration or application features must stop, and previously collected information must be deleted without delay unless a recorded, legally supported exception permits the exact restricted retention. Processing needed to perform that deletion or comply with the cited obligation remains permitted.

    A child-directed or unresolved classification must receive a qualified legal decision on its required COPPA treatment before signoff.

    The qualified legal review must decide what evidence establishes actual knowledge of a child under 13 and any narrow lawful retention; unresolved legal or procedure decisions block signoff.

    The under-13 legal rule is distinct from Pickup Lane's 18-and-older product rule for ages 13 through 17. This requirement does not authorize parental-consent registration or an additional age-verification service.



6. <a id="p07-r06"></a>**P07.R06 — Registration age restriction**<br>Subject to the age restriction in Pass 07 requirement 5, an unverified-email user may sign in, create or repair the application profile and account-context records required to request verification or recover access, browse public content, request verification or recovery, and sign out.

   The provisional account and repair path in Pass 07 requirement 4 are the only records this unverified user may create through identity synchronization. The user may reach verification, age and terms completion, recovery, support, deletion, public browsing, and sign-out without gaining another protected activity.

7. <a id="p07-r07"></a>**P07.R07 — Verified email for hosting**<br>A currently Firebase-verified email is required before a user can host a game, join a game, create a booking, pay, use a Need a Sub interaction, send a private message, receive an elevated privilege, or use an administrative function. A local verification timestamp must not authorize an operation after Firebase reports the identifier unverified.

   Need a Sub participation mutations include creating or publishing a post, applying or responding, accepting or assigning a participant, and related private messages. Public reads, support, and deletion remain available without a currently verified email when their other authorization rules permit them. Before implementing a withdrawal exception, the child plan must list each existing withdrawal operation permitted without a currently verified email, the authenticated identity and current state it requires, its allowed database and external effects, and its response. Only those listed withdrawal operations remain available under this exception; they must not create new participation, bookings, payments, messages, or administrative actions. The child plan must map each route and mutation to this gate.

8. <a id="p07-r08"></a>**P07.R08 — Backend verified-email enforcement**<br>The verified-email gate must be enforced on the backend for every operation listed above. Hiding a frontend control does not satisfy the gate, and every administrator must use a currently verified identifier.

   For each protected operation in Pass 07 requirement 7, the backend must verify the Firebase ID token and read the current Firebase user record for that UID before the effect; neither token email-verification claims nor PostgreSQL snapshots alone establish current verification. A Firebase read failure must leave the operation unchanged with the recorded non-mutating unavailable result. Choose the read latency and quota budget under Section 3.3.

9. <a id="p07-r09"></a>**P07.R09 — Firebase browser authentication persistence**<br>The browser may persist Firebase player authentication through the Firebase SDK's supported persistence. The identity/application-security responsibility must choose, under Section 3.3, inactivity and absolute authentication-age limits for player and administrator sessions, identify the activity that resets the inactivity clock, and record the different risk that justifies each limit, with the administrator absolute authentication-age limit shorter than the player limit.

   The browser must sign out through the SDK and clear private state when its inactivity limit is reached, including after a restored tab or device wake; a silent Firebase ID-token refresh must not reset either limit.

   The backend must reject a protected operation when the verified Firebase authentication time exceeds the applicable absolute limit, even if the SDK refreshed the ID token, until Firebase reauthentication establishes a new authentication time.

   Logout and user switching must sign out or replace the prior Firebase SDK session and clear application state, cached private data, and any application-managed credentials belonging to the prior user before the next account is shown. Restoring the browser session must not restore access to the prior user's private data after logout or a switch.

   Local logout and inactivity sign-out terminate that browser session but do not claim to revoke an already copied ID token. For initial launch, Pickup Lane accepts browser-enforced inactivity without separate server-side inactivity tracking: an independently copied, otherwise-valid Firebase ID token may remain usable after inactivity sign-out until its expiry, the backend authentication-age limit, or Firebase revocation first makes it invalid.

   When account-wide termination is required after suspected credential compromise, Firebase refresh tokens must be revoked and Pass 07 requirement 1 must reject the revoked identity on the next protected request.

10. <a id="p07-r10"></a>**P07.R10 — Bounded identity read retries**<br>An identity read may retry only within the attempt count and total time selected under Section 3.3 and Pass 06 requirement 15. An identity mutation may retry only when an idempotency rule prevents a second effect or Pass 07 requirement 13 first establishes the original result. A timeout must not cause an unlimited or blind mutation replay.

11. <a id="p07-r11"></a>**P07.R11 — Immediate access revocation**<br>Account-disabled, role-changed, and access-revoked states must take effect on the next protected backend operation even if the browser holds an older token.

12. <a id="p07-r12"></a>**P07.R12 — Enumeration-safe account responses**<br>Pickup Lane's status code, response body, and user-visible message for password reset, email verification, and account recovery must not differ based on whether an email address belongs to a Pickup Lane account. Behavior displayed on a Firebase-hosted page that Pickup Lane cannot configure is outside this requirement and must be recorded as a Firebase limitation rather than copied into a Pickup Lane response.

    **Direct Firebase sign-in limits.** Production Firebase Authentication must enable email-enumeration protection and configure a provider-supported direct sign-in attempt limit or tighten the relevant Identity Toolkit API quota from its default for Firebase email/password sign-in; a Pickup Lane API limit cannot protect requests sent directly to Firebase.

    **Password policy.** For Firebase email/password registration and password changes, the production provider policy must require at least 15 characters, permit at least 64 characters without silent truncation, and avoid mandatory character-class composition rules. Pickup Lane must not force periodic password rotation without compromise evidence or another documented security event. No application account may be activated through a default, shared, or hard-coded authentication credential.

    **Compromised-password capability.** The identity/application-security responsibility must inspect whether the selected Firebase plan can reject common or known-compromised passwords at the provider boundary; it must enable a supported provider control if available and otherwise record that screening is unavailable and the resulting credential-stuffing risk, without adding password storage or a separate password database.

    **Credential-stuffing protection and telemetry.** The sign-in interface must not reveal whether a failed email/password attempt named an existing account. The identity/application-security responsibility must record the Firebase setting and its documented scope, the legitimate sign-in peak and rejection behavior used to choose any numeric quota under Section 3.3, and the detection signal and response for repeated failures or suspected credential stuffing. Direct attempts must not remain unbounded, ordinary sign-in at the recorded legitimate peak must remain possible, and passwords or authentication secrets must not enter Pickup Lane telemetry.

13. <a id="p07-r13"></a>**P07.R13 — Unknown identity-mutation outcomes**<br>When a timeout or lost response leaves the result of a Firebase identity mutation unknown, Pickup Lane must not repeat the mutation until it rereads the Firebase identity and the linked PostgreSQL account and determines whether the requested change occurred. A matching result must be recorded as the original operation's result. A conflicting or still-unknown result must leave account ownership unchanged and create the operational and audit records for investigation. Unknown Firebase account-deletion results follow Pass 16 requirement 5 instead.

14. <a id="p07-r14"></a>**P07.R14 — Initial acceptance and server evidence**<br>Before an application account becomes active, the user must affirmatively accept the current legally approved, versioned Terms of Service required by Pass 02 requirement 6. The backend must record the authenticated account, exact document version, and server-assigned acceptance time linked to the text presented; a notice that account creation implies agreement, a checked browser control alone, or a client-supplied acceptance claim must not establish acceptance.

    **Activity-specific terms and acceptance.** The qualified legal review in Pass 02 requirement 6 must identify any additional participation-specific terms or acknowledgment required before hosting, joining, booking, or paying for each game type and the affected operations; the backend must prevent each such operation until the user's applicable acceptance is recorded. One acceptance may cover multiple operations when the reviewed terms and version do so; this requirement does not require a separate waiver or consent flow for each operation.

    **Material and nonmaterial revisions.** For each published revision, the legal review must determine whether its changes require renewed acceptance and name the affected activities. A revision that materially changes participation risks, liability allocation, or an activity's contractual obligations must require affirmative acceptance of that version before the next affected activity; typographical or other changes that do not alter obligations need not trigger renewal when the review records why.

    **Blocked activity and permitted access.** An unaccepted or unavailable required version must block account activation or the affected activity before a game, booking, payment, or other protected effect; users must still be able to view the terms and use permitted account recovery, support, and deletion paths.

    **Retained text and production publication.** Acceptance evidence and the corresponding document text must remain available for the retention treatment assigned under Pass 16; a later edit must not silently change what an earlier acceptance means. Development and staging implementation may use the legally approved version before production publication; Pass 17 requirement 22 owns its release-build presentation, and Pass 21 requirement 4 must verify that the current approved version is published and enforced at the production address before final acceptance.

15. <a id="p07-r15"></a>**P07.R15 — State-changing request safeguards**<br>Every request that changes stored or external state must require an authenticated credential that an unrelated website cannot create or read. If the browser automatically attaches that credential to a request initiated by another website, the backend must also reject the request unless it contains a valid cross-site-request-forgery proof tied to the intended Pickup Lane site and the authenticated session.

#### Integration boundaries

- Pass 08 requirements 1–10; Pass 09 requirement 16; Pass 10 requirement 7 own role, resource, and operation authorization after this pass establishes identity.
- Pass 15 requirement 6; Pass 16 requirements 1–5 own execution and recovery of user-data deletion, including the treatment required by Pass 07 requirement 5; Pass 07 requirement 13 here owns other unknown Firebase identity-mutation results.
- Pass 07 requirement 14 consumes the approved version and activity-specific legal decision from Pass 02 requirement 6; it does not require Pass 21 production publication for development or staging implementation. Pass 17 requirement 22 owns release-build presentation, Pass 21 requirement 4 owns production publication, and Pass 16 owns the retention and deletion treatment of acceptance evidence.
- Pass 07 requirements 1 and 2, 10; Pass 10 requirements 2, 4 and 5; Pass 17 requirement 21 consume Pass 04 requirements 2–4; Pass 06 requirements 2, 5 and 6, 15; Pass 07 requirement 15; Pass 18 requirement 18. Pass 10 requirement 1 also consumes the identity/application-security assignment from Pass 01 requirement 2; that assignment may be recorded before the remaining Pass 01 work and does not authorize production access.
- Passes 08, 09, 12, 13, 16, and 17 must name the specific identity requirement they consume rather than depend on completion of this entire pass.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p07-v01"></a>**P07.V01 — Browser authentication transport**<br>For Pass 06 requirements 3 and 4; Pass 07 requirement 15, test each supported way a browser sends authentication. When the browser attaches the credential automatically, send a state-changing request from an unrelated site with the cross-site-request-forgery proof missing and invalid. When the client must add a bearer credential explicitly, confirm that an unrelated site cannot obtain or supply that credential. Separately omit or invalidate authentication and add an unrecognized security-sensitive field. Repeat each singular security-sensitive query, JSON, form, and request-header field supported by the route; submit the same field through two conflicting input sources, alternate percent encodings, and malformed encodings. Record the parsed value or rejection at the request boundary and confirm that no authorization or business effect uses a different interpretation or follows a rejected request. Include double-encoded and mixed-case encoded values and compare the final proxy, framework, and business-layer interpretation; confirm decoding is completed before validation exactly once and no second decode bypasses a denial. For each source model, populate every internal, Firebase-only, Stripe-only, Cloudflare-R2-only, administrator-only, and other-user field that model can contain, then request each actual public, authenticated-user, and administrator response that can represent it. Repeat each protected request as the wrong user and wrong role. Record the exact rejection or permitted response and confirm that no protected state changes and that every field prohibited for that response remains absent.

2. <a id="p07-v02"></a>**P07.V02 — Firebase token rejection cases**<br>For Pass 07 requirements 1 and 2, send a valid Firebase ID token and separately send expired, revoked, wrong-project, wrong-audience, malformed, unsigned, and incorrectly signed tokens through the production release build connected to the Firebase test project. Send a valid token in the authorization header, URL, form body, log input, and telemetry input; separately inspect Firebase SDK-managed persistence, application code, and application-managed browser storage for a separate token copy. Inspect the production Firebase authorized domains, Google provider and OAuth redirect configuration, and the supported SDK flow. In isolation, complete a valid Google sign-in and attempt an unlisted origin, altered callback or return destination, and provider result unrelated to the initiating browser transaction; record the resulting Firebase user, browser destination, and Pickup Lane account. Confirm that only the valid SDK flow establishes the intended user, and every altered or unrelated result fails without an untrusted redirect or account change. Record the status, established identity, and storage owner for each case. Confirm that only the valid header token establishes Pickup Lane API identity, SDK-managed persistence remains usable, application code makes no separate browser-storage copy, and no token appears in a URL, form, response, log, or telemetry record.

3. <a id="p07-v03"></a>**P07.V03 — Verified-email operation gates**<br>For Pass 07 requirements 6–8, 12, exercise every listed operation with verified and unverified email identities and run password-reset, verification, and recovery requests for registered and unregistered addresses. Record backend and user-visible results. Confirm that only the expressly allowed unverified-user operations succeed, every protected operation rejects the unverified identity before its effect, and Pickup Lane responses do not reveal account existence.

   Inspect the production Firebase email-enumeration and direct sign-in attempt or quota settings and the provider documentation for their scope.
