In the staging Firebase project with the same settings and synthetic accounts, exercise registered and unregistered email/password failures, repeated attempts to the recorded control boundary, and legitimate sign-in at the recorded peak without exhausting production capacity.

   Inspect the selected Firebase plan's documented password-policy and common or compromised-password screening capabilities and the production policy. In the staging Firebase project with matching policy, attempt a 14-character password, a 15-character password, a 64-character passphrase without mixed character classes, and a provider-recognized weak or compromised password if screening is supported; record acceptance, rejection, and any unsupported screening limitation without retaining the passwords. Confirm that 14 characters are rejected, 15 and 64 characters are accepted without a composition rule, and a provider-recognized weak or compromised password is rejected only when the selected plan supports that control; do not claim screening from a length check.

   Record Firebase responses, the failure signal and its destination, and whether a token or Pickup Lane account was created. Confirm that direct attempts reach the provider control, the user-visible result does not reveal account existence, legitimate sign-in remains possible at the recorded peak, and no failed attempt establishes identity, creates an application account, or copies a password or secret into telemetry.

   Verify there is no default/shared account credential or unannounced periodic forced password change in the application-controlled sign-in and account lifecycle.

   For each Pass 07 requirement 7 route, change current Firebase email verification after token issuance and before the operation. Inspect the current same-UID Firebase read, local snapshot, response, and effects; confirm a stale verified claim or snapshot cannot permit a mutation and Firebase read failure produces no effect.

4. <a id="p07-v04"></a>**P07.V04 — Identity timeout and unknown-outcome cases**<br>For Pass 07 requirements 10, 13, force an identity-read timeout, a Firebase mutation timeout before the request reaches Firebase, and a lost response after Firebase applies the mutation. Record attempt counts, elapsed time, Firebase state, PostgreSQL state, the final user-visible result, and audit or operational records. Confirm that reads stop within their limits, a mutation is not repeated until its result is determined, and no scenario duplicates or reverses an identity change.

5. <a id="p07-v05"></a>**P07.V05 — Account registration and synchronization flows**<br>For Pass 07 requirements 5 and 6, exercise email signup, Google sign-in, Firebase identity synchronization, profile completion and change, session restoration, and every direct backend or API account-creation path with a valid adult birth date, the eighteenth birthday, an age below 18, an age below 13, and a missing or invalid birth date. Include a Google or Firebase identity created before age is learned, a provisional PostgreSQL record, an existing account later found underage, and a failed or outcome-unknown deletion. Inspect the recorded COPPA child-directed determination and its basis; confirm that a child-directed or unresolved result cannot pass signoff without the qualified legal treatment decision. Record the rejection or permitted response, access to each registered-user and game operation, Firebase identity state, PostgreSQL account and related state, and the Pass 16 inventory treatment and result in every affected store. Confirm that an eligible adult can complete registration; no rejected underage or age-unknown identity obtains or retains an active account or protected access; discovery blocks an existing account on its next protected request; and collected information is deleted or remains only in the exact legally supported restricted scope for its recorded duration. For a known child under 13 in a general-audience service, confirm that further collection, use, and disclosure for registration and application features stop and that no personal information remains outside the recorded lawful treatment.

   Submit an unsupported allegation, a report meeting the recorded credibility criteria but not yet confirmed, and a validated underage birth date separately. Record the evidence, identity/privacy actor, restriction time, numeric review deadline, review result, and missed-deadline action. Exercise confirmed, disproved, and unresolved review results. Confirm that the unsupported allegation alone does not trigger the report-based restriction; a credible unconfirmed report triggers temporary restriction and review; a disproved report ends only that restriction; an unresolved report remains restricted with visible next review or escalation; and confirmed inactivity and Pass 16 treatment occur only after determination. Inspect the recorded qualified legal decision for under-13 knowledge and retention.

   Inspect the approved calendar time zone and February 29 rule. In that time zone, exercise registration and later age revalidation immediately before and after the eighteenth-birthday boundary, including a February 29 birth date reaching age 18 in a non-leap year and a time when UTC and the approved local calendar date differ. Record the server date, response, account state, and protected-access result; confirm every path applies the same approved rule and grants no early access.

**Development-stage manual verification:** For Pass 07 requirement 14, after the qualified legal approval in Pass 02 requirement 6, use dedicated development Firebase identities, the Section 3.2 development-stage build, and isolated API and PostgreSQL resources. Attempt account activation and each affected activity already implemented with affirmative acceptance of the applicable current version, no acceptance, a client-only acceptance claim, and an unavailable version. Also test a superseded version: use a later legally approved revision if one exists, or a controlled versioned test fixture to prove only the backend gate logic without treating that fixture as legal approval or production publication. Inspect the text and version presented, authenticated account, server-assigned acceptance time and retained text link, response, and game, booking, payment, and other protected effects that the exercised operation could create. Confirm that only the applicable recorded acceptance permits activation or an affected activity, a client claim alone never establishes it, rejected attempts produce no protected effect, and terms viewing, recovery, support, and deletion remain available as required. Each later activity must verify this gate before its own feature completes; Pass 20 verification 60 repeats the complete email, Google, activity, and revision matrix for the exact release candidate.

#### Completion condition

Account and identity rules work against dedicated development Firebase, with passing development-stage manual verification for backend enforcement of acceptance against the legally approved, versioned Terms content under requirement 14 and Pass 07 verification 1–5 clauses that exercise implemented identity operations. Record each clause requiring a later activity or Pass 16 store treatment and its implementation trigger as incomplete; verify it before that owning feature completes. Release-build presentation, later full application/administrator and production-equivalent scenarios remain in Passes 17 and 20; deployed production publication and its final check remain in Pass 21.

<a id="p08"></a>

### Pass 08 — Authorization and Access Control

#### Purpose

Implement backend ownership, user/administrator permission checks, protected-query filtering, denied-operation atomicity, and explicit route authorization contracts before privileged workflows.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p08-r01"></a>**P08.R01 — Non-public operation access gate**<br>Every non-public backend operation must deny access until the backend has checked the authenticated user's current PostgreSQL role, account state, ownership or relationship to the requested resource, the resource's current workflow state, and the exact permission rule stated by the parent requirement that defines the operation. All checks must occur before protected data is loaded for response or state is changed.

2. <a id="p08-r02"></a>**P08.R02 — Client-supplied protected fields**<br>A client-supplied user identifier, role, ownership field, price, capacity, or moderation state must be treated as input to validate, not as authorization evidence.

3. <a id="p08-r03"></a>**P08.R03 — Administrator access gate**<br>The current administrator gate must require a valid identity, an existing local user, a currently verified email, active and non-deleted account state, and current administrator permission on every request. A general authenticated user, host relationship, hidden frontend control, or remembered browser state must not satisfy this gate.

4. <a id="p08-r04"></a>**P08.R04 — Individual administrator accounts**<br>Each administrator must use an individually attributable account. Initial administrator creation and offboarding must preserve at least one account with a tested recovery route. Shared administrator accounts, user impersonation, and emergency application access are prohibited.

5. <a id="p08-r05"></a>**P08.R05 — List and export authorization**<br>List, search, cursor, bulk, and export operations must apply authorization and visibility filtering inside the data query. An aggregate must operate on a query that already contains those filters. Fetching unauthorized rows and hiding them only in the response is prohibited.

6. <a id="p08-r06"></a>**P08.R06 — Nested-resource authorization**<br>Object and nested-resource identifiers must not grant access. Requests for another user's private resource, or a child resource paired with the wrong parent, must return no protected content even when the identifier is known.

7. <a id="p08-r07"></a>**P08.R07 — Authorization failure atomicity**<br>Authorization failures must not partially perform the requested mutation, enqueue follow-up work, create a charge, or disclose protected object existence beyond the endpoint's defined `403` or concealed `404` behavior.

8. <a id="p08-r08"></a>**P08.R08 — Stale permission and ownership changes**<br>Permission, role, suspension, deletion, and ownership changes must be rejected or enforced on the next protected backend request; an old token or cached frontend state must not preserve authority.

9. <a id="p08-r09"></a>**P08.R09 — Mass-assignment protection**<br>Purpose-specific schemas and explicit field mappings must reject mass assignment (client-supplied fields entering protected model fields) of roles, ownership, account state, payment state, Firebase/Stripe/R2 identifiers, timestamps, and administrative fields.

10. <a id="p08-r10"></a>**P08.R10 — Protected endpoint inventory**<br>The Pass 08 child plan must list every protected endpoint family and state the response for an authenticated caller requesting an existing but inaccessible resource, a nonexistent resource, or a resource identifier paired with the wrong parent. It must choose `403` or a concealed `404` for lack of resource access and record the response for each of those three cases before the family is implemented. For a concealed `404` family, the three cases must be indistinguishable in externally observable status, response fields, and other response behavior that could reveal existence. Every route in a listed family must use the recorded responses, and neither response may contain protected fields or reveal whether a concealed resource exists.

#### Integration boundaries

- Pass 07 requirements 1–4, 6–9, 11; Pass 10 requirements 1, 6 establish the identity and identity state consumed by Pass 08 requirements 1–4, 8; Pass 09 requirement 16; Pass 10 requirement 7 here.
- Pass 01 requirement 2; Pass 03 requirement 3; Pass 10 requirement 8; Pass 19 requirements 1–3 own access to Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL hosting, application hosting, DNS, CI, monitoring, and backups; this pass owns authorization inside the Pickup Lane application.
- Pass 12 owns moderation state and actions, Pass 13 owns financial and booking state, Pass 14 owns venue-image state, and Pass 09 owns audit records. Each of those passes must apply Pass 08 requirements 1–10; Pass 09 requirement 16; Pass 10 requirement 7 here to its protected operations.
- Passes 09, 12, 13, 14, 17, and 19 must name the exact authorization requirement they consume instead of depending on completion of this entire pass.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p08-v01"></a>**P08.V01 — Protected backend authorization responses**<br>For Pass 08 requirement 8, change and revoke a permission, role, account state, and ownership relationship while the affected user has an active token and populated frontend cache. Send the next protected backend request and record the authorization result and stored state. Confirm that every change takes effect on that request and stale browser or token state preserves no authority.

2. <a id="p08-v02"></a>**P08.V02 — Authorization denial state checks**<br>For Pass 08 requirement 9, add role, ownership, account-state, payment-state, Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, timestamp, and administrator fields to player and administrator requests whose purpose does not accept them. Record validation responses and committed state. Confirm that the backend rejects the fields before assignment and does not copy any prohibited field into a model or external request.

**Development-stage manual verification:** Before this pass completes, use the Pass 03 requirement 9 mapping to run the authorization contract against every protected route implemented at this stage. For each route, exercise the allowed caller and the wrong role, account state, owner, resource state, and parent relationship applicable to it; inspect query filtering and the recorded `403` or concealed `404` contract, response fields, database changes, jobs, and external effects. Confirm an allowed result and no protected disclosure or partial effect for each denial. Record routes not yet implemented and require the same check before each later feature completes. Pass 20 verification 9 and 10 recheck the full released route set in the isolated release deployment.

#### Completion condition

All implemented protected routes have passing development-stage authorization evidence for the contract and prohibited effects. Each subsequently added feature must use and verify the same contract before its own completion; full release-wide coverage remains with Pass 20.

---

<a id="p09"></a>

### Pass 09 — Audit Logging and Monitoring Foundations

**Selected monitoring boundary:** Grafana Cloud Pro provides isolated Development/Staging/Production stacks for application/worker OpenTelemetry instrumentation (the configured source of sanitized application telemetry for Grafana) and Grafana IRM incident handling. The instrumentation alone does not prove delivery, retention, or which telemetry types are enabled. Render Pro-native log explorer is the **separately named centralized source for Render-generated platform/deployment/runtime logs** when it meets the same access, retention, correlation, and interrupted-delivery evidence requirements; it currently retains logs for 14 days on Pro. It is **not** a Grafana log stream, and independent per-service Render Pro log-stream destinations are not assumed. For each source, the architecture record must name the actual destination and its verified retention/access/absence detection; a source that cannot deliver a required log needs the exact alternate detection under Pass 09 requirement 6. No untested log forwarding or 30-day retention claim may close the logging obligation. Production notification fallback and the capable alternate operator are governed by Pass 18 and Pass 01 requirement 2.

#### Purpose

Establish request telemetry and tamper-resistant administrator audit contracts, including transactional audit obligations, logging policy, protected reads, and stable metrics before implementing sensitive administrative actions.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p09-r01"></a>**P09.R01 — Server-generated request identity**<br>Every inbound request must receive a new server-generated internal request identifier. A caller-supplied correlation identifier may be retained only after it matches the recorded length and character rules, and it must be stored as a separate field linked to the internal request identifier; it must never replace the internal identifier. Every durable job created by a request must store that internal request identifier and must always store a correlation link to its originating business record.

2. <a id="p09-r02"></a>**P09.R02 — Log event identity and context**<br>Logs must identify time, service, environment, immutable release identity, operation, outcome, error class, and correlation identifier. Every security-event timestamp must use UTC or an explicit time-zone offset, and all log-producing components must obtain their timestamps from synchronized system clocks or documented provider-managed time sources rather than trusting a browser-supplied time. They must not record passwords, tokens, secrets, full payment data, private message contents, moderation evidence, or unrestricted personal data. A failed backend TLS certificate or hostname check must produce a sanitized security-event or operational failure record that identifies the affected destination class and failed check without logging a credential or full protected endpoint string.

3. <a id="p09-r03"></a>**P09.R03 — Sensitive identifier log handling**<br>User IDs, object IDs, Firebase, Stripe, or R2 identifiers, request identifiers, URLs containing variable values, and free-form error text must not become metric-label values. Before release, the telemetry contract must list every permitted route template, operation, service, outcome, and normalized error-class (an approved stable category without raw error text or secrets) label value; emitting an unlisted value must be rejected or replaced by the recorded fallback value.

4. <a id="p09-r04"></a>**P09.R04 — Critical service metrics**<br>Metrics must cover request availability and latency, dependency failures, the numbers of open, available, and waiting PostgreSQL connections, payment and refund correctness, queue delay and terminal failure, reconciliation backlog, image-processing failure, and data freshness for scheduled work.

5. <a id="p09-r05"></a>**P09.R05 — Telemetry contract for critical flows**<br>For each flow that uses them, the telemetry contract must link the HTTP request identifier and the error response defined by Pass 06 requirement 2 to job and attempt identifiers; internal booking, payment, refund, and credit identifiers; architecture-record aliases for Stripe records; R2 image, object, and job records; administrative action or audit identifiers; and environment and release identity. The link must permit investigation without placing a prohibited external identifier or personal value in an unrestricted log.

6. <a id="p09-r06"></a>**P09.R06 — Log-source inventory**<br>The production architecture record must list every frontend, API, worker, PostgreSQL, Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, Postmark, PostHog, Render hosting/edge/deployment, AWS S3 backup, DNS/registrar, repository/CI, and Grafana monitoring/incident log source that Pickup Lane receives or can configure for delivery.

    **Required event types.** For each source, it must inventory the event types required to prove the exact logging, security, failure, deployment, and operational-detection requirements that source supports, citing those requirements.

    **Per-source and event record.** For each source–event-type pair, the record must name a centralized destination where structured records are delivered through configuration or collected natively by the provider, or the alternate signal required below when that event has no deliverable log.

    **Destination controls and evidence.** Each named log destination must have restricted access, detection of interrupted delivery or missing records, and the Pass 16 retention treatment; its records must carry environment and release context.

    **Provider-native logging.** Provider-native collection does not imply forwarding to Grafana.

    **Log-delivery permissions.** A workload's ordinary log-delivery credential, when one is used, must permit sending its records but must not permit reading, modifying, or deleting previously collected records or changing the destination's access or retention settings.

    **Alternate detection for unavailable sources.** If a named external service exposes no deliverable log for an event, the record must identify the unavailable source and the metric, webhook, audit event, or service notification used to detect that event. Local console output alone does not satisfy production logging.

    **Evidence by event type.** For every required source–event-type pair, distinguish a record observed through the named logging pipeline, an alternate signal whose delivery was exercised, and a provider limitation established by documentation and the isolated-response procedure in Section 3.3. A configured destination or provider document alone does not prove that the event was delivered or that missing delivery was detected. An unverified required event type remains incomplete.

7. <a id="p09-r07"></a>**P09.R07 — Untrusted log field encoding**<br>Untrusted log and export fields must be encoded so newline, terminal, HTML, JSON, and spreadsheet control characters cannot create a forged record or executable output.

8. <a id="p09-r08"></a>**P09.R08 — Audited privileged operations**<br>Audit records must be created for authentication-administration changes, role and privilege changes, account disabling or restoration, moderation actions, private-message and moderation-evidence reads, detailed payment reads, financial corrections, venue-image changes, retention and deletion overrides, production access changes, secret rotation, backup or restore operations, and audit-record access.

   The authentication-administration inventory includes production Firebase authentication-policy changes, administrator MFA enrollment/removal/recovery, privileged identity changes, account disable/restore, administrator-initiated token revocation, and Pickup Lane privilege changes. Routine sign-in is authentication evidence, not an administration change. Planned provider-console and recovery actions use the restricted operator procedure in Pass 09 requirement 11.

   For a planned provider-console action, the restricted operator must create an operation ID and durable audit intent in the existing PostgreSQL audit store before the provider action, then link the provider event evidence and observed outcome. An unavoidable emergency action when the audit store is unavailable must preserve independent provider and incident evidence and be reconciled into the audit record promptly; the later record must not claim that an impossible pre-action intent existed.

   The operator procedure must set a numeric maximum time under Section 3.3 for linking emergency evidence and reconciling the audit record after storage recovers; an unresolved action remains in the restricted queue until that link is established.

9. <a id="p09-r09"></a>**P09.R09 — Privileged audit coverage**<br>Required audit coverage must include privileged success; private-message, moderation-evidence, detailed-payment, and audit-data reads; authorization denial; stale-state conflict; duplicate or replay outcome; Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL, backup-storage, and application-hosting failures during an administrative action; and security or access configuration changes. Each of those outcomes must create the record required by Pass 09 requirement 10. A failed or outcome-unknown required audit write is not a successfully recorded audit event: the protected action must follow Pass 09 requirement 11, and the independent operational signal in Pass 18 requirement 8 must report the write failure because the failed audit store cannot prove that it recorded its own failure.

   Detailed-payment coverage applies whenever any actor loads fields beyond the owner-visible transaction summary defined in Pass 12 requirement 14. The child plan must inventory the routes and queries and place the audit commit before the restricted load.

10. <a id="p09-r10"></a>**P09.R10 — Audit record identity and fields**<br>Each audit record must identify an immutable record ID, authoritative time, authenticated actor and current role context, affected resource, action, result, stable failure code when unsuccessful, request/correlation ID, environment, release identity, and required domain links. A moderation or enforcement action, financial correction, retention or deletion override, sensitive reveal, privilege change, backup restore, production-access change, or secret rotation must also store its validated structured reason. The record must store the fields changed by the action rather than unrestricted before/after snapshots.

   Each event type must define an allowed audit schema with changed field names and only the safe typed prior and resulting values needed to prove that outcome. Redact or omit secrets, tokens, unrestricted personal information, and raw provider payloads. A restricted financial-correction event must retain the prior and resulting local financial state required by Pass 13 requirement 12, without copying unrelated payment or customer content. Provider-console events link the operation ID, permitted provider evidence reference, and confirmed or unresolved result.

   For a provider-console or recovery operation without an initiating API request, the operator record must identify the named provider actor and current Pass 01 authorized role, use the durable operation ID as correlation identity in place of a nonexistent HTTP request ID, record the affected environment and deployed release identity at action time, and distinguish provider-observed result from local interpretation. An emergency record must mark any unavailable pre-action field as unavailable rather than inventing it.

11. <a id="p09-r11"></a>**P09.R11 — Atomic state-change audit**<br>For a PostgreSQL state change, the required audit record must commit in the same transaction as the protected change or a durable audit obligation must commit in that transaction before success is returned. For a protected read, the audit record must commit before the backend loads or returns the protected fields. Failure or an unknown result from the required audit write must leave a local PostgreSQL state-changing action uncommitted and a protected read undisclosed. For an external mutation, the pre-dispatch audit failure blocks normal dispatch; a post-dispatch failure cannot undo a provider effect and follows the unresolved-outcome path below. Forwarding an already committed audit record to another system must use its immutable record ID so retry or duplicate delivery creates one forwarded event.

   For an external provider mutation requiring the audit coverage in Pass 09 requirement 8, persist a durable audit intent with operation ID before dispatch, perform the provider operation once, and record its confirmed outcome afterward. Failure or unknown outcome of the pre-action audit write blocks normal dispatch. A failed or unknown post-action audit write or provider result must preserve provider evidence, trigger the independent Pass 18 requirement 8 signal, and remain open for reconciliation before retry or closure. PostgreSQL cannot roll back an already completed provider effect; no audit record or response may claim that it did. The unavoidable emergency exception follows the evidence and reconciliation procedure in Pass 09 requirement 8.

   The caller or operator must receive a recorded pending or unresolved result when an external effect occurred but its provider or post-action audit outcome is unknown; it must not receive an unqualified success or a claim that the external action was undone.

12. <a id="p09-r12"></a>**P09.R12 — Immutable audit records**<br>No application or administrator role may update or delete an original audit record, regardless of which actor created it. Corrections must use the linked record required by Pass 09 requirement 13. Only the retention process may delete an audit record under Pass 16 requirement 8.

13. <a id="p09-r13"></a>**P09.R13 — Linked audit corrections**<br>A correction must create a linked record that explains the correction. It must not overwrite the original record.

14. <a id="p09-r14"></a>**P09.R14 — Restricted audit reads**<br>Audit reads must satisfy Pass 08 requirements 3, 5 and 6, 10; Pass 09 requirement 16, enforce the filters and page-size limit recorded for the endpoint, and return only the fields in Pass 09 requirement 10 that the audit-read contract lists. They must not return unrestricted metadata, request bodies, private messages, full payment details, secrets, or unrelated user fields. Each list and detail read must create its own audit record before returning protected audit data. A granular audit role is prohibited.

15. <a id="p09-r15"></a>**P09.R15 — Untrusted audit field encoding**<br>Encoding audit fields for storage or output must handle untrusted fields before persistence or emission so newline, terminal, HTML, JSON, and spreadsheet control characters cannot create a forged record or executable output.

16. <a id="p09-r16"></a>**P09.R16 — Protected-operation audit gate**<br>Before reading protected data or changing state, the backend service for each role change, account suspension, administrator-initiated account deletion or restoration, financial correction, private-message reveal, audit read, existing export, platform notice, moderation enforcement, and venue-image change must recheck the administrator gate in Pass 08 requirement 3, load the named target, and verify the action and current-state conditions stated by the requirement that defines that operation. Granular application administrator roles are prohibited.

#### Integration boundaries

- Audit records establish accountability; operational logs establish runtime diagnosis. One does not substitute for the other.
- Pass 07 requirements 1, 7 and 8, 11; Pass 10 requirement 1 supply identity and recent authentication. Pass 08 requirements 1–10; Pass 09 requirement 16; Pass 10 requirement 7 supply application authorization and field filtering. Pass 05 requirements 1–4, 8, 11 and 12; Pass 20 requirement 4 supply immutable relations, transactions, deletion treatment, and unknown-outcome handling.
- Pass 09 requirements 1 and 2, 5, 7; Pass 18 requirements 2 and 3, 6 own operational detection and routing without replacing audit content. Pass 02 requirements 2–5; Pass 04 requirement 1; Pass 15 requirement 6; Pass 16 requirements 3–7 own audit retention, archive treatment, scoped pauses or holds, and backup treatment.
- Every parent requirement that requires audit must cite the exact audit event and whether Pass 09 requirement 11 uses the same transaction, a durable obligation, or a pre-read commit. Completion of Pass 09 as a whole is not proof that a particular protected action is audited.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p09-v01"></a>**P09.V01 — Prohibited log values and encoding**<br>For Pass 09 requirements 2, 5, 7, emit controlled passwords, tokens, signed capabilities, card data, private-message text, moderation evidence, personal data, newline, terminal control, HTML, JSON delimiter, formula prefix, and free-form error inputs through every logging and export path. Confirm removal of each prohibited value, encoding that prevents every control value from creating a forged record or executable output, and use of the fixed attribute vocabulary.

2. <a id="p09-v02"></a>**P09.V02 — Stored and emitted audit records**<br>For Pass 09 requirement 15, insert newline, terminal, HTML, JSON, and spreadsheet-formula control values into every untrusted audit field. Inspect the stored and emitted audit representation. Confirm that encoding prevents every inserted value from creating a forged record or executable output.

**Development-stage manual verification:** Before a privileged feature relies on this foundation, use the Pass 03 requirement 9 mapping to exercise one representative PostgreSQL state change with its same-transaction audit, one protected read with its pre-read audit, and one simulated external mutation with durable pre-dispatch intent and post-dispatch outcome. Force each required audit write to fail or remain unknown at its decision point; inspect the protected state or fields, caller or operator result, audit and operation identities, and independent failure signal. Emit a sanitized structured log and verify its required fields and restricted content through the development logging path. These checks establish the foundation; Pass 20 verification 27 and 30–35 test full flow and source coverage and production-equivalent delivery, and Pass 21 checks deployed sources.

#### Completion condition

The transactional audit and structured logging foundation has passing development-stage evidence and is usable by subsequent privileged features. Final cross-product audit coverage and production-equivalent log delivery are tested in Pass 20; delivery and retention for deployed production sources are checked in Pass 21.

<a id="p10"></a>

### Pass 10 — Advanced Identity and Administrator Security

#### Purpose

Implement recent-authentication requirements, administrator MFA, App Check enforcement contract, Firebase account-conflict auditing, safe privileged mutations, and individual administrator authentication. Final staging/production settings remain release checks.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p10-r01"></a>**P10.R01 — Recent authentication for sensitive changes**<br>Changing a Firebase-sensitive identity factor, deleting an account, restoring administrative access, or changing an elevated privilege must require recent authentication or another Firebase step-up method. Before implementation begins, the identity/application-security responsibility assigned by Pass 01 requirement 2 must record the maximum authentication age under Section 3.3, the accepted Firebase factor, the recovery method, and the rejection returned when Firebase cannot confirm recency. Each trigger must reject stale or unverifiable authentication before changing Firebase or Pickup Lane state. An email/password user must be able to initiate the Firebase-supported password-change or reset flow; an authenticated password change must require provider-recognized recent reauthentication with the current password or an equivalent enrolled stronger factor, while an account-recovery reset must use the provider's expiring recovery proof without bypassing an enabled administrator factor.

2. <a id="p10-r02"></a>**P10.R02 — App Check browser-route classification**<br>App Check, which lets Firebase attest that a request came from the configured Pickup Lane browser application, must remain an additional request check rather than a replacement for user authentication, authorization, request-frequency controls, idempotency, or replay protection. Rollout must use the separate Firebase staging application, begin in the `observe` mode defined by Pass 10 requirement 4, record results separately for every protected route group and supported browser class, and verify rollback to `observe` or `disabled`. Before enforcement begins, every protected route group must receive at least one otherwise-valid observe-mode request from every supported browser class during the observation interval recorded under Section 3.3. A route-group and browser-class combination with no observed request blocks enforcement. Enforcement also remains blocked if any otherwise-valid observed request produces missing or invalid proof or rollback has not been verified.

   The rollout inventory must classify every supported browser-called Pickup Lane API route, including public reads by default, in one versioned route classification shared by the React caller and FastAPI verifier. A route may be exempted only by a recorded route-specific review of its caller and risk. Stripe webhooks, non-browser callbacks, and direct signed R2 requests remain outside the browser API classification. A missing or disagreeing route classification blocks enforcement rather than silently excluding the route.

3. <a id="p10-r03"></a>**P10.R03 — Firebase and PostgreSQL identity conflicts**<br>When a Firebase identity conflicts with an existing PostgreSQL account link, synchronization must stop without changing account ownership, role, account state, or Firebase linkage and must create the audit record required by Pass 09 requirement 8.

4. <a id="p10-r04"></a>**P10.R04 — App Check operating modes**<br>App Check configuration must support only `disabled`, `observe`, and `enforced` modes. Staging and production must set the mode explicitly; `observe` records counts by classified route and verification outcome without denial and without user or token identifiers; `enforced` rejects missing or invalid proof on classified non-exempt browser API routes with a `403` and Firebase-verification unavailability with a `503` before route side effects. Both errors must use the response fields and omit the protected information required by Pass 06 requirement 2.

   The `observe` and `enforced` results must use the same versioned browser-route classification from Pass 10 requirement 2. A newly added browser route must enter the inventory and complete the observation and rollback gates before enforcement applies to its group.

   A browser CORS preflight is checked under Pass 06 requirement 10 without App Check proof; it is not an App Check exemption for the requested route. After an allowed preflight, `enforced` mode must verify proof on the subsequent classified browser API request before route side effects. An invalid preflight must not authorize that request, and a successful preflight must not authorize a missing or invalid App Check proof.

5. <a id="p10-r05"></a>**P10.R05 — Firebase App Check verification**<br>App Check verification must use Firebase Admin verification, require the verified app ID to match the configured Pickup Lane web app, and accept the token only from the dedicated header on classified Pickup Lane browser API routes. It must not accept a query/body token or client-supplied app ID, manually trust decoded claims, cover Stripe webhooks, non-browser callbacks, or direct signed R2 uploads, or log raw tokens or app IDs.

   The dedicated proof header belongs to the actual classified browser API request, not to its CORS preflight. The preflight may name that header among its requested headers only when Pass 06 requirement 10 allows it; naming the header is not proof. A route-specific App Check exemption remains subject to the review in Pass 10 requirement 2.

   A missing or mismatched browser-route classification must block rollout to `enforced`; if discovered after rollout, the affected route must fail closed before side effects until the contract is corrected. It must never be silently treated as exempt; a classified exemption must cite its recorded route-specific review.

6. <a id="p10-r06"></a>**P10.R06 — Production TOTP enforcement and provider capability**<br>Every application administrator must have multifactor authentication enforced before production access is approved. The selected method is authenticator-app time-based one-time password (TOTP) through Firebase Authentication with Identity Platform; the actual production project must have the required upgrade and TOTP capability enabled, with email verification and the completed second factor enforced for privileged sign-in rather than enrollment alone. The production pricing and quotas of the upgrade must be included in the Pass 20 recurring-cost and capacity evidence, and a cost or provider-capability conflict must be resolved without silently downgrading administrator MFA.

    **Assisted factor replacement and prohibited shortcuts.** The decision required by Pass 10 requirement 1 must name the accepted factor and recovery method. Administrator factor replacement or recovery must verify ownership at least as strongly as the original enrollment and must not permit email access alone, possession of an unverified telephone number, or an administrator-only database change to bypass the enrolled factor. For initial launch, telephone/SMS OTP is not an administrator factor-recovery path. The assigned identity/security authority must document a high-assurance assisted Firebase factor-replacement procedure, including evidence stronger than access to the enrollment email, the authorized operator, audit of each step, and completion of TOTP re-enrollment before administrator privilege resumes.

    **Alternate route, deadline, and signoff.** The actual Firebase plan, replacement capability, qualified operator and alternate administrator recovery route proposed under Pass 01 requirement 2 must be demonstrated before signoff, including that the route works when the primary administrator or their credentials are unavailable and the operator can complete the application-specific recovery within the numeric response or recovery deadline. An unverified procedure, login reset alone, or an unavailable operator cannot satisfy this requirement. If the selected Firebase or Identity Platform plan cannot enforce that decision, production signoff is blocked.

7. <a id="p10-r07"></a>**P10.R07 — Sensitive administrative change safeguards**<br>Privilege grants and removals, account disabling or restoration, ownership changes, and financial corrections must require validation of current state, recent authentication satisfying Pass 10 requirement 1, explicit confirmation of the named target and change, an idempotency key when the request can be replayed, and the audit record required by Pass 09 requirement 8. Failure of any safeguard must leave the protected state unchanged.

8. <a id="p10-r08"></a>**P10.R08 — Named production access with MFA**<br>Human production access must use named accounts with multifactor authentication. Shared credentials and shared administrator accounts are prohibited.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p10-v01"></a>**P10.V01 — Concurrent identity linking and synchronization**<br>For Pass 07 requirements 3 and 4; Pass 10 requirement 3, race concurrent first-login requests for one Firebase identity, repeat synchronization for that identity, and present a different Firebase identity with the same email address or a conflicting PostgreSQL link. Inspect the committed account rows and audit records. Confirm that one stable account link exists, permitted Firebase facts may synchronize, and no conflict transfers ownership, role, account state, or linkage.

   With an unverified same-UID identity, create and repair the provisional context twice, then attempt roles, membership, booking, payment, and active privilege creation through synchronization. Change the verified Firebase email and display verification state, then change PostgreSQL-owned profile and authority fields. Confirm only allowed same-UID snapshots change and no protected relationship or application-owned field is overwritten.

2. <a id="p10-v02"></a>**P10.V02 — Recent-authentication and recovery cases**<br>For Pass 10 requirement 1, exercise every listed recent-authentication trigger with authentication inside the recorded age, outside that age, and unverifiable because Firebase is unavailable. Record the factor used, backend response, Firebase result, and PostgreSQL result. Confirm that only accepted recent authentication permits the protected change and that recovery follows the recorded method. Exercise the Firebase-supported signed-in password-change and forgotten-password reset flows for an email/password test user, including stale reauthentication, an expired or reused recovery proof, and a recovery attempt after administrator MFA enrollment; confirm that the change or reset is accessible to the legitimate user but no stale, reused, or lower-assurance proof changes the credential or bypasses enrolled MFA.

3. <a id="p10-v03"></a>**P10.V03 — Administrator account and privilege cases**<br>For Pass 08 requirement 4, exercise initial administrator creation, attempted shared-account use, attempted impersonation, attempted emergency application access, offboarding of an ordinary administrator, and attempted removal of the last recoverable administrator. Record that each administrator remains attributable, each prohibited access path is rejected, and offboarding cannot remove the last tested recovery route.

4. <a id="p10-v04"></a>**P10.V04 — Sensitive change safeguard cases**<br>For Pass 10 requirement 7, exercise every listed sensitive change with fresh and stale authentication, correct and changed confirmation data, exact replay and changed-input idempotency keys, valid and stale current state, and a forced audit-write failure. Record the response and protected state. Confirm that only the fully safeguarded request commits once and every failed safeguard leaves state unchanged.

#### Completion condition

Advanced administrator and identity controls are implemented and tested in development. Production MFA configuration, the demonstrated qualified alternate recovery route and its deadline, full route coverage and staging App Check observation/enforcement remain incomplete release gates until verified under Pass 20; their absence cannot be treated as completed MFA recovery.

---

<a id="p11"></a>

### Pass 11 — Durable Background Jobs and Scheduling

#### Purpose

Implement durable jobs, leases, idempotent handlers, retry classifications, scheduler behavior, cancellation, compatible job versions, and operational visibility before business-specific asynchronous execution.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p11-r01"></a>**P11.R01 — Durable obligations for state changes**<br>When a payment, refund, booking, notification, deletion, image cleanup, reconciliation, or another cited parent requirement must continue after its initiating request could end, the obligation (work recorded in PostgreSQL so it survives the initiating request or process failure) must be committed to durable PostgreSQL state before that request reports that the obligation was accepted. Failure to persist the obligation must leave the caller-visible result and related business state in the failure or unresolved state defined by the cited parent requirement.

2. <a id="p11-r02"></a>**P11.R02 — Persisted job identity and fields**<br>Each persisted job must contain a stable identity, job type and version, a reference to its payload in the authoritative restricted store, status, creation and availability times, attempt identity and count, lease (time-limited permission for one worker attempt) and claim state, idempotency and correlation references, normalized failure data (approved stable error categories without raw messages or secrets), and terminal outcome. The general queue record must not copy sensitive payload data from that store.

3. <a id="p11-r03"></a>**P11.R03 — Atomic job claim**<br>A worker must claim work through one atomic PostgreSQL change that records its attempt identity and lease deadline. Another worker must not claim the same job until the recorded lease has expired or the first worker has completed or explicitly released it. Claiming after expiry must use a new attempt identity and must not assume that an external effect from the expired attempt failed.

   Before dispatching an external call, persist the attempt identity and external-call intent. A worker that loses the lease must stop further external effects and must not commit a result as the current attempt. The successor must inspect the prior attempt and provider evidence; an external call begun without a known final result remains unknown and must be reconciled before repetition, even when a new worker owns the lease.

4. <a id="p11-r04"></a>**P11.R04 — Idempotent job effects**<br>Job handlers must be idempotent at the business-effect boundary. Retrying after a crash between an external effect and local acknowledgement must not duplicate a charge, refund, message, deletion, booking, or file operation. For Postmark transactional email, keep a durable per-recipient operation identity, one attempt record (including the request/response uncertainty), the Postmark message identifier when known, accepted/submitted state, and observed delivery, bounce, suppression, or unresolved result. **Postmark does not currently support API idempotency keys.** Therefore a lost response after a possibly accepted send must never be assumed duplicate-safe for automatic retry; local idempotency prevents duplicate queued intent but cannot turn an ambiguous remote send into guaranteed exactly-once delivery. Provider API acceptance is not proof of recipient delivery. Use supported provider event or status evidence when available, with authenticating and deduplicating any webhook; mark failures and deadlines for the existing restricted operational queue (the access-controlled list of unresolved items and authorized recovery actions, distinct from queued worker jobs).

   A current lease is required before each external-call dispatch and before local completion. An expired attempt cannot dispatch a new provider call or mark its former result current; an already-dispatched call remains an unknown effect until its persisted intent and provider evidence establish the outcome for the successor.

5. <a id="p11-r05"></a>**P11.R05 — Job failure and retry classification**<br>Retryable failures, terminal failures, repeated failures caused by the same job input, and unsupported job versions must be classified by explicit error conditions. Each job type must define under Section 3.3 its maximum attempts, minimum and maximum delay between attempts, method for preventing many failed jobs from retrying at the same instant, handling of a `Retry-After` value returned by the named external service, processing deadline, and terminal result (the end of automatic processing, which may still require recovery).

   A timeout after a Stripe, Firebase, or Cloudflare R2 mutation with an unknown outcome must enter that service's reconciliation path rather than repeat the mutation.

   For a Postmark email send whose HTTP result is lost or otherwise ambiguous, inspect available Postmark message/event records and the local attempt evidence before any new send. Because the provider offers no API idempotency-key deduplication, **do not automatically repeat an uncertain send** even soon after the timeout; when acceptance cannot be disproved, preserve an unresolved delivery obligation for a recorded operator/recovery decision rather than silently drop the notice or risk an uncontrolled duplicate.

   Retry only when a supported response/evidence establishes that no message was accepted, and retain the original local operation identity.

   A retry after lease expiry must first apply the prior-attempt outcome check from Pass 11 requirement 3. An unknown prior provider effect, including Postmark acceptance, cannot be converted into a safe retry merely by obtaining a new claim.

6. <a id="p11-r06"></a>**P11.R06 — Failed and overdue job visibility**<br>A terminally failed, repeatedly failing, unsupported-version, or deadline-exceeded job must remain in a restricted operational queue with its job type and version, affected business-record reference, attempt count, last normalized error class, terminal reason, and the exact recovery actions permitted for that job type. Repair or replay must use the original obligation and business idempotency identity; it must not create an unrelated replacement job that can repeat the effect.

7. <a id="p11-r07"></a>**P11.R07 — Safe job interruption points**<br>Each job type must list its safe interruption points: steps after which the durable record proves either that no external effect began or that reconciliation can establish the external outcome before a retry. On shutdown, a worker must stop new claims, let an active attempt reach a listed point within the graceful-shutdown deadline chosen under Section 3.3, and then complete, release, or leave the claim to expire. Forced shutdown at any other step must still leave enough recorded state to prevent blind repetition.

8. <a id="p11-r08"></a>**P11.R08 — Scheduler and durable occurrence**<br>A scheduled task must run through a separately deployed scheduler or the selected hosting service's scheduled-work feature; it must not depend on one web process remaining alive. Each schedule occurrence must have a stable identity derived from the task and scheduled occurrence time, and enqueuing it must be idempotent so multiple scheduler instances or restarts create one business obligation.

    **Due occurrences and failure detection.** Each required recurring obligation, including reconciliation, retention cleanup, and backup generation when its schedule is owned by this mechanism, must have an expected due occurrence and numeric completion/deadline boundary under Section 3.3.

    **Independent detection, alert, and safe backfill.** A missing, delayed, cancelled, or failed invocation must be detected from durable occurrence or outcome state by an independent subsequent scheduled check or existing operational signal, must reach Pass 18 before its business deadline, and must be backfilled through the original occurrence identity when safe.

    **Backup monitoring independence.** Backup monitoring in Pass 15 must remain effective even when this scheduler is unavailable; a cron trigger alone is not completion evidence.

   **Grafana outcome monitoring and alert boundaries.** The existing Grafana monitoring path must evaluate age or absence of the last successful critical-schedule outcome independently of the Render Cron invocation that creates it. No-data and monitoring-query errors must fail visibly and alert through Pass 18 within the business deadline; backup monitoring retains its independent Pass 15 path. The child plan must name the success signal, monitor evaluation interval, and alert deadline under Section 3.3.

9. <a id="p11-r09"></a>**P11.R09 — Queue health measurements**<br>Queue growth, oldest eligible job age, throughput, attempt rate, terminally failed job count, expired claim count, worker availability and version, and processing duration must be measured separately for each job type and supplied to the Pass 18 alert and dashboard requirements.

10. <a id="p11-r10"></a>**P11.R10 — Queue cleanup retention**<br>Queue cleanup must retain the job identity and type, affected business-record reference, terminal outcome, attempt count, last normalized error class, idempotency and correlation references, and completion time for the retention period assigned under Pass 16. Cleanup must not remove state still required to investigate a duplicate effect.

11. <a id="p11-r11"></a>**P11.R11 — Bounded job lease renewal**<br>A job type may renew its lease only when measured execution can exceed the initial lease period and the handler can prove that the renewing attempt still owns the claim. Renewal must update the current claim rather than create permanent history for every heartbeat. Claim ordering must use the defined availability and priority fields; this pass does not authorize a separate fairness scheduler.

12. <a id="p11-r12"></a>**P11.R12 — Adjacent worker and scheduler release compatibility**<br>Before adjacent worker or scheduler releases overlap, the deployment contract must list the job versions each release can enqueue and execute. Every version that can remain pending during the overlap must have at least one deployed compatible handler. An unsupported job version must remain visible and unchanged; it must not be discarded, treated as another version, or executed under a different payload contract.

#### Integration boundaries

- This pass defines durable execution, not a general workflow product or fairness scheduler.
- Each business pass owns the obligation, authoritative external result, permitted recovery action, and user-visible outcome. Pass 11 owns durable persistence, claim and lease behavior, retry timing, worker shutdown, schedule identity, version compatibility, and terminal visibility.
- Pass 05 requirements 1–4, 11 and 12; Pass 20 requirement 4 supply the database constraint, transaction, serialization, and unknown-outcome controls used by jobs. Pass 05 requirements 13–16, 22–26 supply configurable connections, sessions, query contracts, process behavior, and migration compatibility; Pass 19 requirements 5 and 9–12 supply their final production values, measurements, and grants. Pass 09 requirements 1–4; Pass 18 requirements 1–4 consume the measurements and terminal states from Pass 11 requirements 6, 9 here.
- Pass 12 consumes durable notice delivery; Pass 13 consumes durable webhook, compensation, refund, and reconciliation work; Pass 14 consumes durable image processing and cleanup; Pass 16 consumes durable deletion; and Passes 15 and 20 consume worker deployment, shutdown, and recovery behavior. Each child plan must cite the exact business requirement that creates a job and the exact Pass 11 requirement that keeps it durable.
- Numeric attempt, retry, lease, deadline, shutdown, and retention settings must not be selected until their Section 3.3 inputs exist. A provisional worker design cannot complete a requirement whose implementation or verification would change when the production deployment, external-service behavior, or operating volume is chosen.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p11-v01"></a>**P11.V01 — Durable persistence and worker interruption**<br>For Pass 11 requirements 1 and 2, 4, end the initiating request before and after durable persistence, then stop a worker before an external effect, after the effect but before local acknowledgement, and after local completion. Inspect the business record, job identity and version, the payload record identifier from the restricted authoritative store, attempt and correlation data, external state, and terminal result. Confirm that the queue record contains the identifier but none of the protected payload, an accepted obligation survives the request, a failed persistence is not reported as accepted, recovery completes one business effect, and sensitive payload content remains only in its restricted authoritative store.

2. <a id="p11-v02"></a>**P11.V02 — Concurrent worker job claims**<br>For Pass 11 requirements 3 and 4, 11, run concurrent workers against the same job, let one claim expire during work, attempt renewal from the owning and a different attempt, and claim again after expiry. Exercise one job whose external result is known and one whose result is unknown. Record claim identities, lease changes, database locks, external calls, and final business state. Confirm one valid outcome, no concurrent active claim, no renewal by a non-owner, and reconciliation before repetition of an unknown effect.

   Pause a provider call after intent is persisted, expire its lease, let a successor claim, and release the former worker. Inspect both workers and provider calls; confirm the former performs no further effect or current-state commit and the successor reconciles an unknown effect before any repeat.

3. <a id="p11-v03"></a>**P11.V03 — Job retry and terminal-state cases**<br>For Pass 11 requirements 5 and 6, 9, produce every job type's retryable, terminal, repeated-input, unsupported-version, and deadline-exceeded error. Exercise the first and final permitted attempt, delay bounds, simultaneous failures, and a valid and invalid `Retry-After` response. Record attempts, scheduled times, operational-queue fields, metrics, alerts, and each permitted recovery. Confirm that retry timing follows the recorded rule, terminal work remains visible, and repair preserves the original obligation and idempotency identity. For a Postmark notification, exercise API acknowledgement without delivered event, delayed delivery, bounce or suppression, lost response after possible provider acceptance, and replayed or unauthorized delivery webhook. Attempt a repeated send both before and after an ambiguous response and demonstrate that no automatic duplicate occurs because Postmark offers no idempotency-key deduplication. Inspect the original local operation, attempts, provider message identity when known, authenticated event/state evidence, recipient outcome, operational queue, and documented operator recovery decision. Confirm that accepted and delivered are distinct, bad outcomes remain visible, and unknown sends are **never blindly repeated** or falsely reported as delivered.

4. <a id="p11-v04"></a>**P11.V04 — Safe worker shutdown**<br>For Pass 11 requirement 7, request graceful shutdown before a claim, at every listed safe interruption point, during an external call, and after an external effect with an unknown result; also force the process to stop before the graceful deadline. Record new-claim behavior, claim state, durable progress, resource release, later recovery, and external effects. Confirm that shutdown stops new claims and leaves every unfinished obligation recoverable without blind repetition.

5. <a id="p11-v05"></a>**P11.V05 — Queue cleanup cutoff**<br>For Pass 11 requirement 10, run cleanup before, at, and after the assigned retention cutoff while a duplicate-effect investigation is open and after it closes. Inspect the retained fields and removed payload data. Confirm that cleanup keeps every listed investigation field for the required period and does not remove state still needed to determine whether an effect was duplicated.

**Development-stage manual verification:** Before this pass completes, use the Pass 03 requirement 9 mapping to create a due schedule occurrence in isolated development, restart the web process and scheduler, and submit the same occurrence from two scheduler instances. Suppress an invocation and use the recorded independent development signal or subsequent check to detect the missing occurrence and backfill through its original identity. Inspect due and completion state, enqueued job identities, alert or unresolved result, and business effects; confirm one durable obligation per occurrence and no dependence on a running web process. Pass 20 verification 21 later exercises the selected Render Cron, Grafana outcome monitoring, no-data and query-error alerts, and adjacent worker/scheduler versions.

#### Completion condition

Durable queue and scheduler have passing development-stage evidence with explicit job contracts; final Cron-independent alerts and worker-overlap evidence are tested after staging monitoring exists.

---

<a id="p12"></a>

### Pass 12 — Moderation and Administrative Workflows

#### Purpose

Implement versioned moderation findings, review cases, sensitive reads, enforcement, notices, and administrator UI safeguards using the prior access, audit, and job foundations.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p12-r01"></a>**P12.R01 — Versioned moderation-rule catalog**<br>Saved-content and chat scanners must use one versioned moderation-rule catalog. Before scanner implementation begins, the Pass 01 production-readiness program responsibility must record the moderation-policy decision maker and that decision maker must approve the initial catalog.

   **Approved coverage.** For each covered content context, the approved catalog must identify the source fields and languages covered, each rule's matching and nonmatching conditions, positive and negative synthetic examples, and the expected finding or no-finding result for each example.

   **Rule identity and changes.** Every rule must identify its stable rule name, version, match behavior, scanner profile (the named, versioned settings for that content context, including evidence limits), content context, source-field purpose, supported languages, and evidence type. A change to match logic, supported context, language coverage, evidence production, or normalization must create a new rule or profile version rather than silently changing the meaning of an existing rule identifier. Machine-learning moderation is prohibited.

2. <a id="p12-r02"></a>**P12.R02 — Moderation finding provenance**<br>Every persisted finding or chat detection must identify the target and source field, exact source-content hash, scanner and rule-set versions, matched rule versions, configuration hash, text-normalization version, evidence-record format version, and UTC scan time. Execution duration belongs in operational telemetry and must not be recorded among those unchangeable facts about the finding and its scan.

3. <a id="p12-r03"></a>**P12.R03 — Moderation evidence size limits**<br>Evidence must be limited by the item-count and text-length limits stored in the finding's versioned scanner profile and must reference the exact content hash in Pass 12 requirement 2. Evidence for a matched portion of text must store the registered evidence type, the profile-limited excerpt or hash, and offsets into the original text. Evidence for a condition that is not one text span must store its registered condition name, version, result, and referenced-content hash without invented offsets. Finding evidence, rules, and investigation details must never enter player APIs, notices, analytics, or public logs.

4. <a id="p12-r04"></a>**P12.R04 — Repeated scan finding identity**<br>Repeating an identical scan must reuse the current finding identity without duplicating evidence or attachment events. An edit, rule/configuration change, or changed evidence creates a new identity; findings absent from a complete current scan as defined in Pass 12 requirement 5 become immutable historical findings rather than being overwritten or reactivated.

5. <a id="p12-r05"></a>**P12.R05 — Current finding reconciliation**<br>Finding reconciliation compares the scan result with the locked current target and saved findings. It must lock and re-read the current target, use the locked current field values, and commit findings and case changes in one PostgreSQL transaction. A scan is complete only when every source field and rule assigned to that target and content context by one recorded catalog and scanner-profile version has run against the locked current content hash, every required evidence result has persisted, and no scan, evidence, transaction, or queue step has failed or timed out. Record the field and rule coverage, versions, content hash, and completion result before an absent finding is made historical or content is marked clean. A stale scan, concurrent scan, timeout, partial result, evidence-write failure, or queue failure must not mark content clean, clear newer findings, or commit a partial finding set.

6. <a id="p12-r06"></a>**P12.R06 — Community Game and Need a Sub content and chat review**<br>Community Game and Need a Sub content and chat review cases must remain distinct by target and category, with at most one open case for each target/category identity. Case creation and updates must use PostgreSQL uniqueness and acquire the same affected rows in one documented order rather than rely on coordination inside one application process.

7. <a id="p12-r07"></a>**P12.R07 — Versioned moderation review cases**<br>A review case must carry a version. Manual closure must provide the expected version and return a conflict without closing when the version is stale. Note creation and closure must use scoped idempotency keys; an exact replay returns the original result, while reuse with different input returns a conflict.

8. <a id="p12-r08"></a>**P12.R08 — Immutable review notes and events**<br>Review notes and case events must be immutable. Case creation, finding or signal attachment, note creation, priority change, enforcement link, automatic closure, and manual closure each create one ordered event linked to the resulting case version. A correction or reversal must create a new linked action; it must not rewrite earlier history.

9. <a id="p12-r09"></a>**P12.R09 — Enforcement and reversal approval**<br>Before implementing each enforcement, restoration, or reversal action, the moderation-policy decision maker assigned under Pass 12 requirement 1 must approve an enforcement-action matrix. For each permitted action, the matrix must identify the target and product area, authorized decision maker, required prior state, resulting state, restoration or reversal behavior, and required notice under Pass 12 requirements 10 and 11. It must state whether review is offered; when offered, it must give the request route, deadline, and review decision authority, and otherwise designate the action as non-reviewable. An action absent from the approved matrix must be rejected without a domain, case, notice, or audit outcome that reports it as performed; a new product workflow or state requires a master amendment under Section 3.4. The enforcement-action record must list every permitted action and the current state required before that action may occur. Each action must require active-administrator authorization, a named target, structured reason, scoped idempotency, one documented database-lock order, and a separate restoration or reversal action. The domain change, case/event link, audit record, and required notice must commit in one PostgreSQL transaction when they belong to one application outcome.

10. <a id="p12-r10"></a>**P12.R10 — Recipient notice transaction**<br>For an available intended recipient, enforcement success requires the notice defined by Pass 12 requirement 11 or the corresponding existing typed notification to commit in the same transaction. If the current stored relationship shows that a chat-message sender no longer exists, the action may omit notice only through the existing `recipient_unavailable` marker; the system must not create a placeholder recipient or add a generalized suppression workflow.

11. <a id="p12-r11"></a>**P12.R11 — Moderation notice content**<br>A notice must state the action, affected product area, the public reason code and user-facing text registered for that action type, stored duration when the enforced state has one, and the next step or review route defined for that action type. It must not contain internal administrator reasons, source content, finding evidence, rule identifiers, reporter or third-party identity, fraud/security details, Firebase/Stripe/R2 error details or configuration, SQL, or secrets.

12. <a id="p12-r12"></a>**P12.R12 — Moderation action approval prerequisites**<br>The enforcement-action record must carry the review decisions required by Pass 12 requirement 9 for every action before signoff. A completed review must record the requester, reviewer, evidence considered, decision, and time and must create a separate reversal or modification action without overwriting the original enforcement.

13. <a id="p12-r13"></a>**P12.R13 — Excerpt-first chat moderation responses**<br>Normal chat-moderation responses must be excerpt-first and must not contain unrestricted message bodies or unused sensitive identifiers. The server excerpt must normalize whitespace, replace each recognized phone, email, and link value with a fixed marker that contains none of the original value, and cap output at 160 characters including the ellipsis. Full message content may be returned only by an explicit, resource-bound reveal for one message.

   The versioned moderation catalog must define the normalization and matching contract for common email addresses, domestic and international telephone numbers, HTTP(S) and `www` links, and schemeless link forms. The child plan must specify the parser and false-negative tests. When a value cannot be masked safely under the contract, suppress the excerpt instead of returning the raw value; every emitted excerpt retains the fixed markers and 160-character cap.

14. <a id="p12-r14"></a>**P12.R14 — Pre-read sensitive audit**<br>A sensitive-read audit must commit before the backend loads protected message content, review evidence, private notes, detailed payment context, or audit detail to build the response. If audit persistence fails or its outcome is unknown, the protected content must not be disclosed.

   Detailed payment context means fields beyond the owner-visible status, amount, currency, time, and payment purpose, including Stripe Customer or PaymentMethod identifiers, card metadata, credit-ledger details, refund or dispute details, and correction context. For any actor, inventory each route and query that loads such fields and commit its sensitive-read audit before loading them; a failed or unknown audit result must disclose none of them.

15. <a id="p12-r15"></a>**P12.R15 — Sensitive message reveal authorization**<br>A reveal must verify active-administrator state in the service and confirm that the message belongs to the supplied parent and game/post type. A valid message paired with the wrong parent must not disclose content or cross-resource existence.

16. <a id="p12-r16"></a>**P12.R16 — Moderation mutation responses**<br>Moderation note, close, mark-reviewed, remove, and restore mutations must return acknowledgment fields only. They must not return full message content, review evidence, or private notes as a mutation side effect.

17. <a id="p12-r17"></a>**P12.R17 — Late sensitive-read result handling**<br>The frontend must discard a late sensitive-read result after the modal closes, the selected message or parent changes, pagination/filter context changes, the user changes, or the component unmounts. Sensitive responses must remain private and non-cacheable.

18. <a id="p12-r18"></a>**P12.R18 — No sensitive-data export**<br>Moderation and audit interfaces must not provide a sensitive-data export.

19. <a id="p12-r19"></a>**P12.R19 — Campaign records and idempotency**<br>A platform-notice campaign must keep immutable notice content/version, audience definition or snapshot, recipient state, and delivery attempts as separate records. Campaign creation, recipient generation, delivery, cancellation, correction, and retry must be idempotent. Recipient generation or delivery that cannot finish within the Pass 06 request-duration and transaction budgets must use durable batched work.

   **1. Campaign activation and audience snapshot.** At campaign activation, record one audience cutoff and materialize the eligible recipient set against that cutoff. Retry and partial-batch recovery must use that same set and must not add a user who became eligible later.

    **2. Delivery prohibition inventory.** Before activation, the campaign contract must list each delivery prohibition for that notice type and its authoritative state, including deletion, applicable opt-out, and suppression.

    **3. Durable dispatch intent.** The final provider-dispatch decision is the durable external-call intent required by Pass 11 requirement 3. At that decision, re-read the materialized recipient's state and use the same serialization point as any Pickup Lane change to that state so a prohibition committed before the intent cannot be missed. A prohibited recipient must receive no new provider-call intent or provider call; record a skipped result under the original campaign and recipient identity.

    **4. Final pre-call checks.** Before starting a call for an existing intent, recheck current prohibitions and cancel the unsent call if one has taken effect. If an external provider owns a prohibition state, the contract must name its final pre-dispatch check and the failure result when that state cannot be read; it must not claim atomicity with that provider.

    **5. Calls already started or accepted.** A state change after a provider call has begun or been accepted cannot retroactively cancel that call and follows the unknown-outcome or provider-result rules in Pass 11. A newly prohibited recipient must not receive a notice from any new provider call after the final pre-call check finds the prohibition in effect.

#### Integration boundaries

- Pass 07 requirements 1, 7 and 8, 11; Pass 10 requirement 6 and Pass 08 requirements 1–3, 6 and 7, 10; Pass 09 requirement 16 supply identity and authorization for moderation operations. Current moderation lists, review-case detail, and explicit message reveal use that active-administrator gate and the pre-disclosure audit in Pass 12 requirement 14 without adding a recent-authentication requirement or a granular administrator role.
- Pass 05 requirements 1–4, 11 and 12 supply constraint, transaction, lock-order, serialization, and unknown-commit behavior. Pass 11 requirements 1–7 own durable delivery after a notice obligation commits. Pass 09 requirements 8–14 own audit storage and protected audit reads.
- Assignment, reassignment, reopen, merge, formal note correction, generalized suppression, a moderation history explorer, machine-learning moderation, and a new export product are outside this master.
- The Pass 12 child plan must name the exact upstream requirement used by each scan, case, enforcement, notice, review, and reveal operation rather than depend on completion of an entire parent pass.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p12-v01"></a>**P12.V01 — Moderation scanner groups**<br>For Pass 12 requirements 1–5, run the following scan groups. For each case, inspect finding identity, exact source hash, stored version fields, profile limits, evidence offsets or condition records, current and historical status, case state, and transaction result.

   - **Finding identity and version changes:** Scan identical content twice; edited content; content under a changed rule, profile, configuration, or normalization version; and a finding that later reappears. Confirm that identical input reuses one finding and changed input creates the required new identity. Inspect current and historical status for the reappearing finding.
   - **Approved catalog examples:** For every approved context, source field, language, and rule, run its positive and negative synthetic examples and inspect the catalog version, matched rule, evidence, and expected finding or no-finding result. Confirm that unapproved field, language, or matching behavior is not silently treated as covered.
   - **Complete and competing scans:** Run a clean rescan covering every assigned source field and rule for one recorded catalog and scanner-profile version and the locked current content hash; inspect persisted evidence and the completion result. Then omit one field, one rule, or one required evidence result, and run a stale and concurrent scan and a scan that stops partway. Confirm that only the complete current scan makes absent findings historical or marks content clean and that stale, incomplete, or failed work never clears newer findings.
   - **Persistence and queue failures:** Force an evidence-write failure and a queue failure. Confirm that failed work never clears newer findings or marks content clean.

   For each group, inspect a player response, notice, analytics event, and public log; confirm that no evidence leaks through any of them.

2. <a id="p12-v02"></a>**P12.V02 — Concurrent review-case creation**<br>For Pass 12 requirements 6–8, race creation of the same target/category case, different notes, exact and changed-input note replays, note against closure, finding attachment against closure, enforcement linking against closure, and manual against automatic closure. Inspect database constraints, lock order, case versions, ordered events, and prior history.
