Confirm that at most one case remains open for the identity, exact replays return the original result, stale or changed requests conflict, and no correction or reversal rewrites an earlier note or event.

3. <a id="p12-v03"></a>**P12.V03 — Moderation action and reversal cases**<br>For Pass 12 requirements 9–12, exercise every enforcement, restoration, reversal, and review action with valid and stale state, exact replay, changed-input idempotency reuse, an available and unavailable recipient, notice-write failure, audit-write failure, and each reviewable and non-reviewable action. Inspect domain state, case version and events, notice copy, audit record, and transaction result. Confirm that one complete outcome commits or every part rolls back, the only missing-recipient exception uses `recipient_unavailable`, notice text contains every permitted field and none of the prohibited information, and review creates a new linked action.

   Compare every exercised action with the approved enforcement-action matrix: test its prior and resulting state, authorized decision maker, separate reversal, required notice, review route and deadline, and one unlisted action. Confirm each listed action follows its approved row and an unlisted action has no enforcement effect.

4. <a id="p12-v04"></a>**P12.V04 — Pre-read audit failure cases**<br>For Pass 12 requirements 13–17, list chat moderation results, reveal one message, pair a valid message with the wrong parent and type, force the pre-read audit to fail or return an unknown result, perform each minimized mutation, cache a response, and deliver a late frontend result after each invalidating event in Pass 12 requirement 17. Record the excerpt length and substitutions, returned fields, audit commit time, cache headers and contents, and displayed frontend state. Confirm that unrestricted content appears only for the correctly bound reveal after its audit commits and that mutation, cache, wrong-parent, failed-audit, and stale-frontend paths disclose no protected content.

   Test each catalogued email, domestic/international phone, HTTP(S), `www`, and schemeless-link form after normalization, plus malformed and ambiguous examples. Confirm the registered version and that an unsafe-to-mask result suppresses the excerpt rather than exposing its value.

   For an owner summary and each detailed-payment route/query in the inventory, inspect load order and audit commit. Force a failed and unknown pre-read audit for a user and administrator; confirm restricted identifiers, card metadata, ledger, dispute, refund, and correction fields are never loaded or returned before a committed audit.

5. <a id="p12-v05"></a>**P12.V05 — Moderation data export inspection**<br>For Pass 12 requirement 18, inspect every moderation and audit route and interface for download or export behavior and attempt to request results through query, pagination, and content-negotiation inputs. Record the routes and returned fields. Confirm that no sensitive-data export exists.

6. <a id="p12-v06"></a>**P12.V06 — Platform notice delivery and replay**<br>For Pass 12 requirement 19, exercise platform-notice creation, audience generation, delivery, exact replay, changed-input replay, duplicate worker claim, partial batch failure, cancellation, correction, and retry. Inspect immutable content and version, audience definition or snapshot, recipient rows, delivery attempts, batches, and user-visible notices. Confirm one truthful result per recipient and durable batched work whenever generation or delivery exceeds the request or transaction budget.

   Change audience eligibility after activation and separately delete, opt out, or suppress a materialized recipient before the final dispatch decision; change one prohibition between an earlier eligibility check and that decision, another after intent is persisted but before the provider call begins, and another after provider acceptance. Inspect the authoritative prohibition state, final and pre-call checks, provider-call intent and result, and skipped-recipient record. Confirm retry preserves the original cutoff set, adds no newly eligible user, dispatches nothing when a prohibition was committed before the final decision, cancels an unsent call when the pre-call check finds a new prohibition, and does not describe an already accepted send as retroactively cancelled.

#### Completion condition

Moderation and notice behavior, audit and notification obligations, and denial handling work in development; they are rechecked in final integration qualification.

---

<a id="p13"></a>

### Pass 13 — Bookings, Payments, Refunds, and Credits

#### Purpose

Implement booking/payment/refund/credit ledgers and Stripe consistency, including the previously approved credit calculation, customer checkout disclosures, reconciliation and receipts. Live Stripe credentials are configured at release.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p13-r01"></a>**P13.R01 — Financial record monetary fields**<br>Booking, payment, refund, credit, and compensation records must each store their own current state and links to the related records. A single status field must not imply that both Stripe and Pickup Lane capacity reached the same outcome.

   **Approved transitions.** Before implementing each financial operation, the named Pass 01 Stripe/finance responsibility must approve a state-transition table for that operation. Every permitted transition in that table must identify its triggering event, required prior and resulting states for the affected records, authoritative Stripe or Pickup Lane evidence, booking-capacity and credit effects, durable obligations, user-visible outcome, and treatment when an external or database result is unknown. An operation with no approved transition must not be implemented as an inferred state change.

2. <a id="p13-r02"></a>**P13.R02 — Stripe payment and refund authority**<br>Stripe determines Stripe-side payment and refund truth. Pickup Lane determines booking and capacity truth. Reconciliation compares Stripe payment and refund state with Pickup Lane's linked local payment, booking, and capacity state, records a mismatch, and follows the governing payment or booking requirement to reach or preserve a truthful result instead of overwriting one authority with the other.

3. <a id="p13-r03"></a>**P13.R03 — Authoritative payment amount calculation**<br>The server must calculate the payable amount, currency, booking target, and permitted credit use from authoritative application data. Client-submitted totals and success claims must not determine a charge or booking.

4. <a id="p13-r04"></a>**P13.R04 — One operation per purchase attempt**<br>One business purchase attempt must map to one local operation and one Stripe PaymentIntent, the Stripe object that tracks that payment through its lifecycle and can include multiple payment attempts. Creating or confirming that payment operation must use an idempotency key tied to the business attempt. An exact replay returns the original operation; reusing the key with a different user, booking, amount, currency, or operation type must conflict. Repeating a request after a timeout must not create a second charge, booking, refund, or credit consumption.

5. <a id="p13-r05"></a>**P13.R05 — Raw Stripe webhook signature verification**<br>Stripe webhook signatures must be verified against the raw request body. An invalid signature, wrong secret, malformed event, or unsupported event type must not change application state.

6. <a id="p13-r06"></a>**P13.R06 — Duplicate and delayed Stripe webhooks**<br>Stripe webhook delivery may be duplicated, delayed, or out of order. Processing must record the Stripe event identity, apply each business transition once, and reject a transition that would move state backward or create duplicate effects.

7. <a id="p13-r07"></a>**P13.R07 — Unknown payment outcome recovery**<br>When Stripe authorizes or captures payment but Pickup Lane cannot assign capacity, the local operation must enter a persisted compensation state linked to the payment and failed booking. If Stripe has authorized but not captured the payment, Pickup Lane must cancel the authorization. If Stripe has captured the payment, Pickup Lane must automatically refund the full amount charged to the original payment method. Pickup Lane must not substitute application credit or require the user to choose a compensation type. If the Stripe outcome is unknown, Pickup Lane must reconcile the payment before attempting cancellation or refund. The compensation state must remain visible in the user's booking or payment status and the restricted operational view until Stripe confirms the cancellation or full refund or the operation enters a recorded failure requiring intervention. Processing or replay must not create a booking, duplicate cancellation, duplicate refund, or credit, and the user must not be shown a confirmed booking while capacity is absent.

   Credit reserved or spent for the same booking attempt must remain linked to that attempt. When booking failure or compensation is terminal, release an unused credit reservation or create one immutable reversal of spent credit. While Stripe or booking truth is unknown, keep the credit result pending until reconciliation establishes whether to release or reverse it. The credit and Stripe compensation steps must each be idempotent; replay must not restore credit twice or change the required cancellation or original-method refund.

8. <a id="p13-r08"></a>**P13.R08 — Payment-gated booking confirmation**<br>A booked spot must not become confirmed until the server-recorded payment requirement for that booking type is satisfied. A payment record alone must not bypass capacity or authorization checks.

9. <a id="p13-r09"></a>**P13.R09 — Refund outcome tracking**<br>Refund creation, webhook confirmation, failure, retry, and manual intervention must remain distinguishable. Retrying a refund must not issue a second refund for the same obligation.

10. <a id="p13-r10"></a>**P13.R10 — Credit source and redemption traceability**<br>Credit issuance and redemption must have a traceable source and exact amount. Concurrent redemption must not consume the same credit twice or create a negative balance.

   Every reservation and spend must reference its booking attempt. A terminally failed or compensated attempt follows Pass 13 requirement 7 for release or one immutable reversal; unknown payment or booking state holds the credit result pending, and concurrent or replayed settlement must not return credit twice.

11. <a id="p13-r11"></a>**P13.R11 — Financial reconciliation mismatches**<br>Reconciliation must detect at least: Stripe success without booking, booking requiring payment without Stripe success, refund obligation without a Stripe refund, Stripe refund without matching application state, duplicate Stripe event, and state unchanged beyond its processing deadline. Each detected mismatch must create or update a restricted reconciliation item containing the affected local and Stripe record references, mismatch type, first and latest detection times, current resolution state, and authorized next action; it must remain visible until resolved.

    **Approved resolution.** Before implementing a reconciliation handler, the named Pass 01 Stripe/finance responsibility must approve a mismatch-to-action table covering each mismatch in this requirement and each additional mismatch handled under Pass 13 requirement 18. For each mismatch, the table must identify the authoritative Stripe and Pickup Lane checks, prerequisites and current-state validation, the permitted automatic action or administrator-only action, its original obligation and idempotency identity, the result when evidence is missing or an external outcome is unknown, and the evidence required to close the item. A handler must not infer a repair from a mismatch type alone.

12. <a id="p13-r12"></a>**P13.R12 — Administrative financial correction safeguards**<br>An administrative financial correction must satisfy Pass 08 requirement 3; Pass 09 requirement 16; Pass 10 requirement 7 and commit the correction-outcome Pass 09 audit record or durable audit obligation in the same PostgreSQL transaction as the local correction. The record must show the prior local state, requested action, resulting state, administrator, reason, and linked Stripe or local operation. A failure of the same-transaction outcome audit or durable obligation must leave the local financial correction unchanged; an external post-action audit failure follows Pass 09 requirement 11.

   An earlier correction-attempt audit record is supplementary and does not prove that the local correction committed. Any related Stripe or other external mutation follows Pass 09 requirement 11: durable intent before dispatch, confirmed outcome afterward, and reconciliation of an unknown result without a rollback claim.

13. <a id="p13-r13"></a>**P13.R13 — Prohibited financial and secret storage**<br>Logs, errors, audit records, and application tables must not store full card data, PaymentIntent or SetupIntent client secrets, Stripe webhook secrets, Stripe secret keys, or credentials for Firebase, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL, hosting, DNS, CI, monitoring, or backups.

14. <a id="p13-r14"></a>**P13.R14 — PaymentIntent client secret access**<br>A PaymentIntent client secret may be returned only to the authenticated user for the matching active checkout. It must not appear in a URL, log, analytic event, support artifact, audit record, or another user's response.

15. <a id="p13-r15"></a>**P13.R15 — Permitted stored data and prohibited fields**<br>A saved card may store only the Stripe Customer identifier, Stripe PaymentMethod identifier, Stripe card fingerprint used for duplicate detection, card brand, last four digits, expiration month and year, attachment lifecycle status, default flag, and lifecycle timestamps. It must not store the primary account number, CVC, PIN data, magnetic-stripe data, client secret, or an unrestricted Stripe API response or webhook payload.

    **Stripe ownership, attachment, and isolation.** The server must verify Stripe Customer and PaymentMethod ownership, current attachment state, consent, and cross-user isolation.

    **Consent to save.** Before saving a card for later checkout, the user must see how it may be reused and affirmatively choose to save it; the consent record must identify the accepted disclosure version and time.

    **Separate off-session charge consent.** Saving a card and consenting to a later off-session waitlist charge are separate decisions and records. Before an off-session waitlist charge (a later charge when the user is not actively checking out) is authorized, the user must see the charge trigger, how its amount is determined, and how to cancel the authorization; the separate affirmative consent record must identify that disclosure version and time. A card must not be saved or charged for a use lacking its required consent.

   **Same-user and same-customer duplicate detection.** For duplicate detection, compare the Stripe PaymentMethod ID and fingerprint only among saved methods belonging to the same Pickup Lane user and Stripe Customer. When a match is established, reuse or offer the existing saved-card entry instead of creating a second local entry. A fingerprint must not be compared across users or customers, disclosed as a cross-account match, treated as ownership proof, or used alone to deny a charge.

16. <a id="p13-r16"></a>**P13.R16 — Refund amount and currency validation**<br>A refund obligation must validate amount, currency, and remaining refundable value against authoritative records. Before implementing an administrative refund amount input, the named Pass 01 Stripe/finance responsibility must approve whether the administrator may propose an amount or only confirm the amount calculated for the obligation, the permitted currency and range, and the authoritative records used to validate them. A proposed amount is input for server validation, not authority to refund more than the remaining obligation. Stripe success, Stripe failure, unknown Stripe outcome, local recording, notice delivery, and reconciliation must remain distinguishable.

17. <a id="p13-r17"></a>**P13.R17 — Immutable credit ledger entries**<br>Credit issuance, spend, and reversal must each create a separate immutable ledger entry containing the user, exact amount and currency, source booking or payment, operation type, time, actor or originating operation, and link to the entry it spends or reverses. Before implementing an administrative credit amount input, the named Pass 01 Stripe/finance responsibility must approve whether the administrator may propose an amount or only confirm the source-derived amount, the permitted same-source currency and range, and validation against the remaining issuance cap approved under Pass 02 requirement 7. A proposed amount is input for server validation, not authority to exceed that cap. Generic administrative issuance requires a validated source booking or payment owned by the credited user and cannot exceed the remaining source-derived amount. Source-less discretionary credit is prohibited.

18. <a id="p13-r18"></a>**P13.R18 — Stripe and local reconciliation**<br>Reconciliation must compare Stripe PaymentIntents, charges, refunds, disputes and asynchronous reversals with local payments, bookings, refund obligations, compensation records, and credits. It must run on demand and at the numeric interval chosen under Section 3.3 after measured transaction volume, expected webhook delay, maximum unreconciled financial exposure, and operations response capacity are recorded. The scheduled reconciliation work must remain unstarted while those inputs could change its design or verification.

   Reconciliation must include credit reservation, spend, release, and reversal linked to a failed or compensated booking attempt. It must preserve a pending credit result while Stripe or booking truth remains unknown and detect duplicate or missing credit recovery.

19. <a id="p13-r19"></a>**P13.R19 — Public payment disclosures**<br>Before accepting a live payment, the public site must make Pickup Lane's business name, game-service description, transaction currency, customer-service contact, the legally approved, versioned Terms of Service required by Pass 02 requirement 6 and presented under Pass 17 requirement 22, and the actual refund, dispute, and applicable cancellation rules available without sign-in; the refund rules must explain how a customer requests a refund, and all of these disclosures must be accessible before checkout confirmation. Development and staging checkout implementation and verification use that approved version without waiting for production publication; Pass 21 requirement 4 separately verifies its correct production publication before final acceptance.

    A checkout must not proceed to payment confirmation while a required disclosure is missing or inaccessible to that customer or the acceptance required by Pass 07 requirement 14 is absent.

    The displayed terms and rules must match the implemented booking, payment, refund, credit, and dispute outcomes and must not describe an official-game payment or community-organizer responsibility inaccurately.

20. <a id="p13-r20"></a>**P13.R20 — Administrative financial action controls**<br>Administrative refund creation or retry, credit issuance or reversal, financial correction, payment-event repair, and manual reconciliation mutation must require active-administrator authorization, recent authentication, a server-validated target, reason, preview and confirmation, current-state validation, idempotency, and audit. A dual-approval workflow is prohibited.

    **Amount input.** Before implementing each action, the named Pass 01 Stripe/finance responsibility must record whether it accepts an administrator-proposed amount, uses only a server-calculated amount, or has no amount, and must approve the currency, range, and authoritative validation rule for any amount-bearing action. The rule must satisfy Pass 13 requirements 16 and 17 where they apply. For an amount-bearing action, the server must derive the authoritative currency and permitted amount or range from linked records and validate any proposal within it. A non-amount action must reject an amount field.

    **Preview and confirmation.** The server-generated preview must identify the validated target, operation, amount and currency when applicable, and current state; confirmation must bind to that preview and revalidate current state and the allowed amount before any local or Stripe effect. A changed target, amount, currency, operation, or state must require a new preview.

21. <a id="p13-r21"></a>**P13.R21 — Checkout return URL handling**<br>A checkout request can omit `return_url`. When the request contains `return_url`, the value must use `http` or `https`, contain no embedded credentials, query, or fragment, match an exact configured application origin, and use exactly `/games/{game_id}/checkout` for the same game before a Stripe request begins.

22. <a id="p13-r22"></a>**P13.R22 — Prohibited generic financial mutation routes**<br>Generic client-writable routes that create, read, update, or delete arbitrary payment, refund, or payment-event records must remain unavailable. A retained payment-event repair may change only its approved processing linkage, status, and error fields and must not rewrite Stripe event identity, type, raw payload, or Stripe timestamps.

23. <a id="p13-r23"></a>**P13.R23 — Saved payment method synchronization input**<br>Saved-payment-method synchronization must accept one trimmed, nonblank `setup_intent_id` of at most 255 characters. This identifier names Stripe's record for collecting and approving a payment method without making a charge, called a SetupIntent. Pickup Lane must treat the identifier as an opaque value and rely on Stripe to verify its existence, status, customer, ownership, and PaymentMethod legitimacy rather than trusting a string prefix.

24. <a id="p13-r24"></a>**P13.R24 — Durable Stripe webhook event intake**<br>The webhook request must verify and durably record the Stripe event or resulting work before acknowledging success within the Pass 06 request budget. Work that can outlive that budget must continue through Pass 11 rather than keeping the webhook request open or dropping the event.

25. <a id="p13-r25"></a>**P13.R25 — Customer payment receipts**<br>For each completed Stripe payment transaction in a released Pickup Lane checkout or charge flow, Pickup Lane must offer the customer one complete and accurate transaction receipt. Stripe may provide that receipt on Pickup Lane's behalf through its configured receipt service; this requirement does not require a separate receipt system. The receipt must identify Pickup Lane and the payment purpose and intended game or service, and reflect the actual successful Stripe charge, amount charged by Stripe after any application credit, currency, and transaction time. A payment authorization without capture, failed payment, request retry, or duplicate webhook delivery must not produce a receipt that falsely reports a completed payment or a second receipt for the same completed transaction. A receipt for a captured payment later requiring compensation must report the payment truthfully without representing an unavailable booking as confirmed; the refund and user-visible compensation outcomes remain governed by Pass 13 requirements 7, 9, 11, 16, 18, and 19.

   The completed transaction must make its Stripe-hosted receipt reachable through an authenticated customer transaction view bound to the correct charge and customer. An existing view must be used when it can satisfy this access rule; a new interface requires its exact requirement and scope to be recorded before expansion. Stripe receipt email is supplemental, and an expired hosted link must offer the provider-supported email reissue path without implying a failed or uncaptured payment completed. The receipt verification plan must identify how the selected Stripe staging environment can supply an expired hosted link and exercise that reissue path safely, or record the provider limitation and the Section 3.3 evidence method before claiming that behavior verified.

#### Integration boundaries

- This pass does not authorize a generalized financial workflow engine.
- Pass 07 requirements 1, 7 and 8, 11, 14; Pass 10 requirement 1 supply identity, recent-authentication, and applicable terms-acceptance behavior. Pass 08 requirements 1–3, 5–7, 9; Pass 09 requirement 16; Pass 10 requirement 7 supply authorization and field restrictions. Pass 05 requirements 1–5, 10–12; Pass 20 requirement 4 supply monetary storage, transaction, concurrency, idempotency, and unknown-database-outcome controls. Pass 05 requirements 15 and 16, 22–24, 27 supply database operation and pagination behavior. Pass 02 requirement 6 supplies the reviewed terms, Pass 17 requirement 22 presents them in the release build, Pass 13 requirement 19 requires their availability at payment confirmation, and Pass 21 requirement 4 publishes them at the production address.
- Pass 11 requirements 1–9, 12 own durable webhook, compensation, refund, and reconciliation execution after this pass defines the business obligation and result. Pass 09 requirement 4; Pass 18 requirements 1–3 own alerts for unresolved financial and reconciliation states. Pass 09 requirements 8–14 own the audit records required here. Pass 01 requirements 3, 6; Pass 03 requirements 1–3; Pass 10 requirement 8; Pass 19 requirements 3 and 4 own Stripe credentials and production access.
- Pass 13 requirements 18, 24; Pass 19 requirement 7 must not complete until the production Stripe account and endpoint, release versions, deployment design, and durable-work path they use have been selected. Existing payment code and test-mode evidence may be inspected, but neither proves those production bindings.
- The Pass 13 child plan must map each booking, payment, refund, credit, compensation, webhook, and reconciliation operation to the exact requirements it consumes; completion of an entire upstream pass does not prove a financial outcome.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p13-v01"></a>**P13.V01 — Paid booking and payment failure cases**<br>For Pass 13 requirements 1–4, 7 and 8, exercise a successful paid booking, decline, timeout before Stripe receives the request, timeout after Stripe success, exact request replay, changed-input idempotency reuse, two users racing for the final place, authorization without capture after capacity is lost, captured payment after capacity is lost, and an unknown Stripe outcome after capacity is lost. In the successful checkout exercise, separately submit and record conflicting client total, currency, and credit-use claims with otherwise valid input. Independently derive and record the permitted amount, currency, and credit from authoritative application data; confirm each conflicting claim is rejected or ignored as required by the route contract. For every scenario, including the conflicting-claim attempts, inspect the non-production Stripe attempt and local records: the server-derived amount and currency, local operation, Stripe PaymentIntent, authorization, charge, cancellation or refund, booking and capacity rows, compensation state, job, notice, and user-visible status. Confirm that conflicting client claims cannot change the Stripe amount or currency, permitted credit use, or local booking and ledger results; rejected claims create no prohibited Stripe or local effect. Confirm that an uncaptured authorization is cancelled, a captured payment is fully refunded to the original payment method, an unknown outcome is reconciled before compensation proceeds, and no scenario creates a second charge, booking, capacity assignment, cancellation, refund, or credit.

   Compare each exercised transition with the finance-approved table for that operation. Record its trigger, prior and resulting states, authoritative evidence, capacity and credit effects, durable obligation, user-visible result, and unknown-result treatment; confirm no unapproved transition occurs.

2. <a id="p13-v02"></a>**P13.V02 — Refund failure and replay cases**<br>For Pass 13 requirements 9–11, 16, 18, exercise refund success, Stripe failure, timeout with unknown Stripe outcome, exact refund retry, concurrent refund attempts, duplicate Stripe refund, Stripe success without booking, booking without required Stripe success, refund obligation without refund, Stripe refund without local state, duplicate event, processing deadline exceeded, dispute, and asynchronous reversal. Run both scheduled and on-demand reconciliation. Inspect Stripe and local payment, booking, refund, compensation, credit, notice, job, audit, and reconciliation-item state. Confirm that each mismatch remains visible until one truthful resolution and that no retry or repair duplicates financial value.

   Use credit on a booking that fails before spend, on one that fails after spend, and on one with an unknown Stripe or booking outcome. Replay compensation and credit settlement. Inspect the shared attempt identity and immutable ledger; confirm one release or reversal only after terminal truth, no premature credit restoration, and the required original-method Stripe compensation.

   For each detected mismatch, compare the authoritative reads, prerequisites, automatic or administrator-only action, idempotency identity, unknown-result state, and closure evidence with its finance-approved mismatch-to-action row. Withhold one required piece of evidence and attempt an unapproved repair; confirm the item remains visible and no unsupported mutation or duplicate value occurs.

3. <a id="p13-v03"></a>**P13.V03 — Administrative financial action cases**<br>For Pass 13 requirements 12, 17, 20, 22, attempt each administrative refund, retry, credit issuance or reversal, correction, event repair, and reconciliation change with valid and stale authentication, authorized and unauthorized administrators, correct and changed confirmation data, valid and stale current state, exact and changed-input replay, missing reason, invalid source, excessive value, and forced audit failure. Attempt to change every immutable Stripe-event field. Confirm that only the fully authorized action commits once, every ledger entry has its required immutable fields and links, and a failed safeguard, source check, audit, or prohibited-field change leaves financial state unchanged.

   For each approved credit source, test settlement, same-currency gross customer-paid value before refunds or chargebacks, previously issued application credit excluded from that base, later refund or finalized chargeback loss, prior spent credit, and a reversal with and without extinguished liability. Record the gross base, distinct returned or lost amounts, prior issued credits, net retained customer-paid value, and nonnegative remaining issuance capacity. With $100 gross settled customer payment, a $20 refund, and $10 previously issued credit, confirm $70 remains; a second record or replay of the same $20 refund must leave $70, and overlapping refund and chargeback records must not deduct the same returned or lost amount twice. When deductions exceed the gross base, confirm the remaining issuance capacity is zero. Inspect immutable links and the named finance approval; confirm issuance stays within the recorded cap and no undecided source or accounting rule is treated as approved. Force audit failure after an earlier attempt record but before local correction commit; confirm no correction without same-transaction outcome audit or durable obligation.

   For each administrative action, inspect the finance-approved amount proposal rule or record that the action has no amount. Where a proposal is permitted, test a valid proposed amount; where only a server-calculated amount is permitted, attempt a proposal and confirm rejection. Also test an out-of-range or wrong-currency amount, an amount on a non-amount action, and confirmation after the target, amount, currency, operation, or state differs from the server-generated preview. Confirm only the currently valid preview can commit and that every rejected input leaves local and Stripe state unchanged.

4. <a id="p13-v04"></a>**P13.V04 — Checkout and reconciliation test artifacts**<br>For Pass 13 requirements 13–15, 23, search application tables, logs, errors, audit records, analytics, URLs, and support or test artifacts after checkout, saved-card synchronization, webhook, failure, and reconciliation scenarios. Request one user's client secret or saved method as another user; submit a SetupIntent belonging to another customer, unattached method, invalid status, blank identifier, 256-character identifier, and opaque valid identifier; and separately record card saving and off-session consent. Inspect the disclosure shown before each decision, its accepted version and time, and the result when consent is absent or covers a different use. Confirm that only the allowed saved-card fields persist, Stripe performs the authoritative SetupIntent and PaymentMethod checks, the two consent decisions cover their stated uses and remain separate, no card is saved or charged without its required consent, and no prohibited secret, card data, payload, credential, or cross-user result appears.

   Save the same PaymentMethod twice and a different PaymentMethod with a matching fingerprint within one user and Stripe Customer, then exercise another user and Stripe Customer. Confirm one local saved entry where a same-scope match is established, no cross-scope lookup or inference, and no payment denial solely from the fingerprint.

5. <a id="p13-v05"></a>**P13.V05 — Generic financial route rejection**<br>For Pass 13 requirements 21 and 22, submit return addresses with the wrong scheme, credentials, query, fragment, origin, path, or game identifier and attempt every generic client-writable payment, refund, and event mutation route. Record rejection before a Stripe request or local financial change. Confirm that only the exact configured origin and `/games/{game_id}/checkout` path for the same game is accepted and that generic mutation routes remain unavailable.

**Development-stage manual verification:** For Pass 13 requirement 25, use the Section 3.2 development-stage build, synthetic customers, and the dedicated development Stripe general sandbox. Complete one captured payment, one authorization without capture, and one failed payment. For the captured transaction, open its receipt from the authenticated customer transaction view as the owner and attempt the same view as another customer. Compare the receipt with the Stripe charge and local payment and booking records; record the business and transaction purpose, amount after any application credit, currency, transaction time, receipt access result, and any later compensation wording. Confirm owner-only access and a truthful completed-payment receipt for the captured charge, and no completed-payment receipt or false booking confirmation for the uncaptured or failed transaction. Inspect local and Stripe state to confirm the denied access and unsuccessful transactions create no prohibited charge, booking, or receipt-triggering effect. Pass 20 verification 20 repeats receipt coverage with the staging Stripe sandbox and adds expired-link, reissue, and final provider-setting evidence.

#### Completion condition

Development Stripe sandbox evidence from the development-stage manual verification for requirement 25 and Pass 13 verification 1–5 clauses that exercise implemented financial operations proves core financial invariants, credit formula, public disclosures, and customer receipt access without live charges. Record each clause requiring final production Stripe bindings and its Pass 19 or Pass 20 trigger as incomplete until that assigned check passes.

<a id="p14"></a>

### Pass 14 — Venue Images and Cloudflare R2

#### Purpose

Implement administrator-controlled venue image handling, Cloudflare R2 metadata/objects, signed capabilities, validation, lifecycle, cleanup, and privacy protections.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p14-r01"></a>**P14.R01 — Administrator-only image changes**<br>Only an authenticated active administrator may initiate, publish, replace, or remove a venue image. Players and community hosts must not upload avatars or venue images. A separate venue-image permission is prohibited.

2. <a id="p14-r02"></a>**P14.R02 — Generated avatar initials**<br>User avatars must use generated initials. Preset-avatar and user-uploaded-avatar systems are outside the production requirement.

3. <a id="p14-r03"></a>**P14.R03 — Unpredictable R2 object keys**<br>The backend must generate an unpredictable R2 object key without a filename, user-controlled path part, or personal data. A time-limited signed upload or download capability must use the expiry chosen under Section 3.3 and must authorize only its recorded HTTP method, exact object key, and pending upload or protected-read record. Only an authenticated, authorized active administrator may obtain a signed upload capability from the backend. The backend may return its signed `PUT` URL in an authenticated HTTPS API response with `Cache-Control: no-store`; the administrator browser may hold it temporarily in memory and use it as the HTTPS request destination for the approved upload to the private R2 bucket. Its presence in that browser network request, browser developer network inspection, and the request received by Cloudflare R2 is permitted. A signed URL may be reused until it expires; possession of the URL or a successful upload does not establish administrator authority to publish or bypass pending-record and publication checks. Signed private-read capabilities must remain server-side and must not be returned to the browser. The capability and its signature must not appear in application logs, analytics, audit records, or error telemetry.

4. <a id="p14-r04"></a>**P14.R04 — Staged venue-image workflow**<br>Venue images must use a staged workflow: authorize, create pending database metadata, upload privately, confirm actual object metadata, validate and process, then publish sanitized output. Client claims or a successful upload alone must not publish an object.

5. <a id="p14-r05"></a>**P14.R05 — Image type and content validation**<br>Upload validation must compare the client-declared type and extension with the file signature and decoded content and must measure received bytes, encoded bytes, dimensions, decoded-pixel count, decompression behavior, and decode time. Before implementation, the venue-image contract must state whether SVG and animated input are rejected or converted to a named non-scriptable, non-animated output. The filename and client-declared type alone must never approve a file. Exceeding a byte, dimension, pixel, decompression, or processing-time limit chosen under Section 3.3 must reject publication.

6. <a id="p14-r06"></a>**P14.R06 — Bounded image processing**<br>An accepted image must be processed within the CPU, memory, decoded-pixel, and elapsed-time limits chosen under Section 3.3, have embedded metadata removed, and be re-encoded into each exact output format, dimension, and quality listed in the venue-image contract. Only those newly encoded outputs may be published. A processing failure must leave the upload unpublished with an explicit failed state, and the original upload must never be served publicly.

7. <a id="p14-r07"></a>**P14.R07 — Public image and private source separation**<br>Sanitized public outputs and private staging or original objects must use separate key spaces and access rules. A public response must use the content type that matches its bytes, the inline or download behavior recorded for that format, and the cache rule recorded for its immutable versioned key.

   A private object must reject public access and may be read only through a backend-authorized, time-limited capability bound as required by Pass 14 requirement 3. A private-read capability remains server-side; it must not become a browser image address or public delivery path.

   The selected venue-image contract uses a **public sanitized-output bucket and a separate private original/staging/master bucket for each of development, staging and production**.

   Temporary uploads, originals, and private masters must be in the non-public bucket with separately scoped access; key prefixes inside a public bucket are not an access boundary.

   Only validated, re-encoded derivatives may enter the public bucket. A configured public domain may serve only those derivatives, without a signed private-read capability. The private bucket's browser-origin CORS rules must permit the recorded administrator frontend origin, `PUT` method, and required upload headers for the approved direct upload and must not permit other browser origins, methods, or headers. CORS does not authenticate the administrator or make a signed URL single-use.

   Any future controlled private-delivery alternative requires an explicit recorded architecture revision and equivalent negative tests; it is not an undocumented shortcut.

   Production buckets must not expose the `r2.dev` development URL; any public published-image domain must use the recorded production custom domain and cache controls.

8. <a id="p14-r08"></a>**P14.R08 — Image derivative cache safety**<br>Image derivatives and immutable/versioned cache keys must prevent key collision, overwrite of an unrelated object, or stale content after replacement becomes active.

9. <a id="p14-r09"></a>**P14.R09 — Image replacement and cleanup order**<br>Replacement or removal must update application state before obsolete-object cleanup. A cleanup failure must not restore the removed image or leave the application pointing to a missing object.

10. <a id="p14-r10"></a>**P14.R10 — Private temporary originals**<br>Temporary originals must remain private. The venue-image contract must either require deletion immediately after successful processing or cite the exact recovery failure that requires short-term retention and assign a numeric expiry under Section 3.3. A retained original must remain restricted under Pass 14 requirement 7 and must be deleted when that expiry occurs; routine troubleshooting is not a reason for indefinite retention.

11. <a id="p14-r11"></a>**P14.R11 — Image reference discrepancy detection**<br>The system must detect missing referenced objects, published objects with no database reference, temporary uploads that remain after their deadline, failed derivatives, failed deletions, database/object status mismatches, and approach to the storage limit recorded under Section 3.3. Automatic repair may change only a case for which the current PostgreSQL and R2 states permit one correction defined by the child plan.

    **Fresh-state repair.** A scan result alone must not authorize repair: immediately before each repair mutation, the worker must re-read the affected PostgreSQL record and exact R2 object key and state, including confirmed absence where relevant. The repair must use the existing publication/replacement serialization or a conditional operation to ensure that the state on which the correction depends has not changed before the mutation, without retaining a database transaction across an R2 call contrary to Pass 05 requirement 23. If the state changed, cannot be checked, or cannot be kept valid through the mutation, leave it unchanged and create or retain the restricted discrepancy item; a begun R2 operation with an unknown result follows Pass 11 requirements 3–5 before any repeat.

    **Unresolved cases and manual correction.** Every other case must remain unchanged and create a restricted alert or operational-queue item identifying the discrepancy and affected database records and object keys. An active administrator must inspect the current states, select and record one permitted correction, and create the required audit record; this requirement does not require a storage-management interface.

   The existing Render Cron must initiate bounded, paginated consistency checks and hand durable repair or investigation work to the existing worker. Choose under Section 3.3 a maximum detection interval for each listed discrepancy, a batch and request/cost budget, and the storage-approach warning boundary. A known operation failure must create its discrepancy signal immediately; the recurring scan detects otherwise unknown drift. Use the existing storage-usage metric for approaching the limit and retain ambiguous cases for administrator choice.

12. <a id="p14-r12"></a>**P14.R12 — Missing image fallback**<br>A missing or unreadable venue image must show the configured default image. An administrator must be able to replace the broken image without direct bucket manipulation.

13. <a id="p14-r13"></a>**P14.R13 — Sanitized image master retention**<br>The venue-image contract must state whether it retains one sanitized master for derivative regeneration or regenerates nothing after publication. If retained, that master must meet Pass 14 requirements 6–8 and its access and retention treatment must be listed under Pass 16. R2 venue images are replaceable assets and do not require a zero-loss guarantee, but database references, cleanup state, default-image fallback, and the administrator replacement path must remain recoverable.

14. <a id="p14-r14"></a>**P14.R14 — Image lifecycle audit events**<br>Initiation, upload, publication, replacement, removal, failed validation, and administrative repair must create audit records without storing image bytes or signed URLs in the audit record.

#### Integration boundaries

- Numeric capability, file, image-processing, storage-warning, and lifecycle limits follow Section 3.3. Pass 14 requirements 3, 5–7, 10 and 11; Pass 19 requirement 8 must not complete until the selected production R2 account, bucket access model, image contract, processing environment, and expected storage use needed by that requirement are recorded.
- Pass 08 requirements 1–3, 6 and 7, 9 and 10; Pass 09 requirement 16 supply authorization and request-field protection. Pass 05 requirements 1–4, 8, 11 and 12; Pass 20 requirement 4 supply database constraints, staged transaction state, and unknown-outcome handling. Pass 11 requirements 1–7, 9–12 own durable processing and cleanup. Pass 09 requirements 8–14 own image audit records. Pass 01 requirements 3, 6; Pass 03 requirements 1–3; Pass 10 requirement 8; Pass 19 requirements 3 and 4 own R2 credentials and account access.
- Pass 16 assigns retention and deletion treatment to pending upload records, originals, sanitized masters, public derivatives, cleanup records, and audit references. Pass 18 owns alerts for the discrepancy and storage conditions emitted by Pass 14 requirement 11.
- The Pass 14 child plan must map every initiation, upload, confirmation, processing, publication, replacement, removal, cleanup, and repair step to its exact requirement and must not treat test-bucket behavior as evidence of a production-bucket setting.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p14-v01"></a>**P14.V01 — Image operation authorization**<br>For Pass 14 requirements 1 and 2, attempt image initiation, publication, replacement, and removal as a player, community host, inactive administrator, and active administrator. Attempt avatar upload through every player and administrator route. Record authorization, database state, R2 calls, jobs, and audit records. Confirm that only the active administrator can change a venue image and that avatars remain generated initials with no upload or preset path.

2. <a id="p14-v02"></a>**P14.V02 — Administrator upload capability**<br>For Pass 14 requirements 3–6, upload a valid file and files with a misleading filename, spoofed declared type, wrong file signature, malformed bytes, excessive received or encoded bytes, excessive dimensions or decoded pixels, decompression expansion, unsupported SVG or animation, metadata, and processing timeout. Exercise the authorized administrator's authenticated HTTPS upload-capability response and direct browser HTTPS `PUT` to the private bucket; attempt to obtain the capability without administrator authorization. Inspect the response's `no-store` directive, generated key, capability scope and expiry, pending state, browser request destination, measured properties, sanitized outputs, metadata, and publication state without retaining a usable signed URL in evidence. Confirm that only the authorized administrator obtains the capability, only the fully validated and re-encoded outputs publish, and no rejected or original input becomes public.

3. <a id="p14-v03"></a>**P14.V03 — Public and private image access**<br>For Pass 14 requirements 7 and 8, 10, 13, request every public derivative, private original, staging object, and retained sanitized master through public and authorized private paths. Replace an active image and inspect responses before and after cache expiry using its old and new versioned keys. Attempt an object-key collision and wait through each original-retention deadline. Confirm correct content types and disposition, private rejection, no unrelated overwrite or stale active image, and deletion of each expired original. Exercise every configured custom-domain, direct-bucket public path, and `r2.dev` path for both sanitized public images and deliberately marked private staging and original objects. Inspect browser-facing image addresses and private-read capability delivery; confirm that no signed private-read capability reaches the browser or serves a public derivative, published derivatives are accessible only as recorded through the public bucket and any configured public domain, private bytes never appear through an anonymous public endpoint, and production `r2.dev` requests do not serve bucket objects.

4. <a id="p14-v04"></a>**P14.V04 — Missing and abandoned image cases**<br>For Pass 14 requirements 9, 11 and 12, simulate a missing referenced object, unreferenced published object, abandoned upload, failed derivative, failed deletion, status mismatch, and storage-limit approach. Exercise each uniquely determined automatic repair and each case requiring administrator choice. Record the application state, default-image response, alert or queue item, selected correction, audit, cleanup retry, and replacement result. Confirm that cleanup failure does not restore a removed image, an ambiguous case is never changed automatically, and replacement requires no direct bucket manipulation.

   Run the existing Render Cron/worker scan across a bounded page for every listed discrepancy class and omit one Cron occurrence. Inspect detection interval, batch/request cost, immediate known-failure signal, and existing storage-usage warning; confirm each discrepancy is surfaced within its Section 3.3 deadline without unbounded scanning.

   Pause an automatic repair after its scan, then publish or replace the affected image or change the exact R2 object before the worker attempts repair. Inspect the fresh PostgreSQL and R2 reads, serialization or conditional check, repair intent, resulting references and object state, and discrepancy item. Confirm stale scan evidence never causes deletion or replacement of a newly current object, an unverifiable change remains unresolved, and an unknown R2 result is reconciled before repetition.

5. <a id="p14-v05"></a>**P14.V05 — Image audit record completeness**<br>For Pass 14 requirement 14, perform initiation, upload, successful and failed validation, publication, replacement, removal, and administrative repair. Inspect each audit record and confirm that it identifies the action and affected record without image bytes, signed capabilities, or reusable private object addresses.

#### Completion condition

Dedicated development R2 verifies admin upload, safe serving, and cleanup; deployed bucket isolation and final permissions are verified after staging/production configuration.

---

<a id="p15"></a>

### Pass 15 — Backup and Recovery Foundations

#### Purpose

Implement backup coverage, encrypted regional copies, restore materials, recovery design, and the protected deletion/hold-decision mechanism that deletion execution and safe restoration need.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p15-r01"></a>**P15.R01 — Backup data and configuration coverage**<br>Backup coverage must include PostgreSQL state and the configuration or metadata required to reconstruct the application. Stripe and Firebase records that Pickup Lane cannot back up directly must have documented reconciliation or reconstruction procedures.

2. <a id="p15-r02"></a>**P15.R02 — Replaceable venue-image recovery treatment**<br>The recovery-tier record must state whether venue images use the replaceable-asset tier. If they do, recovery must preserve or rebuild database references, detect each missing R2 object, show the configured default image, and permit active-administrator replacement without promising zero image loss. If they do not, the record must state the selected recovery method and objectives.

3. <a id="p15-r03"></a>**P15.R03 — Production PostgreSQL recovery design**<br>The final PostgreSQL recovery design must record backup schedule (initially test full compressed exports about every 30 minutes), whether each backup copy is a logical export, physical base backup, or provider snapshot, retention, encryption, credentials, Ohio-to-Oregon cross-region replication configuration, completion/replication/age monitoring, S3 Object Lock/legal-hold handling, restore dependencies, and the point-in-time recovery window (the range of restore instants available from a compatible physical base backup and archived PostgreSQL write-ahead log (WAL), or a documented provider-managed equivalent). A logical export alone cannot be used for WAL replay.

    It must measure the **newest verified restorable Oregon PostgreSQL backup copy (the most recent complete, replicated, validated copy that can be restored)**, including the recoverable source-state time, copy creation or export, upload and replication delays, validation, and time an actual isolated regional-loss restore takes.

    If full exports become too large or slow to meet objectives or the authorized cost, assess an incremental or managed alternative before signoff rather than weaken the one-hour/eight-hour requirements.

    Point-in-time recovery using a compatible physical base backup and archived WAL, or a documented provider-managed equivalent, is required when scheduled backup copies alone would lose more data than Pass 01 requirement 1 permits.

4. <a id="p15-r04"></a>**P15.R04 — Encrypted and restricted backups**<br>Backups must be encrypted during transfer and storage, readable only by the recorded backup and restore roles, monitored for missed or failed creation, and protected from alteration or deletion by the API, worker, migration, and ordinary human-administration credentials. The backup design must identify the separate credential or service control that enforces that protection. Production protection must cover independent Amazon S3 Ohio and Oregon objects with versioning, appropriately configured S3 Object Lock and retention, and separate write, replication, restore and administrative privileges. S3 Object Lock protects an object version from alteration or deletion before its recorded retention ends. Compliance-mode retention may be selected only after legal hold/deletion and expiry requirements are determined; provider privileges that can bypass governance retention must not be assigned to ordinary roles. A replicated object does not establish a recoverable point until its complete content and restore readiness are verified.

5. <a id="p15-r05"></a>**P15.R05 — Recovery material inventory**<br>Recovery material must include the non-secret application configuration, DNS records, Firebase, Stripe, R2, PostgreSQL-hosting, application-hosting, and monitoring/IRM settings, Postmark/PostHog bindings, both S3 regions and AWS account recovery settings, infrastructure definition, and procedures for recreating or reinjecting production secrets during a service rebuild. The recovery material must use secret inventory names and recovery steps and must not contain the secret values.

   Recovery material must identify the protected S3 decision-journal write/read path, ordering and completeness proof, region recovery check, credential and access restrictions, retention and erasure treatment, and procedure to replay decisions before traffic resumes. The exact mechanism, cost, legal/privacy treatment, and failure behavior remain decisions under Sections 3.3 and 3.5; an Ohio write alone does not prove Oregon recovery.

6. <a id="p15-r06"></a>**P15.R06 — Protected backup copy metadata**<br>Each backup copy must have a creation time, expiry or destruction event, and access restriction. Before a restored system can serve production traffic, it must reapply every completed deletion, investigation or dispute retention pause, and approved legal or security hold recorded after the backup point and verify the resulting treatment in each restored store.

   Record every deletion, investigation or dispute retention pause, and legal or security hold decision after a backup point in a minimal, independently recoverable journal in the already selected protected S3 backup environment. Each event needs a stable identity, verifiable order, and a completeness check. Before acknowledging the decision, confirm through a protected read-back that it is recoverable in the region required for restoration; an Ohio write alone cannot establish Oregon recoverability. A failed or uncertain journal result must not be acknowledged as protected. Before implementation, record and verify the protected write/read and acknowledgment method, region behavior, ordering and gap detection, retention and erasure treatment, access, cost, legal/privacy decision, and recovery replay. A gap or unknown decision blocks restored traffic under Pass 20 requirement 17; no new provider is authorized.

#### Integration boundaries

- Pass 01 requirement 1's recovery objectives require the recorded business and incident-impact inputs under Section 3.3 and must be chosen before Pass 01 requirement 4 selects and records the production architecture. Backup and transaction-log periods, exercise intervals, response deadlines, and notification deadlines require the recorded business, legal, service, and incident-impact inputs and evidence from the selected PostgreSQL, backup, and hosting systems under Section 3.3. Implementation and verification of the remaining recovery requirements depend on the architecture and named systems recorded by Pass 01 requirement 4. A temporary backup or restore design must not complete an affected requirement.
- Pass 05 requirements 13–15, 18–26; Pass 19 requirements 5 and 9–12; Pass 20 requirement 5 supply PostgreSQL connection exhaustion, migration, role, and hosting behavior. Pass 13 requirements 1–18, 20–24 supply financial reconciliation and duplicate-effect rules. Pass 19 requirement 7 supplies production Stripe account binding. Pass 11 requirements 1–12 supply restored-job and scheduler behavior. Pass 14 requirements 9–14; Pass 19 requirement 8 supply R2 reference, fallback, cleanup, and account behavior.
- Pass 09 requirements 1–7; Pass 18 requirements 1–6 supply detection, correlation, alerts, and dependency signals. Pass 09 requirements 8–15; Pass 16 requirement 8; Pass 18 requirements 7 and 8 supply audit integrity. Pass 02 requirements 2–5; Pass 04 requirement 1; Pass 15 requirement 6; Pass 16 requirements 1–7 supply deletion, pause, hold, and backup reapplication. Pass 01 requirements 2 and 3, 6; Pass 03 requirements 1–3; Pass 10 requirement 8; Pass 18 requirements 9–11; Pass 19 requirements 1–4 supply recovery access and secret recreation. Pass 01 requirement 4; Pass 06 requirement 1; Pass 19 requirements 5 and 6; Pass 20 requirements 6–16; Pass 21 requirements 1–3 supply the deployed architecture, rollback, capacity, release, and final service configuration.
- Pass 20 requirement 17 and Pass 15 requirement 6 are verified by the same isolated restore. Pass 20 requirement 18, Pass 13 verification scenarios 1–4, and Pass 11 verification scenarios 1–4 may share one duplicate-effect exercise when its evidence records every observation required by each referenced scenario; otherwise the missing observations require another exercise.

#### Verification

**Development-stage manual verification:** Before this pass completes, use the Pass 03 requirement 9 mapping and isolated development data to create and inspect a PostgreSQL backup copy and the protected decision journal in the dedicated development backup resources. Write a deletion, pause, or hold decision; verify the protected read-back in the region required by the selected recovery method; restore a copy predating that decision and replay it before permitting test traffic. Separately fail or make uncertain the journal write and required-region read-back and remove one ordered decision; confirm that no decision is acknowledged as protected and no restored traffic resumes while a gap or outcome remains unknown. Inspect backup access, encryption, retention, and the non-secret recovery instructions. This development evidence does not claim the final Ohio-to-Oregon data-loss and recovery objectives, which Pass 20 verification 36 and 51–55 exercise with the selected release and regional resources.

#### Completion condition

Backup and protected-journal mechanisms have passing development-stage backup, read-back, gap, and replay evidence with isolated data. Complete Ohio-to-Oregon restore objectives and failure recovery are demonstrated in Pass 20.

<a id="p16"></a>

### Pass 16 — Privacy, Retention, and Account Deletion

#### Purpose

Apply the approved privacy inventory, purpose and retention rules through account deletion, third-party processing, audit-retention treatment, data restoration safety, and user-facing privacy accuracy.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p16-r01"></a>**P16.R01 — Authenticated account deletion request**<br>An account-deletion request must verify the requesting user's current identity and recent authentication under Pass 10 requirement 1 or satisfy the current administrator requirements in Pass 08 requirement 3; Pass 09 requirement 16; Pass 10 requirement 7. Before reporting the request as accepted, it must record the deletion identity and request time, commit the local inactive state that blocks every protected Pickup Lane operation, including requests carrying a still-valid Firebase ID token, and commit durable work for every affected store in the inventory from Pass 02 requirement 3.

   **Acceptance and access gate.** Acceptance requires the local inactive state and durable per-store deletion work to commit together before the accepted response. From that point, every protected Pickup Lane backend operation must reject even a valid Firebase ID token; only necessary deletion-status, recovery, and support paths remain available.

   **Permitted exceptions.** Before implementing deletion, the Pass 16 child plan must list each existing operation retained as an exception, or state that none exists, and cite the exact requirement that needs it. For each retained operation, record the caller and how that caller is verified after local deactivation or Firebase disablement, allowed deletion states, exact response fields, permitted state changes, and required audit result. A path whose caller cannot be verified in that state must remain unavailable; an exception must not reactivate the account or permit ordinary protected operations. Any new route or workflow requires the Section 3.4 amendment rule.

   **Firebase result.** Firebase disablement or revocation must complete or enter the Pass 16 requirement 5 unknown-outcome reconciliation path. While that external result is pending, local access remains blocked and the response must not claim that Firebase sign-in has stopped.

2. <a id="p16-r02"></a>**P16.R02 — Deletion across data stores**<br>Deletion execution must attempt the recorded treatment for PostgreSQL, each Firebase account action Pickup Lane can perform, Cloudflare R2 objects, browser-facing cached content controlled by Pickup Lane, every named analytics and error-monitoring system, notification data, and each Stripe or other processor record that Pickup Lane is authorized to delete or anonymize. When a processor prohibits deletion, the inventory must identify the exact record type, service limitation, remaining fields and access, and the event that ends processor retention.

3. <a id="p16-r03"></a>**P16.R03 — Restricted retention for legal and financial needs**<br>Records required for payment, refund, dispute, fraud, a documented safety investigation, audit integrity, or an approved legal/security hold may be retained only under the recorded category, record scope, duration or release condition, and access restriction. Retention of those records must not preserve unrelated profile fields.

4. <a id="p16-r04"></a>**P16.R04 — Deletion integrity safeguards**<br>Deletion or anonymization must preserve the database links and financial fields required by the approved retained obligation while removing or irreversibly replacing profile fields that obligation does not require. Public, authenticated-user, administrator, support, list, search, export, and direct-object application routes must not reconstruct or return the deleted profile.

5. <a id="p16-r05"></a>**P16.R05 — Uncertain deletion outcome tracking**<br>A failed or outcome-unknown deletion step must remain in a restricted operational queue with the affected store and record category, last attempt identity and time, normalized failure class, external operation identity when one exists, and permitted recovery action. Before retrying an unknown Firebase or other processor mutation, recovery must read the service's authoritative state or use its idempotency mechanism. The deletion request must not be marked complete while any required store remains failed, unknown, or unfinished.

6. <a id="p16-r06"></a>**P16.R06 — Third-party processor inventory**<br>Each third-party processor must be named and inventoried with the data sent, purpose, deletion mechanism, and service-specific retention limitation. Data not required for the recorded purpose must not be sent.

7. <a id="p16-r07"></a>**P16.R07 — Privacy notice accuracy**<br>Before signoff, each statement in the privacy notice and each in-product privacy or deletion description must map to the implemented category, purpose, collection, processor disclosure, retention end date or event, and deletion result in the inventory. A statement that does not match implementation, or implemented collection not described by the privacy notice or in-product description that covers that category, must block release.

    The accurate production privacy notice must be available at a public address without sign-in. The assigned Pass 01 privacy responsibility must record which privacy-notice laws apply to the states served at launch, whether each law's business-coverage conditions are met, and the collection points and notice content each applicable law requires.

    Where an applicable law requires notice at or before collection, the required information must be available at the collection point before that personal information is collected, including on a public page when collection begins before sign-in; a link to the relevant notice section may serve this purpose only when the applicable law permits it. Collection without a required notice must block release.

    Before enabling product analytics, the assigned privacy responsibility must also record whether the actual browser collection and persistence require consent in each launch jurisdiction. Analytics collection and browser persistence must remain disabled until the applicable disclosures are available, any required consent has been obtained for that visitor, and the actual browser and processor configuration has been verified against the approved data inventory and event scope.

    Missing or withdrawn required consent must stop further analytics collection and persistence for that visitor; an intended configuration or test double must not be treated as proof of the production processor setting.

    Before nationwide launch, the assigned privacy responsibility must document which federal and state privacy laws govern the recorded launch scope: nationwide registration and community games, Illinois official games, adults-only accounts and participation, and the selected PostHog collection.

    For each comprehensive state consumer privacy statute and other federal or state privacy law potentially governing those operations, the determination must cite its current statutory provisions and effective date; state its jurisdictional, entity, business-size, revenue, consumer-count, sensitive-data, sale or sharing, and processing criteria or exclusions, including the statute's measurement periods and definitions; and compare each relevant criterion with a recorded launch fact. It must classify each law and affected processing activity as applicable now, conditional on a named future trigger, or outside scope under a cited unmet criterion or exclusion. A missing fact about current operations is unresolved and must not be treated as an exclusion.

    For each applicable law, the determination must identify its actual consumer-rights, request and response, notice, processor, consent, opt-out, assessment, and other launch duties and map each duty to an exact existing master requirement and verification scenario or require a justified amendment before implementation.

    The record must set a next review date and identify how changes in business size, revenue, consumer counts, served geography, data categories or processing, sale or sharing, provider use, or law will be detected. It must be reassessed when any recorded criterion changes and before approving a production change that would newly satisfy a trigger.

    Production signoff must remain blocked while a current applicability fact or an applicable launch duty lacks a recorded resolution and verified requirement. A verified unmet trigger or supported exclusion does not require speculative implementation of duties outside its scope.

8. <a id="p16-r08"></a>**P16.R08 — Audit retention and deletion treatment**<br>Audit records must use a retention category and a deletion operation that only the retention process can perform and that itself creates an audit record. If records move to archive storage, the retention record must name that destination and its access restriction. A documented investigation or dispute must pause scheduled deletion only for the identified records and duration. A legal or security hold applies only after an approved legal or security decision identifies its authority, record scope, accountable Pass 01 privacy or incident role, and release condition.

9. <a id="p16-r09"></a>**P16.R09 — Nevada privacy request treatment**<br>If the Pass 02 requirement 1 determination finds Pickup Lane covered by NRS 603A.300 through 603A.360, it must designate and monitor an email address, toll-free number, or website through which those consumers can submit the verified no-sale request defined by NRS 603A.325, 603A.337, and 603A.345; an existing monitored privacy email address may serve.

    A request verified by commercially reasonable means must receive a response within 60 days of receipt, or within at most 90 days if an extension is reasonably necessary and the consumer is notified of the extension.

    After receiving a verified request, Pickup Lane must make no sale, as defined with its exclusions by NRS 603A.333, of covered information collected or later collected about that consumer.

    If no such sale occurs, the response and subsequent processing must remain truthful without implying that a sale occurred.

    A missing designated address, response, or effective no-sale treatment must block signoff when the statute applies; no sale mechanism or separate request system is required by this requirement.

#### Integration boundaries

- Numeric retention, pause, processor, and backup periods follow Section 3.3 and must not be chosen until the named business, finance, legal, privacy, processor, and recovery inputs for that category exist. Work whose data model or deletion procedure could change with an undecided input must remain unstarted rather than implement a temporary production treatment.
- Pass 07 requirements 1, 3 and 4, 7 and 8, 11; Pass 10 requirement 1 supply user identity, account linkage, current account state, and recent authentication. Pass 08 requirements 1–3, 5–7, 9; Pass 09 requirement 16; Pass 10 requirement 7 supply administrator authorization and field protection. Pass 05 requirements 1–4, 8, 11 and 12; Pass 20 requirement 4 supply referential integrity, deletion relationships, and unknown database outcomes.
- Pass 11 requirements 1–10, 12 supply durable deletion and cleanup work. Pass 14 requirements 9–14; Pass 19 requirement 8 supply R2 object treatment. Pass 09 requirements 8–15; Pass 16 requirement 8; Pass 18 requirements 7 and 8 supply audit retention and records. Pass 01 requirement 1; Pass 15 requirements 1–5; Pass 20 requirements 17–19, 21 own backup restoration and jointly verify reapplication of deletion, pause, and hold decisions before traffic resumes.
- The Pass 16 child plan must cite the exact data category, store, processor, and upstream requirement for every treatment. Completion of an entire service pass does not prove that a category has reached its terminal result.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p16-v01"></a>**P16.V01 — Scheduled deletion timing**<br>For Pass 16 requirement 8, run scheduled deletion before, at, and after the assigned retention cutoff; move records to configured archive storage; pause deletion for one documented investigation or dispute; and, only if approved, exercise one legal or security hold through release. Inspect source and archive access, scoped records, dates, deletion audit, and unrelated records. Confirm only the retention process deletes, each deletion is audited, pauses and holds affect only their recorded scope and duration, and release restores the normal terminal treatment.

2. <a id="p16-v02"></a>**P16.V02 — Personal-data category and store trace**<br>For Pass 02 requirements 2–5; Pass 16 requirement 6, build the category-by-store inventory and trace one synthetic record from every classification and collection source through PostgreSQL, Firebase, Stripe or another processor, R2, notifications, caches, analytics, error monitoring, telemetry, audit, and backup stores that receive it. Inspect purpose, readers, copies, processor disclosure, retention start and end, terminal treatment, and backup handling. Confirm every store and category is classified and has one executable terminal result with no indefinite default or soft deletion as the final result.

3. <a id="p16-v03"></a>**P16.V03 — Authenticated deletion request cases**<br>For Pass 16 requirements 1 and 2, 4 and 5, request deletion as the user with current and stale authentication and as authorized and unauthorized administrators. For an accepted request, attempt every incompatible activity, execute every store step, force one local step and one processor step to fail, create an unknown Firebase or processor result, resume the work, and query every public, user, administrator, support, list, search, export, and direct-object path. Confirm accepted durable work for every store, blocked incompatible activity, authoritative checking before unknown-result retry, visible unfinished state, preserved approved financial or referential fields, no recoverable profile, and completion only after every required treatment reaches its terminal result.

   Inspect the pre-implementation exception list for deletion-status, recovery, and support operations. In each listed deletion state, attempt each exception as its authorized caller, a different user, an unauthorized administrator, and a caller whose Firebase identity is disabled or cannot be verified. Compare returned fields, permitted state changes, and audit results with the recorded contract; confirm an unverifiable caller is denied, no exception restores ordinary protected access, and an unlisted path remains unavailable.

4. <a id="p16-v04"></a>**P16.V04 — Retention and hold scenarios**<br>For Pass 02 requirement 4; Pass 16 requirements 3, 5, exercise each approved financial, refund, dispute, fraud, safety-investigation, audit-integrity, legal, and security retention case and one request with no approved basis. Inspect category, record scope, fields, readers, duration or release condition, queue state, and terminal treatment. Confirm approval only by the named roles, retention of only required records and fields, rejection of the unsupported case, and eventual deletion or anonymization after the obligation ends.

5. <a id="p16-v05"></a>**P16.V05 — Evidence invalidation after changes**<br>For Pass 02 requirements 3 and 4; Pass 15 requirement 6; Pass 16 requirements 3, 6, change one processor limitation, policy input, retention decision, backup treatment, or legal release condition. Identify the affected categories and evidence. Confirm only the affected decisions and checks become incomplete and that implementation and notices are updated before signoff.

**Development-stage manual verification:** For Pass 16 requirement 9, inspect the recorded Pass 02 requirement 1 Nevada applicability decision. When it establishes coverage, use synthetic consumers, the Section 3.2 development-stage build, the designated monitored request channel, and an isolated clock to submit an unverified and a verified no-sale request. Inspect the identity-verification decision, intake and monitoring record, receipt and response times, any necessity decision and consumer notice for an extension, and the resulting processor and application treatment of information already collected and later collected. Advance to the 60-day response boundary and, for a recorded necessary extension with notice, to the 90-day outer boundary. Confirm timely truthful responses, no implication that a sale occurred when none did, and no covered sale after the verified request; inspect the actual synthetic processing or applicable no-sale controls rather than treating a response alone as proof. When the recorded decision finds no coverage, record the unmet element or exclusion and its review trigger without claiming operational request handling. Pass 20 verification 38 repeats applicable request and processing checks for the release candidate.

#### Completion condition

Retention, deletion and third-party handling apply to the implemented stores and processors with passing Pass 16 verifications 1–5. The Pass 02 requirement 1 Nevada applicability decision is recorded; if it establishes coverage, Pass 16 requirement 9 has passing development-stage manual evidence for operational request and no-sale treatment, while a not-covered decision has its stated basis and review trigger. Full restored-backup replay, the applicable Pass 20 verification 38, and legal/privacy signoff are qualified later.

---

<a id="p17"></a>

### Pass 17 — Frontend and Browser Readiness

#### Purpose
