Complete the production web interface, published Terms of Service, shared browser API client, accessibility, frontend identity safety, browser third-party inventory, Content Security Policy, and application-facing App Check client behavior.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p17-r01"></a>**P17.R01 — Third-party browser code inventory**<br>Third-party browser code must be inventoried by purpose, accountable Pass 01 role, data received, contacted domains, loading method and scope, failure behavior, and removal procedure. A script may load only on pages that use its named production feature. Package dependencies must be fixed by committed lock data and covered by the Pass 03 dependency scan. An externally hosted static script must use Subresource Integrity, which makes the browser reject script bytes that do not match a pinned hash, when its publisher exposes an immutable file whose hash Pickup Lane can pin. A script whose contents change at one URL must be bundled or self-hosted.

2. <a id="p17-r02"></a>**P17.R02 — Browser destination controls**<br>After the Pass 17 requirement 1 browser-destination inventory is complete, the production `Content-Security-Policy`, which tells the browser where a page may load code and data from, must allow only the script, style, image, network, frame, and navigation destinations used by deployed Pickup Lane features. It must prohibit object execution with `object-src 'none'`, prevent an injected base element from changing relative URLs with `base-uri 'none'`, and prohibit other sites from framing Pickup Lane with `frame-ancestors 'none'`. The source list must be derived from the deployed frontend and that inventory. Application HTML must carry this policy. The recorded response policy must state the exact effective `Content-Security-Policy` for application HTML, static files, API responses, downloads, and redirects returning from Stripe or Firebase, including a recorded absence where that response has no CSP header. The final response received by the caller must match the policy without conflicting header fields. Adding or changing a browser destination requires updating the inventory and verifying the changed production policy before release.

3. <a id="p17-r03"></a>**P17.R03 — WCAG accessibility requirements**<br>Player and administrative interfaces must meet Web Content Accessibility Guidelines (WCAG) 2.2 Level AA for the flows included in production signoff.

4. <a id="p17-r04"></a>**P17.R04 — Automated and manual accessibility checks**<br>Verification must include automated checks and documented manual checks for keyboard use, visible focus, focus movement, dialog behavior, form labels and errors, status announcements, contrast, zoom and reflow, reduced motion, and at least one named desktop and one named mobile screen-reader/browser combination recorded in the accessibility plan. The plan must map each production-signoff flow to every applicable WCAG 2.2 Level A and Level AA success criterion, identify the test method and relevant browser or assistive technology, and record a justified nonapplicability decision for any criterion excluded from that flow's checks.

5. <a id="p17-r05"></a>**P17.R05 — Evidence before WCAG conformance claim**<br>Pickup Lane must not claim WCAG conformance before the verification above is complete and every failure in a production flow is corrected. Excluding a flow from the release requires a product decision that names the excluded flow, user impact, and release boundary.

6. <a id="p17-r06"></a>**P17.R06 — Supported browser versions**<br>Pickup Lane must support and test the current and previous major desktop versions of Chrome, Edge, Firefox, and Safari; the current and previous major iOS versions of Safari; and the current and previous major Android versions of Chrome. The release evidence must list the exact browser versions tested.

7. <a id="p17-r07"></a>**P17.R07 — Production flow loading behavior**<br>For every production flow, the child plan must identify which of these results it can produce and the exact displayed state and permitted next action for each: loading, empty, stale, offline or network failure, validation failure, authorization failure, deleted resource, conflict, still processing, rate limit, service unavailable, retryable failure, permanent failure, and unexpected failure. A loading or processing state must change to a completed, failed, or outcome-unknown state by the frontend deadline chosen under Section 3.3 and must provide the next action defined for that result.

8. <a id="p17-r08"></a>**P17.R08 — Ambiguous mutation user feedback**<br>A timed-out or ambiguous mutation must tell the user that the outcome is being checked or remains unknown. It must not invite a blind resubmission that can duplicate the operation.

9. <a id="p17-r09"></a>**P17.R09 — Browser account and network transitions**<br>After login, logout, account switch, network reconnect, retry, or restored browser session, the frontend must discard private data and permissions belonging to the prior identity or session, request the current authoritative state required by the visible flow, and replace or remove stale optimistic state before presenting the flow as current. Every authenticated player and administrator view must expose a visible, keyboard-accessible way to sign out without navigating to a different identity or losing access to sign-out during a loading or error state.

10. <a id="p17-r10"></a>**P17.R10 — Sensitive data in browser artifacts**<br>Client bundles, source maps, URLs, analytics, error telemetry, and browser storage outside the Firebase SDK-managed authentication persistence permitted by Pass 07 requirement 9 must not expose server secrets, private messages, moderation evidence, payment secrets, authentication tokens, presigned URLs, or personal data unrelated to the reported error. The sole presigned-URL exception is the Pass 14 requirement 3 signed `PUT` URL in the authorized administrator's authenticated HTTPS API response, temporary browser memory, and direct HTTPS request to the private R2 bucket; its presence in that network request and browser developer network inspection is expected. That URL must not enter page navigation or browser history, persistent application storage, HTML links or image markup, analytics, application logs, audit records, or error telemetry. No signed private-read capability may be returned to the browser. Application code must not make a separate browser-storage copy of Firebase tokens.

   Apply the disclosure rule by sink: release bundles and source maps contain no secret or personal data; navigation URLs and browser history contain no secret or personal data, subject only to the authorized R2 request destination in Pass 14 requirement 3; analytics receives only minimized approved events after the Pass 16 requirement 7 privacy gate; error telemetry receives safe error classes and correlation identifiers without private payloads; and application-managed persistent storage contains only nonpersonal preferences. Firebase SDK-managed authentication persistence remains governed by Pass 07 requirement 9. Any additional field requires the recorded Pass 16 privacy approval before collection.

11. <a id="p17-r11"></a>**P17.R11 — Non-public production source maps**<br>Production source maps must not be served from a public application address. They may be stored only in the configured error-monitoring or restricted artifact system and read only by the named accounts assigned to production error investigation under Pass 01.

12. <a id="p17-r12"></a>**P17.R12 — Production build performance budgets**<br>Performance budgets must be measured from the production build on the landing and browse flow, game-detail and booking or checkout flow, authenticated account and messaging flow, and administrative list, detail, and moderation flow included at launch. Section 3.3 must record the device, browser, network profile, cache state, data volume, measured events, numeric budgets, and which exceeded budgets block release. A blocking budget failure must stop release until the flow is corrected and measured again.

13. <a id="p17-r13"></a>**P17.R13 — Verified Vite production artifact**<br>Production must use a verified Vite production artifact, never a development or preview server. The build record must identify mode, environment, API base URL, public feature flags, base path, browser target, and release identity.

14. <a id="p17-r14"></a>**P17.R14 — Public build-time configuration**<br>Every `VITE_` or build-time value must be treated as public. The source, built bundle, and source maps must be checked for secrets, localhost targets, the wrong API origin, the wrong Firebase project or App Check app, the wrong Stripe mode or publishable key, and the wrong Cloudflare R2 public destination.

15. <a id="p17-r15"></a>**P17.R15 — Current Firebase token in API client**<br>The shared API client must obtain the current Firebase ID token at request time. When a caller correlation identifier is used, the client must generate it separately from the authoritative server-generated request identifier, must not replace that server identifier, and must retain the server identifier when returned in a response. The client must own timeout and cancellation, JSON and error parsing, empty responses, and the HTTP replay behavior assigned to the requested operation by Pass 18 requirement 18 and the pass that defines that operation's business effect. It must not repeat a mutation whose outcome is unknown unless that operation's requirement defines an idempotency or reconciliation rule that makes repetition safe. Pass 07 requirements 10, 13 continue to govern backend Firebase identity retries and unknown outcomes; they do not define a general frontend retry policy. Feature code must not create competing retry or authentication policies.

16. <a id="p17-r16"></a>**P17.R16 — Client effect and request cleanup**<br>Effects, subscriptions, and requests must clean up on identity, route, or component change. Obsolete results must be aborted or ignored, cache keys must include identity when data is private, and optimistic changes must reconcile with the backend.

17. <a id="p17-r17"></a>**P17.R17 — Server-enforced authorization**<br>Client route guards and hidden controls may guide navigation only. Direct refresh, unknown route, changed role, suspension, deletion, and a redirect destination outside the configured application-origin allowlist must fail visibly while the backend remains authoritative.

18. <a id="p17-r18"></a>**P17.R18 — Duplicate form submission prevention**<br>A form must disable or otherwise reject a second submission while the first identical mutation remains active, preserve accessible field and form errors after failure, and define the result of navigating away with unsaved changes. Date, time, and file controls must display and validate the format, time zone, type, and limits required by their backend contract. A hidden or disabled field must never serve as authorization evidence. Every Pickup Lane password-entry field must mask entered characters by default, permit pasting and browser or external password-manager autofill, and send the entered password unchanged to the supported Firebase authentication flow; the interface must not require a user to retype a password or apply a client-side character transformation.

19. <a id="p17-r19"></a>**P17.R19 — Escaped untrusted text**<br>Untrusted text must render as escaped text. Rich-text and HTML input are prohibited. Scriptable URL schemes, direct insertion of unsanitized HTML, and redirects to an origin outside the configured application-origin allowlist are prohibited. An external HTTP or HTTPS link opened in a new browsing context must prevent the opened page from controlling the Pickup Lane page. Any application-defined `postMessage` receiver must reject untrusted origins and malformed message payloads before a state change. Application code must not use untrusted values as executable JavaScript or as keys that alter object prototypes through unsafe dynamic merging; a source path with no such operation must be recorded as absent rather than adding a feature.

20. <a id="p17-r20"></a>**P17.R20 — Browser dependency failure handling**<br>Failure of Firebase, Stripe browser code, the named analytics or error-monitoring system, or a third-party script inventoried under Pass 17 requirement 1 must not expose secrets, corrupt application state, duplicate a mutation, or break a page that does not use that integration. Each inventoried integration must have a tested unavailable/degraded state.

21. <a id="p17-r21"></a>**P17.R21 — Shared frontend App Check client**<br>The frontend must obtain App Check proof through one shared client and attach it only to Pickup Lane API requests. It must not persist the proof, send it to arbitrary absolute URLs or direct Stripe or Cloudflare R2 requests, or replay a mutation because proof acquisition failed.

   The shared client must use the same versioned route classification as the backend, including classified public reads, and must not attach App Check proof to an exempt non-browser route or direct R2 upload.

22. <a id="p17-r22"></a>**P17.R22 — Public Terms presentation**<br>The production frontend must implement public presentation of the final, versioned Terms of Service approved under Pass 02 requirement 6 without sign-in and make it reachable before account activation, game hosting or joining, and official-game booking or payment. The text and incorporated policies in the release build must match actual signup, hosting, game, booking, payment, refund, and dispute behavior and the disclosures required by Pass 13 requirement 19. Pass 21 requirement 4 owns publication at the deployed public production address before final production acceptance.

    The release build must not present a placeholder or superseded document as current or permit an account or payment operation when its required text is unavailable. Each version must remain identifiable and reproducible for the acceptance evidence required by Pass 07 requirement 14. A revision must receive the review required by Pass 02 requirement 6 before publication.

#### Integration boundaries

- Backend security and authorization cannot be replaced by frontend hiding or validation.
- Pass 17 requirement 1 supplies the browser-destination inventory required by Pass 17 requirement 2 and does not depend on completion of that requirement. Pass 06 requirements 2, 4–7, 9–12, 15; Pass 17 requirement 2; Pass 18 requirement 18 supply HTTP, repeated-request, cache, contract, response, model, and timeout behavior. Pass 07 requirements 1, 7, 10 and 11, 13; Pass 10 requirement 2 supply token, backend Firebase retry, identity, App Check, and unknown-Firebase-outcome behavior. Pass 08 requirements 1–3, 5–10; Pass 09 requirement 16 supply authorization and concealed-resource behavior.
- Pass 13 requirements 1, 3 and 4, 7–11, 14–17, 20 and 21, 23 supply the booking and financial inputs and outcomes displayed by checkout, account, and administrative flows. Pass 14 requirements 1–9, 12 supply venue-image authorization, input, publication, replacement, removal, and fallback behavior. The frontend must represent those backend states without inventing a competing business result.
- Pass 02 requirement 6 owns reviewed contractual content. Pass 17 requirement 22 owns public presentation in the release build and consistency with implemented behavior; Pass 21 requirement 4 owns publication at the production address. Pass 07 requirement 14 owns acceptance evidence and backend enforcement; Pass 13 requirement 19 owns consistency and availability at payment confirmation. Pass 16 requirement 7 continues to own the privacy notice and privacy-law disclosures.
- Pass 03 consumes every verification scenario and release gate defined here. Pass 20 consumes the production artifact, performance, environment, and supported-client results from Pass 17 requirements 6, 12–14, 20.
- Pass 17 requirement 12 and the deadlines in Pass 17 requirements 7 and 8, 15 must remain incomplete until their Section 3.3 inputs and the production deployment and supported-client facts that can change them are recorded.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p17-v01"></a>**P17.V01 — Browser session after account changes**<br>For Pass 07 requirements 9, 11, log out, switch users, disable an account, revoke access, and change a role while private views, cached data, and requests are active; then restore the browser session after logout and after the switch. Exercise the recorded player and administrator inactivity and absolute limits just before and after each boundary, including tab restoration, device wake, and silent ID-token refresh; after local logout and inactivity sign-out, separately test a previously issued ID token and record its remaining Firebase validity without claiming account-wide revocation. Revoke refresh tokens after a synthetic compromise and send both the old ID token and a refreshed token to the next protected request. Record the Firebase SDK's current user and persistence state, verified authentication time, application-managed browser storage, displayed data, the next protected backend result, and PostgreSQL state. Confirm that inactivity signs out the browser, an exceeded absolute age blocks protected access despite token refresh, revocation rejects the old identity on the next protected request, and the prior SDK session is signed out or replaced, prior-user application credentials and private data are cleared before the next account appears, session restoration does not regain the prior user's private data, and stale tokens or browser state do not preserve revoked authority. With the account otherwise active, record whether an independently copied ID token remains usable after browser inactivity sign-out and confirm that expiry, the applicable backend authentication-age limit, or revocation rejects it when reached; do not describe inactivity sign-out as server-side inactivity enforcement.

2. <a id="p17-v02"></a>**P17.V02 — Accessibility methods and observations**<br>For Pass 17 requirements 3–5, complete the automated and manual accessibility checks for every production-signoff flow. For each flow and applicable WCAG 2.2 Level A or Level AA success criterion, record applicability, test method, relevant browser, viewport, keyboard path, input method and assistive technology, expected and observed result, and correction and retest where needed; record the reason when a criterion does not apply. Confirm WCAG 2.2 Level AA only after every applicable included-flow criterion passes and all failures are corrected. Confirm that every excluded flow has the required product decision and release boundary; a flow exclusion does not turn an applicable criterion into a passing result.

3. <a id="p17-v03"></a>**P17.V03 — Supported browser flow checks**<br>For Pass 17 requirements 6–9, run every production flow on every browser version required by Pass 17 requirement 6. Produce every result listed in Pass 17 requirement 7 from a real backend response or controlled network failure, wait through the frontend deadline, and exercise login, logout, account switch, reconnect, retry, and session restoration. On every protected player and administrator view, verify that a keyboard user can find and use logout even while an unrelated request is failing. Record the exact browser version, displayed state, available next action, backend and frontend identity, cache contents, and final authoritative state. Confirm no indefinite spinner, no blind repeat of an unknown mutation, and no data or permission retained from the prior identity.

4. <a id="p17-v04"></a>**P17.V04 — Release bundle content inspection**<br>For Pass 17 requirements 10 and 11, 13 and 14, build the release artifact and inspect source files, output bundles, source maps, Firebase SDK-managed persistence, application-managed browser storage, URLs, network requests, analytics, error telemetry, build metadata, API address, feature flags, Firebase and App Check binding, Stripe mode and key, and R2 destination. Exercise the authorized administrator upload and inspect the authenticated HTTPS capability response, temporary browser use, direct private-bucket `PUT` request, page navigation and history, persistent application storage, HTML links and image markup, analytics, application logs, audit records, and error telemetry without retaining a usable signed URL in evidence. Attempt public source-map access and access through an unauthorized account. Confirm that the signed upload URL appears only in the permitted response, temporary browser memory, and direct R2 network request, that no signed private-read capability reaches the browser, and that no prohibited location retains or discloses the upload URL; its visibility in browser developer network inspection is expected. Confirm the production build uses the recorded environment and release identity, Firebase authentication persists only through the permitted SDK path, application code stores no separate token copy, and tokens travel only in Firebase SDK authentication exchanges or the permitted Pickup Lane API `Authorization` header. Confirm other prohibited data, localhost or wrong-environment destinations, and public source maps are absent from the inspected outputs.

   For each sink in Pass 17 requirement 10, inspect an authorized upload, an error, an analytics event before and after the privacy gate, and browser persistence. Confirm the permitted signed R2 `PUT` destination appears only in the approved transient network flow, and every other sink satisfies its own restricted field set.

5. <a id="p17-v05"></a>**P17.V05 — Cross-window message origin validation**<br>For Pass 17 requirements 1, 19 and 20, inventory every third-party browser package and remote script, contacted domain, received data, loading page, integrity or bundling treatment, failure behavior, and removal procedure. For any application-defined cross-window message receiver, submit trusted and untrusted origins and malformed payloads and confirm that only the defined sender and validated payload affect application state. Inspect frontend source for evaluation of untrusted expressions and untrusted object merging and, where a sink exists, exercise a controlled unsafe expression or prototype key and confirm that it cannot execute code or modify an object prototype. Render script markup, rich-text or HTML input, and each allowed and prohibited URL form as untrusted content; open an allowed external link; and make Firebase, Stripe browser code, analytics, error monitoring, and each inventoried script unavailable. Confirm escaped rendering, rejection of prohibited input and scriptable or unlisted destinations, protection of the opener page, no integration on an unrelated page, and no secret exposure, corrupted state, duplicate mutation, or unrelated-page failure.

6. <a id="p17-v06"></a>**P17.V06 — Production flow performance measurement**<br>For Pass 17 requirement 12, measure each named flow from the production build with every recorded device, browser, network, cache, and data-volume profile. Record each measured event and result. Confirm that a result above a blocking budget fails the release check and that the corrected build is measured again before the gate passes.

7. <a id="p17-v07"></a>**P17.V07 — Token change and malformed response cases**<br>For Pass 17 requirements 15–18, exercise token change during a request, empty and malformed responses, request timeout and cancellation, route and component change, late response, optimistic conflict, direct protected deep link, unknown route, changed role, suspension, deletion, redirects inside and outside the configured application-origin allowlist, double submission, field and form errors, navigation with unsaved changes, and invalid date, time, and file input. Inspect requests, request identifiers, displayed state, private cache keys, backend state, and mutation count. Confirm one shared API policy, cleanup or rejection of obsolete results, backend authority, one mutation, accessible errors, and exact handling of every form and navigation case. For each password-entry field present in the released Pickup Lane email/password registration, sign-in, recovery, and password-change flows, verify default masking, paste, and browser/password-manager autofill with synthetic credentials; confirm successful supported input reaches Firebase without client-side modification and do not retain the password in test evidence.

#### Completion condition

Browser destinations are inventoried, the effective CSP policy is defined against the completed frontend, and all implemented browser flows are represented, accessible, and development-tested. Pass 20 verifies the release build through the final public path.

---

<a id="p18"></a>

### Pass 18 — Operational Monitoring and Incident Readiness

#### Purpose

Complete the operational monitoring contract, actionable Grafana alerts and escalation, release-blocking indicators, incident procedures, access/secret response, service failure runbooks, and protection against abusive public traffic.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p18-r01"></a>**P18.R01 — Release-blocking production indicators**<br>Each production indicator that can block or stop a release must have a numeric threshold, evaluation period, accountable role from Pass 01 requirement 2, notification destination, and step-by-step response procedure chosen under Section 3.3. The indicator must remain non-blocking and must not be represented as configured until all five fields and its data source are recorded and verified.

2. <a id="p18-r02"></a>**P18.R02 — Actionable human alerts**<br>Alerts must represent a condition that requires human action or confirms an automated protective action. Repeated alerts for one unresolved condition must be grouped or deduplicated.

3. <a id="p18-r03"></a>**P18.R03 — Unresolved business-state monitoring**<br>Each payment, refund, booking, job, deletion, and reconciliation state with a processing deadline must name either an alert or a restricted operational queue that receives it when the deadline expires. That destination must display the operation type, current state, deadline, age, authorized next action, and either the internal Pickup Lane record identifier or a non-secret alias defined by the telemetry contract in Pass 09 requirement 5. It must not display a raw Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, or other external-service identifier or another personal value. The item must remain visible until the condition resolves.

4. <a id="p18-r04"></a>**P18.R04 — Operational dashboard coverage**<br>Dashboards must distinguish user-facing availability, speed, correctness, payment health, job delay, and data freshness. A single aggregate health value must not hide a failure in one of those areas.

5. <a id="p18-r05"></a>**P18.R05 — PostgreSQL and service health monitoring**<br>Monitoring must also cover PostgreSQL query, lock, and storage health; Firebase authentication failures; application deployment health; backup completion; R2 reference divergence; expired job claims; and approach to recorded Firebase, Stripe, R2, PostgreSQL-hosting, and application-hosting limits.

    For Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL hosting, application hosting, DNS or CDN, repository or CI, monitoring, and backup storage, the monitoring record must list each outage, degradation, capacity or quota approach, billing or spending risk, credential or access change, security event, data-loss condition, and backup failure named by this master that can affect Pickup Lane. Each listed condition must have a detection method.

    Use a provider notification when the provider documents that it reports the condition and a delivery test reaches the recorded destination. Use an application metric, health check, failed operation, reconciliation result, or scheduled verification when the provider offers no notification or its notification does not cover the complete condition. A second detection method is required only when the first method leaves a recorded detection gap or when it supplies the required backup delivery route.

    Monitoring must detect repeated API authentication and authorization failures, concealed private-resource probes, and security-sensitive validation or signature failures by route or operation and outcome; each detection must name its threshold and evaluation period under Section 3.3, notification destination, and response. Detection must use bounded, non-personal signals, must not copy attempted identifiers, credentials, or request content into telemetry, and must not itself reject a legitimate request.

    For each security-critical production setting in Firebase authentication and App Check, Stripe webhook delivery, R2 access, PostgreSQL network and roles, public TLS and host routing, repository and CI gates, secret access, backup protection, and logging or alert delivery that can change outside the repository, the monitoring record must identify its approved value under Section 3.5 and either a provider notification that reports that setting change or comparison of the live value with the approved value at the existing Pass 19 requirement 2 access-review interval.

    If the Pass 01 review interval would leave a prohibited effect undetected beyond its recorded response deadline, the setting must instead use a provider change notification or an existing monitoring signal that meets that deadline.

    An unapproved deviation must reach the accountable Pass 01 role and remain visible until corrected or the governing pass confirms that the new value still satisfies its requirement; changing the approved record or a repository value alone must not close it.

6. <a id="p18-r06"></a>**P18.R06 — Alert delivery and escalation**<br>Every alert path must define and exercise initial delivery, escalation when it is not acknowledged within the recorded time, recovery notification, grouping of repeated signals for one condition, and planned-maintenance suppression. A suppression must contain an automatic expiry time or a release or maintenance event that removes it; an event-based removal must also have a latest calendar time under Section 3.3.

7. <a id="p18-r07"></a>**P18.R07 — Time-critical incident access**<br>Before signoff, privilege escalation, administrative account recovery, financial correction, production secret change, and backup restore must each be assigned to either an immediate alert or a restricted operational review queue. The assignment must identify the accountable role from Pass 01 requirement 2, numeric maximum review delay chosen under Section 3.3, and exact response steps.

8. <a id="p18-r08"></a>**P18.R08 — Audit failure monitoring**<br>A failed or outcome-unknown audit write, failed scheduled audit deletion, unauthorized audit-access attempt, and failed archive transfer must each create the alert or restricted operational-queue item named in the audit operations contract. Detection and routing of an audit-write failure must not rely solely on the audit write that failed.

    The item must identify the event type, the immutable audit-record ID when one exists or the operation ID allowed by the audit operations contract, failure code, first and latest failure times, and required response without copying the protected audit content.

    After audit storage recovers, the application must create a failure record linked to the operation ID and independent signal when that signal contains the operation ID, failure code, first failure time, and internal request identifier when one exists or the provider-console operation ID needed to identify the failed write.

    When any of those fields is missing, the queue item must remain unresolved until an authorized investigation establishes them. The later record documents the failure and must not claim that the original required audit write succeeded.

   An external mutation whose post-action audit write fails or remains unknown must use this independent signal, retain its operation ID and provider evidence reference, and remain unresolved until the provider result and audit record are reconciled. A later failure record must not claim that the earlier provider action was rolled back or that a missing pre-action intent was written.

9. <a id="p18-r09"></a>**P18.R09 — Secret inventory ownership**<br>Each secret inventory entry must identify the accountable Pass 01 role, every process and service that consumes it, the exact replacement and rollout sequence, revocation method, old-credential disablement check, and recovery path. Executing that sequence must replace the secret without an unplanned production outage and must confirm that every intended consumer uses the new value before the old value is disabled.

   Each planned provider-console rotation must use a durable Pass 09 operation ID and pre-action audit intent, then link the provider event and confirmed outcome. If the post-action audit write fails or provider result is unknown, follow Pass 09 requirement 11; Pass 18 requirement 8 before retry or closure.

10. <a id="p18-r10"></a>**P18.R10 — Exposed secret response**<br>When a secret appears in source history, an artifact, log, browser output, unauthorized account, public location, or an alert that identifies possible disclosure, it must be treated as exposed. Response must revoke or rotate it, inspect logs and audit records for use, inspect every Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL, hosting, DNS, CI, monitoring, or backup system that accepted it, record the affected period and findings, and confirm that the old credential no longer works.

   Provider-console compromise response must retain the Pass 09 operation ID, durable intent when available, provider evidence, and outcome. An unavoidable emergency during audit-store failure uses the independent incident evidence and later reconciliation required by Pass 09; do not claim a completed provider effect was rolled back.

11. <a id="p18-r11"></a>**P18.R11 — Expiring emergency access**<br>Emergency access must have an expiry recorded when granted, be logged and audited, and be removed when the emergency ends or the expiry occurs, whichever happens first. Its existence must not bypass ordinary named-account requirements.

   Emergency provider access follows the Pass 09 requirement 8 operator procedure when audit storage is available. If immediate containment cannot wait for that store, preserve independent provider and incident evidence, link it promptly after recovery, and leave the action unresolved until audited; do not fabricate pre-action intent.

12. <a id="p18-r12"></a>**P18.R12 — Incident class procedures**<br>Each security, privacy, payment, data-integrity, production-outage, and runbook incident class listed in Pass 18 requirement 16 must have a named incident lead role, technical response role, communication role, backup or escalation holder, severity criteria, notification route, containment steps, recovery steps, and closure criteria. For each time-critical scenario, the backup holder must be capable of performing the cited containment, reconciliation, restore, or rollback step without the primary operator; mere provider account recovery or receiving an alert does not satisfy the response contract. Where provider support cannot execute that step, record the competent alternate and its tested availability within the assigned objective, or leave that objective unverified.

   External containment and recovery actions must follow Pass 09 requirement 11 for durable pre-dispatch intent, post-action outcome, and unknown-result reconciliation, except the unavoidable emergency evidence path in Pass 09 requirement 8. A completed provider mutation cannot be treated as rolled back by a local audit failure.

13. <a id="p18-r13"></a>**P18.R13 — Incident timeline and decisions**<br>Incident records must preserve the timeline, affected systems and users, decisions, evidence, containment, recovery validation, communications, and assigned corrective work.

14. <a id="p18-r14"></a>**P18.R14 — Incident record completeness**<br>Every security, privacy, payment, or data-integrity incident and every outage that exceeds its approved response or recovery objective must follow the notification decision procedure recorded for its class. That procedure must identify the facts to collect, the internal roles and external legal, processor, insurer, customer, or authority contacts that must be considered, each decision deadline, and the person authorized to decide. The decision, supporting facts, decision time, and any notice time must be recorded even when the result is no external notification.

15. <a id="p18-r15"></a>**P18.R15 — External-service failure runbooks**<br>For every external-service condition mapped by Pass 18 requirement 5, the incident contract must identify the application behavior while the condition continues, containment behavior for an account or credential compromise, required response procedure, primary escalation route, and backup route. It must use the detection and delivery path verified by Pass 18 instead of creating another alert for the same condition unless Pass 18 records the additional path as closing a detection gap or supplying the required backup route. A provider, account, architecture, or notification-capability change makes the affected detection and response mapping incomplete until it is reviewed and verified again.

16. <a id="p18-r16"></a>**P18.R16 — Operational incident runbooks**<br>Runbooks must cover API/database outage, connection exhaustion, failed deployment or migration, worker backlog or terminally failed jobs, Stripe webhook or payment mismatch, R2 upload or credential failure, Firebase outage, secret compromise, certificate expiry, backup failure, restore, user-deletion failure, and recovery of domain, DNS, and external-service administrative access.

17. <a id="p18-r17"></a>**P18.R17 — Incident closure and corrective work**<br>Incident closure must record cause, detection source and delay, response timeline, affected users and data, preserved evidence, containment and recovery result, communications, each corrective action's accountable role and deadline, and the observed verification that each completed correction prevents or detects recurrence. The incident must remain open while a closure criterion in Pass 18 requirement 12 is unmet.

18. <a id="p18-r18"></a>**P18.R18 — Repeated-request effects and owners**<br>Before the following workflows receive production traffic, the cited requirements must determine whether repeated requests can reveal whether an account exists, send repeated user-visible content, consume paid or limited external-service capacity, exhaust Pickup Lane capacity, or repeat a business effect.

    **Operation-family ownership:**

    | Operation family | Controlling requirements |
    |---|---|
    | Account creation, account recovery, and Firebase identity requests | Pass 07 requirements 4, 10, 12, and 13 |
    | Private-message sending; existing administrative list, search, and export requests | Pass 18 requirement 18 |
    | Booking, payment, refund, credit, and Stripe requests | Pass 13 requirements 4, 6, 9, 10, 18, and 24 |
    | Administrative image-upload and Cloudflare R2 requests | Pass 14 requirements 3, 4, 8, 9, and 11 |

    **Required control records and verification.** This assignment does not authorize a new export or other endpoint. For each existing operation with one of the listed risks, the cited pass must identify the control that prevents the effect, the rejection or replay result, the recorded monitoring signal, and every effect the control must prevent. A control that counts requests must also identify what distinguishes the caller or protected resource, the permitted count and time period, behavior when several application processes receive the requests, and the retry response. A control based on authentication, a unique database record, an idempotency key (one business-attempt identifier whose exact replay cannot create a second effect and whose reuse with different input conflicts), or current business state must identify the exact repeated input and the unchanged result returned after repetition. Pass 06 may provide one shared HTTP control only when two or more endpoint families or cited operations require the same enforcement behavior. Repeated-request verification must prove that the selected control prevents every listed effect; a second request limiter must not be added when an existing control already proves that result.

    **All-endpoint traffic policy.** Pass 06 must also account for every released public and authenticated API endpoint family, including ordinary game browsing and read-only list, detail, and search requests. For each family, the traffic policy must record the expected legitimate peak, the API, PostgreSQL, and external-service work caused by repeated requests, the capacity or quota at risk, the control that keeps excessive traffic from exhausting it, its enforcement point and response, the monitoring signal, and the effects it must prevent. A rejected request must stop before the API, PostgreSQL, or external-service work that the control protects. Families may use different controls and limits according to measured cost, risk, and legitimate use; an existing control counts only if verification proves it also bounds the repeated work at risk.

    **Both Render instances and control failure.** A request-count control must use the Section 3.3 decision and enforcement details above and must remain effective across both production Render API instances, including when requests alternate between them. The policy must record how requests are handled when a required control is unavailable; that path must not silently admit unbounded work.

    **Preserving required service traffic.** Health checks must keep their Pass 06 requirement 8 meaning, and permitted browser preflights validated without App Check proof under Pass 06 requirement 10 and Pass 10 requirements 4 and 5, valid Stripe webhooks, and other required service requests must remain deliverable at their recorded peak and retry volume even when unrelated callers exceed their limits; the policy must state how those requests are classified and protected without allowing unauthenticated traffic to exhaust the capacity they need.

#### Integration boundaries

- Administrative accountability records belong to Pass 09 and must not be replaced by operational logs.
- Pass 06 requirements 2, 5, 8, 12, 15 supply caller error fields and prohibited content, cache behavior, retry responses, health state, and timeouts. Pass 13 requirements 7, 9, 11, 16, 18 supply financial and reconciliation states. Pass 11 requirements 2, 5 and 6, 9 supply job identities, deadlines, failures, and measurements. Pass 14 requirement 11 supplies image and R2 discrepancy states.
- Pass 09 owns attributable administrative audit records; operational logs may link only to the immutable audit-record or operation identifier permitted by Pass 09 requirement 10; Pass 18 requirement 8 and must not replace or copy protected audit detail. Pass 16 owns telemetry and artifact retention. Pass 01 requirement 2 supplies accountable operational roles; Pass 03 requirements 1 and 3 govern the monitoring secret and permission inventories; Pass 19 requirements 1 through 4 govern production monitoring access grants, reviews, named accounts, MFA, and workload credentials.
- Pass 01 requirement 4; Pass 19 requirements 5 and 6; Pass 20 requirements 6, 12–15; Pass 21 requirements 1 and 2 supply the final release, capacity, hosting, environment, and process facts used by Pass 09 requirements 2, 4, 6; Pass 18 requirements 1, 5 here. Those final signals and Section 3.3 thresholds must remain incomplete until the named production systems and measured inputs exist.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p18-v01"></a>**P18.V01 — Incident response delivery**<br>For Pass 18 requirement 10, place a controlled credential marker in each named exposure location and trigger the response. Record revocation or rotation time, systems inspected, affected period, log and audit findings, consumer recovery, and old-credential attempts. Confirm the old credential fails everywhere that accepted it and that deleting the marker alone does not close the response.

#### Completion condition

Signals, deadlines, escalation, incident ownership and procedures are configured. Final alert delivery, fault coverage and incident exercises are qualified in staging.

---

<a id="p19"></a>

### Pass 19 — Staging and Production-Equivalent Configuration

#### Purpose

Activate and bind isolated staging services only when development warrants it; establish the distinct production control-plane resources, provider settings, account bindings, limits, credentials, and configuration decisions needed before deployment. Inspect those production settings where this pass requires it without treating an undeployed application or its public responses as verified.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p19-r01"></a>**P19.R01 — Production access grant records**<br>A human production-access grant or emergency elevation must record the requester, the named person assigned the affected responsibility in Pass 01 requirement 2 as approver, recipient, exact account, role, and resource scope, start time, expiry for temporary access, and completion result. Revocation and role reduction must record the actor, recipient, removed scope, and result. The initial account bootstrap must record the actor, created account, granted scope, time, result, and tested recovery route.

   A planned production provider-console grant or elevation must use the Pass 09 requirement 8 restricted operator procedure: durable operation ID and audit intent before action, provider evidence and result afterward. An unavoidable emergency with unavailable audit storage preserves independent incident/provider evidence for prompt reconciliation and cannot be marked audited by a nonexistent pre-action record.

2. <a id="p19-r02"></a>**P19.R02 — Production access review**<br>Access must be reviewed before production signoff, after every personnel or responsibility change, after every access or credential incident, and at the numeric recurring interval chosen under Section 3.3. The same decision must set the maximum elapsed time from departure or reassignment to removal of the affected access. Each review must compare current accounts, roles, group membership, keys, and recovery methods with Pass 01 requirement 2; Pass 03 requirements 1–3; Pass 10 requirement 8; Pass 19 requirements 1, 3 and 4 and record every removal or correction.

3. <a id="p19-r03"></a>**P19.R03 — Restricted provider administration**<br>Administrative access to the domain registrar, DNS/CDN, frontend and API hosting, PostgreSQL, Firebase/Google Cloud, Stripe, Cloudflare/R2, source repository, CI, monitoring, and backups must use named accounts, MFA, least privilege, recovery ownership, and offboarding. The corresponding service settings must identify which named account or role may change them.

4. <a id="p19-r04"></a>**P19.R04 — Production account access boundaries**<br>After the final hosting and cloud accounts are selected, each CI and deployed-workload operation must use a provider-issued short-lived credential when the selected service supports one for that operation, including managed identity, workload-identity federation (exchanging an approved workload identity for a provider-issued short-lived credential without storing a private key), or short-lived OpenID Connect credentials. Application-default credentials (the provider SDK’s environment-based credential lookup) satisfy this requirement only when inspection confirms that they resolve to a provider-issued short-lived credential without loading a stored private key. Application-default credentials that load a stored private key count as a long-lived key. A long-lived key may be used only when the selected account does not support a short-lived method for the operation and the secret inventory records its least-privilege scope, access-restricted storage, rotation method, use monitoring, emergency revocation procedure, and evidence that the short-lived method is unsupported.

5. <a id="p19-r05"></a>**P19.R05 — Production process supervisor settings**<br>The production process command and supervisor must disable reload and define API worker count, per-worker concurrency, keep-alive, recycling, graceful-shutdown deadline, instance count, and restart behavior under Section 3.3. After Pass 01 requirement 4 records the selected PostgreSQL service and Section 3.3 records the proposed maximum API, worker, and scheduler instance counts, per-process concurrency, autoscaling ceiling, and rolling-deployment overlap, calculate the largest number of simultaneous PostgreSQL connections the deployment can open using Pass 05 requirement 13. The calculation must include each process's configured set of reusable open connections, permitted additional connections above that set, background workers, migrations, monitoring, backups, administrative maintenance, and simultaneous current and incoming instances. The maximum connection count plus a numeric emergency and maintenance reserve chosen under Section 3.3 must not exceed the selected PostgreSQL service's enforced connection limit. The configured reusable-connection, additional-connection, worker, instance, and migration values must match the calculation. A configuration that exceeds the limit or omits the reserve must block release. Finalize these process settings and the connection calculation together before the affected production configuration or release is approved; they must fit the hosting-service limits during autoscaling and rolling overlap.

6. <a id="p19-r06"></a>**P19.R06 — Per-service quota and response record**<br>Each Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL-hosting, application-hosting, DNS or CDN, logging, monitoring, CI, and backup quota or spending control must identify the Pass 01 accountable role, measured baseline, expected peak use, numeric warning threshold, enforced hard limit when the service offers one, notification destination, and action required before service interruption or uncontrolled spending.

    **Monthly development, staging, and production cost model.** The capacity and spending record must calculate the complete recurring baseline and forecast for production and staging, including actual Render workspace and instance sizes, cron, database plan, Firebase Authentication with Identity Platform TOTP/App Check/reCAPTCHA billing, independent Ohio/Oregon S3 backups, R2, Postmark, Grafana Cloud/IRM, PostHog, CI, network, restoration drills and domain charges. It must include **local development's real vendor-resource usage**, staging and production, and separately priced Oregon replication, S3 storage/versioning and restores, Render disks and cron, Postmark Basic, Grafana Cloud Pro/IRM plus usage-based telemetry, and the three-project PostHog configuration.

    **Shared allowances, overages, and spending authority.** It must distinguish included allowances shared across projects from per-project allowances. It must compare measured and estimated monthly run rates with the **approved $195/month planning ceiling** (the roughly $200–$250 modeling range is not spending approval), list which providers have usage-based overage or hard-stop exposure, and record which workload or plan change would cross the ceiling. A selected provider does not count as free without evidence of its plan allowance and current usage. Any conflict between the quality objectives and that ceiling must be surfaced for an explicit decision; neither objective may be silently weakened. The planning ceiling does not itself authorize a purchase or a recurring commitment. Spending above the ceiling requires separate explicit authorization before commitment.

7. <a id="p19-r07"></a>**P19.R07 — Production Stripe account and mode**<br>Production must use Stripe live mode, the production account and credentials, the production endpoint secret, and the API and webhook versions and subscribed event types recorded in the release contract. Before production traffic, inspection of the production Stripe account must confirm each binding and the named accounts allowed dashboard access.

    A change to the account, mode, API version, webhook version, endpoint, secret, or subscribed event set must invalidate the affected evidence and trigger webhook-contract and reconciliation testing.

8. <a id="p19-r08"></a>**P19.R08 — R2 environment separation**<br>Production and each non-production environment must use separate R2 buckets and credentials, and a non-production credential must not read or change the production bucket. Production tokens must be scoped to their selected production bucket and the narrowest **provider-supported** permissions needed by each role. Record where an R2 permission groups operations (e.g. a combined read/write class) and cannot separately forbid an undesired API operation; do **not** claim unavailable PUT-versus-DELETE granularity. Mitigate remaining token authority with separate bucket roles, short-lived signed object-specific capabilities, audited application restrictions, rotation and demonstrated denied operations across environments. Before signoff, the production Cloudflare account must be inspected for browser-origin rules, public access, lifecycle deletion, usage limits or alerts, deletion behavior, and recovery settings, and the observed settings must match the venue-image contract. Inspect the actual exposure mode for each production R2 bucket, its custom domain and `r2.dev` access, and whether any private staging, original, or retained-master bytes can be requested anonymously through any domain or alternate public endpoint.

9. <a id="p19-r09"></a>**P19.R09 — PostgreSQL production connection budget**<br>After the selected PostgreSQL service and the request or job deadlines are known, choose under Section 3.3 and configure each request and job type's exact connection-wait deadline and maximum attempt count in the fields defined by Pass 05 requirement 15. The configured values must fit the applicable request or job budget. The selected service and final values must be used to verify that a connection failure returns or records the operation's database-unavailable error, rolls back and closes the session, creates no partial business effect, and stops after the recorded attempt count.

10. <a id="p19-r10"></a>**P19.R10 — Production query performance checks**<br>Using every query listed under Pass 05 requirement 16, measure its execution plan, elapsed time, returned-row count, and the plan-reported scan-row total defined in Pass 05 requirement 16 at the record volume projected by Pass 16 and the planned peak workload recorded under Section 3.3. Choose under Section 3.3 the maximum elapsed time, returned rows, and plan-reported scan-row total for each listed query. A query that exceeds any of its three limits must be corrected and measured again before signoff. If the load testing in Pass 20 requirement 13 changes the planned peak workload, every affected measurement becomes incomplete and must be repeated with the changed workload before signoff.

11. <a id="p19-r11"></a>**P19.R11 — Production-scale migration exercise**<br>Exercise every migration identified by Pass 05 requirement 19 that rewrites, locks, backfills, or validates a table with the record volume projected from Pass 16. Before release, record its observed lock duration and total runtime and confirm that both remain within the limits chosen under Section 3.3 for the selected PostgreSQL service and deployment sequence.

12. <a id="p19-r12"></a>**P19.R12 — Production PostgreSQL role configuration**<br>Configure the application, migration, worker, read-only/diagnostic, and backup roles on the final PostgreSQL hosting service with only their required grants. Verify schema ownership, `search_path`, default privileges, and direct human access on that service. The production application role must neither own the database nor act as superuser.

#### Integration boundaries

- Application authorization belongs to Pass 08. This pass owns external-service, deployment, and operational access.
- Pass 08 requirements 1–4, 8; Pass 09 requirement 16; Pass 10 requirement 7 own authorization inside the Pickup Lane application. Pass 01 owns external-service, deployment, repository, monitoring, backup, and operational access and the accountability assignments consumed by the other passes.
- Pass 01 requirements 3, 6; Pass 03 requirements 1–3; Pass 18 requirements 9 and 10; Pass 19 requirements 2–4 require the actual production Firebase project, Stripe account and mode, Cloudflare R2 bucket, PostgreSQL service, application-hosting service, DNS account, repository and CI environment, monitoring destination, and backup location for final verification. They must remain incomplete while a service selection or account design could change the credential type, permission, injection path, recovery route, or test.
- Pass 05 consumes PostgreSQL roles; Pass 13 consumes Stripe credentials and access; Pass 14 consumes R2 credentials; Pass 03 consumes CI and test credentials; Pass 18 consumes monitoring access; and Passes 15 and 20 consume deployment and recovery access. Each child plan must cite the exact Pass 01 role, credential, or account requirement it needs.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p19-v01"></a>**P19.V01 — Final public request path inspection**<br>For Pass 01 requirement 5, inspect the final architecture record from Pass 01 requirement 4 and trace a browser request, an administrator request, and a Stripe webhook from the public address to the API. Record every component on each path, which component accepts the encrypted connection, whether another address can reach the API directly, which components may add or replace request information, and which component calls each health endpoint. Confirm that no new or revised implementation plan for Pass 06 requirements 8–11, 15 began before those facts were recorded. For code that existed before this master, record the comparison with the final path, every conflict found and corrected, and the final verification result; the code remains incomplete until that record exists.

#### Completion condition

Staging and required predeployment production control-plane resources, provider settings, account bindings, secrets, service accounts, payment modes, hosting process values and the matching PostgreSQL connection calculation, database wait/attempt settings, query and migration measurements, database grants, usage assumptions and access rights are established and correctly segregated. Pass 20 qualifies the exact release and inspects applicable final settings; Pass 21 deploys application code and verifies deployed-host responses, runtime bindings, and production-safe operation. No undeployed response or runtime behavior is claimed as complete here.

---

<a id="p20"></a>

### Pass 20 — Release Qualification and Recovery Testing

#### Purpose

Run complete release-candidate, migration, multi-instance, security, incident, capacity and recovery qualification against isolated staging resources; resolve all open production-setting checks and evidence gates.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p20-r01"></a>**P20.R01 — Immutable release evidence identity**<br>Release evidence must identify the exact immutable source revision and the frontend, API, worker, scheduler, migration, and runtime-image artifact identities that were tested or state that a named artifact type does not exist. Evidence from another revision, a rebuilt artifact with a different identity, or an unlisted component does not approve the release candidate.

2. <a id="p20-r02"></a>**P20.R02 — Migration qualification coverage**<br>Migration verification must cover base-to-head, a schema with projected production volume, model/schema drift between the SQLAlchemy model and Alembic-migrated PostgreSQL schema, data preservation, interruption and resume, adjacent application versions, and rollback or forward fix.

3. <a id="p20-r03"></a>**P20.R03 — Release security assessment**<br>Before launch, the production release build must receive a risk-focused security assessment while running with isolated synthetic accounts, PostgreSQL data, and non-production Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, hosting, and notification resources. The assessment must exercise public and authenticated browser and API entry points, administrator and cross-user authorization boundaries, session and input handling, and abuse of high-risk game, booking, payment, refund, and webhook flows.

    For the exact released source and reachable interfaces, the assessment must inventory whether user-controlled data can reach an OS command or shell, regular-expression pattern, dynamic code evaluator, server template or format string, general-purpose object deserializer, email or notification header, JavaScript object merge, or outbound network-fetch URL.

    For each present sink, require interpreter-specific parameterization, sanitization, safe parser options, or fixed approved destinations as dictated by that sink; reject an exploitable injection or unsafe deserialization path before signoff. For an absent sink, identify the source or route inspected and why no input can reach it.

    A URL supplied by a user must never make the backend call an arbitrary protocol, host, address, or redirect target; if an existing flow requires remote fetching, its allowed destinations and redirect behavior must be fixed and verified, without introducing a general outbound-fetch feature.

    This review belongs to the existing security assessment and does not create a scanning service. It must identify the attack paths and expected prohibited effects from the owning requirements and may reuse their existing tests only when those tests exercise the same running boundary and check the same effects; an automated scanner result alone does not establish business-logic or authorization safety.

    Testing must be bounded, comply with the selected providers' testing rules, and must not perform volumetric denial-of-service testing or affect production users or resources.

    Before assessment, the assigned identity/application-security responsibility must record a severity and impact rubric tied to the prohibited effects in this master, including which exploitable effects count as high-impact and block release even when no more specific requirement names that attack path. Each finding must identify the affected path, observed result, prohibited effect, severity and exploitability under that rubric, accountable Pass 01 role, and disposition: correction with a deadline, verified mitigation with remaining risk and correction deadline, or a documented inapplicability decision supported by evidence.

    Each correction or mitigation must be retested through the affected running path before closure. A finding that violates a master requirement or permits an unauthorized read, charge, booking, financial change, or secret exposure must block production approval until a correction or verified mitigation prevents that effect and the affected path is retested.

    Before a later release that changes authentication, authorization, public routing, sensitive data handling, a high-risk business flow, or its security-relevant dependency or configuration, assess which attack paths and prior findings are affected and rerun the affected scenarios; unchanged scenarios may retain valid evidence under Section 3.5.

    Independently of code changes, the Pass 01 identity/application-security role must rerun a bounded, risk-based sample of the actual protected browser/API attack paths and previously accepted high-impact mitigations at a numeric recurring interval chosen under Section 3.3. Before the first recurring run, that role must record the eligible paths and mitigations, a repeatable selection rule, the minimum number of paths to check in each coverage area named below chosen under Section 3.3, and how changed paths, prior failures, and high-impact mitigations enter the next run. Each run must record the paths selected, why they met the rule, the observed results, and the eligible paths not selected; an interval with no check in a required coverage area is incomplete.

    The sample must cover current user/role isolation, administrator access, exposed endpoints, and financial mutation boundaries and must expand to an affected area after a material incident, detected configuration drift, changed attack technique affecting this architecture, or prior security-test failure. Reuse existing mapped tests and isolated environments; do not require a separate paid scanner or routine volumetric tests.

4. <a id="p20-r04"></a>**P20.R04 — Cross-operation invariant verification**<br>For every invariant in Pass 05 requirements 1–8, 10–12, the child plan must identify every API request, background job, administrative operation, retry, and migration that can create or change the protected state. Each identified path must use the same database constraint or transaction rule; a path is incomplete while it can bypass that protection.

    For a critical multi-record invariant that cannot be continuously enforced by a PostgreSQL constraint or atomic transaction and is not already covered by Pass 13 financial reconciliation or Pass 14 image consistency checks, the child plan must name its authoritative records, a bounded check using the existing scheduler and worker, a numeric maximum time between checks under Section 3.3, and a restricted Pass 18 discrepancy signal.

    At minimum, if both a game capacity counter and active membership records exist, check their agreement and distinguish mismatches from legal holds or in-flight transitions.

    The check must not silently change ambiguous business state or scan unbounded data in one transaction; only a uniquely determined correction established from authoritative data may run automatically.

   A cross-record condition is critical when its failure can change authorization, money or credit, capacity, deletion or hold treatment, audit completeness, or a durable obligation. Inventory each such condition and its write paths. Use the same database constraint or atomic transaction whenever one can continuously enforce it; use the bounded scheduled check only when the child plan proves that database enforcement cannot cover it, while reusing the Pass 13 financial and Pass 14 image checks already assigned there.

5. <a id="p20-r05"></a>**P20.R05 — Deferred PostgreSQL verification**<br>Each verification step that can run only against the selected production PostgreSQL service must remain incomplete and release-blocking in its owning parent pass record, with a link from the receiving pass record when another pass performs the check. The verification map must identify the step and its owning and receiving passes. The deferred record must cite the exact requirement, identify the missing production service or setting, state the event that makes the check executable, define the required observed result, and identify the pre-release or deployment stage at which it must run. A result from another PostgreSQL service must not complete that step.

6. <a id="p20-r06"></a>**P20.R06 — Immutable release artifacts**<br>Every release must produce immutable frontend and backend/worker artifact or deployment identities linked to one source revision, dependency-lock identity, migration head or compatibility reference, build configuration, environment, release timestamp, CI result set, hosting deployment identity, and prior rollback artifact identity.

7. <a id="p20-r07"></a>**P20.R07 — Shipped package inventory**<br>The release record must identify the shipped operating-system, Python, Node, browser-bundle, and application package names and versions. A machine-readable software bill of materials is required when the named dependency scanner or selected hosting service reads it or a contractual obligation requires it. Artifact signing is required when the selected release environment provides both a signing identity Pickup Lane can restrict and a deployment-time check that rejects an invalid signature; before then, signing must not be claimed as a release control.

8. <a id="p20-r08"></a>**P20.R08 — Deployment sequence**<br>The production deployment procedure must follow this sequence:

    1. Pre-deployment configuration validation and schema compatibility checks.
    2. Authorization to deploy.
    3. Migration execution.
    4. API and worker or scheduler rollout.
    5. Frontend rollout.
    6. Health verification.
    7. Applicable traffic-routing or transition behavior.
    8. Production-safe post-deploy checks.
    9. Rollback or forward fix when required.

    **Per-step evidence and progression:** For each step it must state its required input, success observation, failure action, and whether later steps may begin.

    **Pre-deployment authorization and supported Render rollout.** Before production deployment, it must require authorization to deploy for the exact release candidate after all required pre-deployment checks, staging verification, security requirements, release evidence, and recovery preparations are complete. It must deploy through Render's supported procedure, confirm readiness and required deployment health conditions, then run production-safe synthetic checks against the deployed release before final production acceptance.

    **First public launch.** For the first public launch, ordinary user activity must remain blocked until final acceptance; before authorization to deploy, the procedure must identify and verify a workable way, using the selected architecture, to run the required synthetic checks without opening protected user activity to the public. If that method cannot be established, authorization to deploy for the first public launch remains blocked. The procedure must not assume a manual Render traffic-promotion gate.

    **Later production updates.** For later production updates, use Render's normal health-gated, zero-downtime rollout and record that production requests may reach the new release after readiness passes, before post-deploy checks finish. A release needing stricter isolation must establish a supported traffic-control procedure before deployment.

    **Post-deploy failure.** If a post-deploy check fails, block final acceptance and follow the required containment, rollback, or forward-fix procedure; do not assume rollback reverses a committed database migration or external operation.

9. <a id="p20-r09"></a>**P20.R09 — Readiness and liveness health checks**<br>Health checks must use the meanings and access rules in Pass 06 requirement 8. Configure the Render API web service's single HTTP health-check path to `/ready`. A new instance must not receive traffic before passing that check. An already-serving instance may continue receiving requests while checks fail; Render withdraws it from new traffic after 15 consecutive seconds of failed checks and restarts it after 60 consecutive seconds of failed checks, even if `/live` continues succeeding. `/live` remains an independent application diagnostic endpoint, not an independently configurable Render restart controller. During the interval before withdrawal, PostgreSQL-dependent requests must fail safely without the prohibited effects in P06.R08.

10. <a id="p20-r10"></a>**P20.R10 — Rolling deployment compatibility**<br>Rolling deployment must preserve the HTTP-caller compatibility required by Pass 06 requirements 6 and 7 and compatibility among adjacent backend, worker, scheduler, and database-schema versions for every combination that can run together during the deployment.

11. <a id="p20-r11"></a>**P20.R11 — Rollback and forward-fix plan**<br>Rollback must identify which application, worker, frontend, configuration, and migration states can be reversed. A non-reversible migration must have a tested forward-fix path before rollout.

12. <a id="p20-r12"></a>**P20.R12 — Production capacity evidence**<br>Capacity evidence must include frontend and API hosting, API and worker compute, PostgreSQL connections and storage, queue throughput and backlog, Firebase and Stripe limits, Cloudflare R2 requests and storage, DNS or CDN, outbound network use, logs and monitoring, CI, backups, and autoscaling or instance ceilings. For each resource it must record the exact service or configuration limit, how that limit was obtained, tested operating range, warning signal, observed or documented failure behavior, scaling action, and Pass 01 role accountable for its cost.

13. <a id="p20-r13"></a>**P20.R13 — Production-mix load tests**<br>After launch-volume and usage assumptions are recorded under Section 3.3, load tests must model the final peak mix of public and authenticated requests, concurrent final-place bookings, payments and webhooks, generated and processed jobs, venue-image administration, and administrative queries. The test must increase load until the first resource reaches its service limit, configured limit, blocking performance budget, or correctness failure. Results must identify that resource, the largest tested load that met every blocking requirement, and the numeric difference between that load and the planned peak.

14. <a id="p20-r14"></a>**P20.R14 — Failed release gate stops deployment**<br>The deployment must stop before the next step when a release gate fails. It must not continue until the failed requirement is corrected and the same gate passes for the release candidate.

15. <a id="p20-r15"></a>**P20.R15 — Release identity in diagnostics**<br>Release identity must appear in runtime diagnostics, logs, and error reports so an observed failure can be tied to the deployed artifact.

16. <a id="p20-r16"></a>**P20.R16 — Runtime image support and ownership**<br>If the selected hosting service deploys a runtime image, the image's base version must remain inside its upstream security-support period, and a security fix required by Pass 03 must be applied within the vulnerability-response deadline chosen under Section 3.3. The image must separate build and runtime content; exclude source-control secrets, service-account files, caches, tests, and unused build tools; run without root or privileged mode; have no container-engine socket; and use the hosting service's recorded CPU, memory, process, capability, and writable-path limits. If the service does not deploy a runtime image, the architecture record must state that this requirement's image controls do not apply.

   The architecture record must state the actual Render deployment mode and artifact owner for the API, worker, Cron, and frontend. Apply image-specific controls only to an application-controlled Docker image; for a native runtime or static frontend, record the mode and why image-only controls do not apply, while retaining the other release and dependency checks. The mode must be inspected before claiming this requirement complete.

17. <a id="p20-r17"></a>**P20.R17 — Isolated restore validation**<br>Every restore exercise and production recovery must first restore into an environment that cannot receive production traffic or send production mutations. Before its data can replace production, checks must verify schema and migration state, Firebase-to-account mapping, roles, bookings and capacity, Stripe reconciliation (comparison of restored local payment and booking records with Stripe-side payment and refund state), durable jobs and duplicate-effect protection, audit integrity, active investigation or dispute retention pauses, approved legal or security holds, completed deletions, and R2 reference and fallback behavior.

   Before traffic resumes, replay the independently recoverable deletion and retention-decision journal required by Pass 15 requirement 6 through the chosen recovery point. Verify ordering, completeness, and every applicable post-backup decision; a gap or unknown outcome blocks traffic resumption.

18. <a id="p20-r18"></a>**P20.R18 — Duplicate-effect prevention during recovery**<br>Recovery must prevent stale jobs, webhooks, scheduled tasks, or restored idempotency state from creating duplicate charges, refunds, bookings, messages, deletions, or image operations.

19. <a id="p20-r19"></a>**P20.R19 — Recovery tabletop and restore exercise**<br>A recovery tabletop (a guided decision and communication exercise) and an isolated technical restore must be completed before production signoff. The feasibility, tier-specific applicability, and restoration results of the Pass 01 requirement 1 recovery objectives must be verified under Section 3.3; off-region backup replication alone is not evidence that they are met. The Pass 01 incident-recovery role must set a numeric recurring interval under Section 3.3, and both exercises must finish within each interval. The affected exercise must also repeat after a change to PostgreSQL hosting, region, backup format, encryption key, restore tooling, schema compatibility, Firebase identity mapping, Stripe reconciliation, job recovery, or deletion reapplication and after an incident exposes a recovery gap.

20. <a id="p20-r20"></a>**P20.R20 — Traffic resumption after recovery**<br>Production traffic must resume after recovery only when the tier-specific invariants and post-recovery checks pass. Service availability alone is not recovery proof.

21. <a id="p20-r21"></a>**P20.R21 — Outbound mutation controls during restore**<br>Workers, schedulers, and outbound operations that can mutate Stripe, Firebase, Cloudflare R2, or email/notification delivery must remain disabled during initial restore validation. They may resume only after local payment/job state is reconciled and duplicate-effect checks pass.

22. <a id="p20-r22"></a>**P20.R22 — Recurring incident and recovery exercises**<br>Within each complete exercise cycle at the interval set in Pass 20 requirement 19, recovery and incident exercises must cover restore, deployment rollback, secret compromise, Stripe webhook outage, worker backlog, one outage for each production dependency named in Pass 18 requirement 15, user-deletion failure, and recovery of domain, DNS, and external-service administrative access. An incident that requires external notification, causes an unauthorized disclosure, creates or risks duplicate financial value, corrupts booking/capacity state, exceeds an approved recovery objective, or exposes a recovery gap triggers an additional exercise for the affected path. A change to backup format, software version, PostgreSQL extension, encryption key, deployment architecture, or recovery control also triggers an exercise for the affected path.

#### Integration boundaries

- Pass 01 requirement 1 supplies the recovery objectives that Pass 01 requirement 4 must use to select and record the production architecture. After those objectives are recorded, Pass 01 requirement 4 must be recorded before hosting-dependent implementation begins in Passes 03, 04, 05, 06, 11, 14, and 17 or for the remaining requirements of Pass 15. Pass 05 requirements 13 and 14 define the connection calculation and validation before Pass 19 requirement 5 selects the actual process settings and confirms that the PostgreSQL connection budget fits. Section 3.3 blocks an affected earlier design when missing process or service facts could change it. Recording the recovery objectives or architecture does not complete the remaining Pass 20 or Pass 15 requirements.
