- Pass 01 requirement 5; Pass 04 requirements 2–4; Pass 05 requirement 28; Pass 06 requirements 2, 6–12, 15; Pass 17 requirement 2 supply startup, HTTP contract, health, client compatibility, process-client, and timeout behavior. Pass 20 requirement 10 consumes Pass 06 requirements 6 and 7 and supplies their final adjacent-release evidence without requiring all of Pass 06 to be accepted first. Pass 05 requirements 13 and 14, 18 and 19, 22, 25 and 26 and Pass 19 requirements 5 and 9–12 supply database capacity and migration compatibility. Pass 11 requirements 7–9, 12 supply worker and scheduler deployment behavior.
- Pass 17 requirements 6, 12–14, 20 supply supported-client, production-build, performance, and degraded-integration results. Pass 03 requirements 4 and 5, 7 and 8, 14, 22; Pass 20 requirements 1–3 supply gates, artifact evidence, migrations, release automation, and the running-application security assessment. Pass 09 requirements 1–7; Pass 18 requirements 1–6 supply health, capacity, release, and external-service signals. Pass 01 requirements 2 and 3, 6; Pass 03 requirements 1–3; Pass 10 requirement 8; Pass 18 requirements 9–11; Pass 19 requirements 1–4 supply deployment credentials, human access, ownership, and recovery.
- Final capacity, load, cost, timing, process, region, and response values follow Section 3.3 and remain incomplete until the named production systems, recorded business inputs, and measurements needed to choose them exist. No temporary production architecture or process setting may complete an affected requirement.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p20-v01"></a>**P20.V01 — Headers and redirects before deployment**<br>For Pass 06 requirements 9–11; Pass 17 requirement 2, use the production release build in an isolated staging deployment with the recorded public request path and matching HTTP server and intermediary components. Send each listed kind of response and inspect the response received by the caller. Record every redirect and effective security, cache, browser-origin, and security-sensitive `Set-Cookie` header; inspect `Secure`, `SameSite`, and applicable `HttpOnly` attributes. Confirm that the result matches the recorded policy, contains no conflicting header fields, and does not redirect repeatedly. Pass 21 verification 2 checks the effective result from the deployed production host.

   Exercise the Pickup Lane-hosted return page and each Pickup Lane redirect after a Stripe or Firebase browser flow, and inspect their headers through the recorded public path. Record provider-owned responses separately as provider behavior; do not claim that Pickup Lane sets their headers or use those responses to prove its own policy.

   **Cookies:** For an application-set security-sensitive cookie, verify that a host-bound cookie uses `__Host-`, `Path=/`, and no `Domain` attribute; verify that any cross-host cookie has the recorded need, `__Secure-`, and a narrowly scoped `Domain`. Test a wrongly scoped cookie and confirm that it does not satisfy the approved policy.

   **Content and HSTS headers:** Confirm the effective `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` response header in isolation. Exercise the recorded HSTS policy and subdomain inventory with production-equivalent host configuration; confirm that a proposed `includeSubDomains` policy protects only a domain whose affected subdomains can all serve valid HTTPS, or record the exact incompatibility and narrower-policy exception. Pass 21 verification 2 inspects the actual production hostname and `max-age`.

   For shared provider-owned hostnames, retain the provider-managed HSTS policy without treating that domain as Pickup Lane-controlled; inspect its deployed result in Pass 21 verification 2.

   **Origin, host, and proxy trust:** In isolation, send an allowed browser origin, an unlisted origin, every supported hostname, an unlisted hostname, and caller-supplied proxy headers both through and around the recorded proxy path; confirm the required acceptance or rejection and confirm that forged information does not alter routing, authorization, redirect destinations, or request attribution.

   Send allowed and unlisted preflight origins, requested methods, and requested headers through the same path. Record the status and every effective CORS header; confirm a valid preflight completes without user authentication or App Check proof, while an invalid preflight supplies no authorization to read a credentialed response and neither kind executes the requested route. Pass 20 verification 6 checks the subsequent classified browser request with valid and invalid App Check proof.

   **TLS and HTTP:** In isolation, test a valid certificate, an expired or wrong-host certificate, and an HTTP protected request through the recorded public and alternate host paths. Confirm that valid HTTPS succeeds, the invalid certificate is rejected, and HTTP performs no protected operation or disclosure. Pass 21 verification 2 inspects production certificates and provider-managed TLS behavior and makes the production-safe HTTPS and HTTP requests.

2. <a id="p20-v02"></a>**P20.V02 — Production limits and rejection cases**<br>For Pass 06 requirements 13 and 14, use the production release build and final limits in an isolated deployment containing the same HTTP server and intermediary components as the selected production request path. Exercise the largest accepted input and the first rejected input for every request, header, URL, multipart, file, decoded-file, pagination, search, export, bulk-operation, time, and simultaneous-work limit. For the three fixed body limits, count actual received bytes and test a correct, absent, malformed, duplicate, and understated length header; conflicting message-length indicators; two correctly framed requests sent on one connection; unsupported content encoding; and a correctly signed raw Stripe request. Record that two valid requests remain separate, malformed or ambiguous framing is rejected, and an exceeded limit is rejected at the required enforcement point without a partial database, job, upload, payment, booking, or external-service effect. If the selected hosting service cannot be reproduced in the isolated deployment, follow Section 3.3 by inspecting the production setting, retaining the service documentation that defines its behavior, and producing the documented rejection in a separate application test. Do not send a framing or exhaustion test to production when it could consume limited capacity, change production data, create a charge, or cause a real request to fail.

3. <a id="p20-v03"></a>**P20.V03 — Caller-specific workflow repetition**<br>For Pass 18 requirement 18, repeat every assigned workflow from the same and different caller identities through the planned number of application processes. For a request-count control, record the permitted attempts, first rejected attempt, retry response, shared enforcement result, and monitoring signal. For an authentication, uniqueness, idempotency, or business-state control, submit the exact repeat and a changed repeat, then record the required original, unchanged, or conflict result. In both cases, confirm the absence of account probing, repeated user content, unintended cost or capacity consumption, and duplicate business effects. Inventory every released public and authenticated route and its assigned traffic policy. Exercise ordinary game browsing, read-only list/detail/search, authenticated and administrative reads, and the assigned mutation and provider-facing families at the recorded legitimate peak and beyond each applicable limit. Send excess requests alternately to both Render API instances while legitimate requests, health checks, permitted browser preflights, valid Stripe webhook deliveries, and other required service requests continue; separately make the required control unavailable. Record responses, `Retry-After` when a retry is invited, API and PostgreSQL work, external-service calls, monitoring signals, and business state. Confirm that legitimate traffic and required service deliveries succeed within their recorded conditions, excessive traffic is rejected or otherwise bounded before it exhausts the protected resource, the control cannot be bypassed by changing instances, an unavailable control follows its recorded policy without admitting unbounded work, and rejection creates no prohibited database, job, upload, booking, payment, or external-service effect.

4. <a id="p20-v04"></a>**P20.V04 — Browser third-party destinations**<br>For Pass 17 requirement 2, load every production page and feature that can contact a third party through the final public path. Record the browser destinations actually contacted and every policy violation. Confirm that each contacted destination appears in the Pass 17 inventory and effective policy and that a page cannot load or contact an unlisted destination. In the isolated release deployment, attempt object execution, an injected base element, and framing by an unrelated origin; confirm that the effective `object-src`, `base-uri`, and `frame-ancestors` directives block each attempt.

5. <a id="p20-v05"></a>**P20.V05 — Layered timeout and cancellation cases**<br>For Pass 06 requirement 15, force each recorded connection, body-read, database, Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, application, proxy, and supported-client timeout or cancellation separately in an isolated deployment using the final release build and settings. Record the elapsed time at every layer, returned error, database-session closure, transaction rollback, stream and client-resource release, and final business outcome. Exercise background-job deadlines separately under Pass 11 rather than treating them as HTTP requests. For an operation that may have succeeded before the timeout, record the required PostgreSQL, Firebase identity, Firebase deletion, Stripe or booking, durable-job, or Cloudflare R2 outcome check and confirm that the operation is not repeated before that check establishes whether it succeeded.

   Exercise each documented browser, provider webhook/callback, and independent HTTP-caller deadline against the final request path. Record the inner-layer, application, proxy, and caller times and margins. Where a provider deadline is undocumented, record the unresolved delivery claim rather than treating browser timing as evidence for that caller.

6. <a id="p20-v06"></a>**P20.V06 — App Check mode and proof cases**<br>For Pass 10 requirements 2, 4 and 5, exercise `disabled`, `observe`, and `enforced` modes with valid, wrong-app, missing, malformed, expired, and Firebase-unavailable App Check proof on every protected route group and on Stripe webhook, non-browser callback, and signed R2 routes that must remain excluded. During observation, send at least one otherwise-valid request for every protected route-group and supported-browser-class combination, then repeat with one combination omitted. Record denial, observation counts by route group and browser class, identifiers present in those records, route effects, the enforcement decision, and rollback. Confirm that observation does not deny requests; the omitted combination blocks enforcement; enforcement also remains blocked after any otherwise-valid request produces missing or invalid proof; enforcement rejects before route effects with the required `403` or `503`; excluded routes do not require App Check; raw proof and app identifiers are absent from logs; and rollback restores the recorded mode.

   For each classified browser route group, send an allowed preflight requesting the actual method and App Check header without a proof value, then send the actual request with valid, missing, invalid, and Firebase-unavailable proof while enforcement is enabled. Repeat the preflight with an unlisted origin, method, and header, including an unlisted proof header. Record the preflight status and CORS headers separately from each actual request's status, authentication and authorization result, route effects, and monitoring signal. Confirm allowed preflights complete without proof or route effects, invalid preflights supply no authorizing CORS headers, and only the actual request with valid proof and all other required credentials and permissions reaches protected work; a preflight success does not change the `403` or `503` result required for a later failed proof check.

   Compare the versioned frontend/backend route inventories with every released browser-called API route, including public reads. Exercise one reviewed exemption and one unclassified or mismatched route; confirm the exemption follows its recorded decision and the mismatch blocks enforcement rather than being silently omitted.

7. <a id="p20-v07"></a>**P20.V07 — Frontend App Check proof cases**<br>For Pass 17 requirement 21, use the production frontend build with valid, missing, expired, wrong-app, and unavailable App Check proof. Inspect browser storage and outgoing requests to Pickup Lane, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, and an unrelated address. Confirm that one shared client attaches proof only to classified Pickup Lane API requests, stores no reusable proof, and does not replay a mutation after proof acquisition fails.

   Inspect the frontend/backend classification version and requests for public reads, protected mutations, a reviewed exemption, Stripe webhook, callback, and direct R2 upload; confirm proof reaches exactly the classified Pickup Lane browser API routes.

8. <a id="p20-v08"></a>**P20.V08 — Read-only production inspection**<br>For Pass 10 requirement 6, inspect sanitized Firebase or Identity Platform production settings and every production administrator's factor-enforcement state read-only. Inspect the actual production Identity Platform upgrade status, enabled TOTP factor setting, policy that requires the completed factor for privileged access, each named administrator's enrollment and role binding, and the qualified alternate recovery route without signing in as, challenging, recovering, or changing a real production administrator. Record the inspected environment and account alias, inspection time, enforced factor, policy and enrollment result, and recovery capability without recording secret material or private identifiers. This production inspection establishes the observed production configuration and administrator enrollment; it does not claim to prove a live production challenge.

    **Active staging challenge and recovery exercises.** In the separate Firebase staging application configured with the same factor policy, exercise enrollment, sign-in enforcement, factor recovery, administrator onboarding, and administrator removal. Compare signed-in administrator access with a fully completed TOTP challenge, password-only authentication, an unenrolled administrator, and a stale or recovered session; record the enforced factor and recovery result. In staging, attempt factor recovery by a caller with only access to the enrollment email, a changed or unverified telephone number, a stale session, and a correctly verified enrolled recovery method; confirm that the former attempts fail without changing the administrator's authority and the authorized recovery succeeds without reducing the accepted assurance.

    **Cross-environment comparison and signoff blockers.** Confirm from the named production policy and per-administrator inspection, together with the active staging results, that a production administrator cannot receive protected access without the required completed factor. If production enforcement, any administrator's enrollment, or equivalence of the staging policy cannot be established, retain MFA enforcement as an incomplete signoff blocker. Confirm that only a correctly enrolled and verified administrator completes protected access in staging and that upgrade-related usage and cost appear in Pass 20 evidence without claiming the upgrade is already provisioned.

   **Staging assisted-recovery and alternate-operator exercise.** In isolated staging with the production-equivalent recovery contract, exercise assisted administrator TOTP replacement with only email access, with the approved stronger evidence, and with the primary administrator or their credentials unavailable. Have the qualified alternate operator use the documented application-specific route, then separately make that operator or route unavailable. Record authority, instructions followed, elapsed time against the numeric response or recovery deadline, audit result, and whether the alternate can restore access without relying on the unavailable primary credentials.

    **Production recovery-capability comparison and blockers.** Inspect the production route, operator assignment, and actual Firebase plan and replacement capability read-only; record their correspondence to the exercised staging route without claiming a production recovery was performed. Confirm no email-only recovery or privileged access before audited re-enrollment; retain a missing production capability, qualified operator, independent route, policy mismatch, or missed deadline as an unresolved signoff blocker until verified.

9. <a id="p20-v09"></a>**P20.V09 — Protected operation authorization matrix**<br>For Pass 08 requirements 1–3; Pass 09 requirement 16, build a table containing every protected operation and the player, host, active administrator, unverified administrator, suspended administrator, deleted administrator, demoted administrator, resource owner, non-owner, and wrong-parent identities that can reach it. Exercise every listed combination. Record the current role, account state, relationship, resource state, returned status and fields, and stored or external effects. Confirm that only combinations authorized by the exact parent requirement read or change protected state.

10. <a id="p20-v10"></a>**P20.V10 — Protected list and export authorization**<br>For Pass 08 requirements 5–7, 10, request list, search, cursor, aggregate, bulk, existing export, direct-object, and nested-object operations with authorized and unauthorized records mixed in the database. For each protected endpoint family, request an existing but inaccessible identifier, a nonexistent identifier, and a valid identifier under the wrong parent. Record the query result, response status, fields and other observable response behavior, jobs, payments, audit records, and committed database state. Confirm that filtering occurs in the query, aggregates exclude unauthorized rows, each response matches the recorded `403` or concealed `404` family contract, concealed cases do not disclose which resource exists, and denial creates no partial or follow-up effect.

11. <a id="p20-v11"></a>**P20.V11 — PostgreSQL constraints and write paths**<br>For Pass 05 requirements 1, 8, 10; Pass 20 requirement 4, inventory every protected relationship and every write path, then attempt an invalid foreign key, duplicate Firebase-account link, duplicate membership, duplicate active record, reused idempotency key in the same scope, missing required field, invalid state, negative money or capacity value, and each prohibited deletion. Inspect database constraints, application errors, defaults, committed rows, and each relationship's cited business requirement and approved Pass 02/Pass 16 treatment or missing-decision approval. Confirm that every path is protected, each invalid write is rejected without partial state, and every permitted deletion produces exactly its recorded and approved result. Create a controlled stored-capacity/active-membership mismatch, including one case that cannot be uniquely corrected, and execute the recorded bounded recurring check through its scheduled deadline. Confirm the signal identifies the affected game and mismatch without disclosing unnecessary private data, no unbounded scan runs, and the ambiguous state remains unchanged pending authorized repair. Confirm that already-covered financial and R2 checks are reused rather than duplicated.

   Inventory cross-record conditions whose failure can affect authorization, money or credit, capacity, deletion or hold treatment, audit completeness, or durable work. For each, inspect the database enforcement or the recorded reason it cannot continuously enforce the condition, then exercise the bounded check only for the latter and avoid duplicating Pass 13 or Pass 14 reconciliation.

12. <a id="p20-v12"></a>**P20.V12 — Interrupted multi-record operations**<br>For Pass 05 requirement 2, interrupt each operation that performs more than one database change after every individual write but before commit. Inspect all affected tables after each interruption. Confirm that either the complete application outcome commits or none of its database changes remain.

13. <a id="p20-v13"></a>**P20.V13 — Concurrent financial and capacity claims**<br>For Pass 05 requirements 3 and 4, 11, run concurrent attempts to claim the final game spot, create the same membership, create the first mapping for one Firebase account, consume the same credit, claim the same job, deliver the same notice, perform the same administrative state change, and apply the same idempotent mutation. Inspect every authoritative row, stored counter, version, and constraint result. Confirm that exactly one permitted outcome succeeds, capacity representations agree, and no duplicate, negative, or partly applied state exists.

14. <a id="p20-v14"></a>**P20.V14 — PostgreSQL error mapping**<br>For Pass 05 requirement 9, cause each database constraint, connection, timeout, and unexpected PostgreSQL error through an affected API or job path. Record the application error and corresponding restricted server record. Confirm that the caller receives the stable operation-specific error without SQL, table structure, credentials, or raw host diagnostics.

15. <a id="p20-v15"></a>**P20.V15 — Maximum database connection use**<br>For Pass 05 requirements 13–15 and Pass 19 requirements 5 and 9, calculate maximum simultaneous connection use from the final process, instance, autoscaling, migration, monitoring, backup, maintenance, and rolling-overlap configuration and compare the total plus reserve with the selected service limit. In an isolated deployment with the same per-process PostgreSQL connection-pool configuration (the reusable database connections each process may hold), occupy every permitted connection and attempt one more request and job connection. Record the wait time, database-unavailable result, attempt count, rollback, session release, partial-effect check, and recovery. Confirm that configured values match the calculation, the reserve remains unused by ordinary work, and excess demand does not leak a session or continue retrying.

16. <a id="p20-v16"></a>**P20.V16 — Production-volume query performance**<br>For Pass 05 requirements 16 and 17, 24, 27 and Pass 19 requirement 10, measure every listed query at the projected end-of-retention volume and final peak concurrency. Record its plan, time, returned rows, the included scan nodes and fields, the plan-reported scan-row calculation defined in Pass 05 requirement 16, indexes used, and application memory use. Exercise the largest accepted and first excessive page request, a modified cursor, a cursor reused with a different endpoint, filter, direction, user, or role, and insert, update, and delete between pages. Compare each later-page result with the endpoint's visibility and ordering contract recorded before implementation, including authorization-context changes. Confirm every measurement stays within its limit, excessive input follows the API contract, cursors cannot cross contexts, ordering remains deterministic, every unchanged matching row that remains authorized appears exactly once, and results contain no unauthorized row.

17. <a id="p20-v17"></a>**P20.V17 — Migration graph and compatibility**<br>For Pass 05 requirements 18 and 19, 26 and Pass 19 requirement 11, apply every migration from a clean database and from the preceding released schema populated to the projected record volume. Run every adjacent application, worker, and scheduler version that can coexist across the boundary, measure lock and total runtime, and execute the declared rollback or forward fix. Interrupt each batched data change after each batch boundary and resume it. Confirm one migration head, required extensions, model/schema agreement, preserved data, no repeated completed batch, compatibility during overlap, and recovery within the recorded limits.

18. <a id="p20-v18"></a>**P20.V18 — Production PostgreSQL grants**<br>For Pass 05 requirements 21 and 25 and Pass 19 requirement 12, inspect each final production PostgreSQL role, credential assignment, grant, object owner, default privilege, `search_path`, and direct-human-access path without changing production data. On an isolated PostgreSQL system with the same roles and grants, attempt every operation listed as permitted or prohibited for the API, worker, migration, backup, monitoring, diagnostic, and human roles. Confirm that distinct grant sets use distinct credentials, the application role is neither owner nor superuser, and each role can perform only its recorded operations.

19. <a id="p20-v19"></a>**P20.V19 — Deferred PostgreSQL evidence**<br>For Pass 20 requirement 5, use the verification map and linked parent pass records to inspect every step deferred until the final PostgreSQL service exists. Confirm that each cites one requirement, missing input, executable start event, required result, and execution stage; remains release-blocking before execution; and closes only with evidence from the production service named in the architecture record.

20. <a id="p20-v20"></a>**P20.V20 — Staging Stripe payment flows**<br>For Pass 13 requirement 25, use the production release build with the **dedicated staging Stripe general sandbox**, not production live mode, to complete each current charged flow, including one using application credit and one captured payment that later loses capacity. Inspect the rendered Stripe test receipt or other chosen customer receipt against the Stripe charge and local payment and booking records; record receipt availability, business and service description, transaction time, actual charged amount and currency, and the booking or compensation wording. Then exercise authorization without capture, decline, failed confirmation, exact request retry, duplicate and out-of-order webhook delivery, and refund after capture. Confirm one accurate receipt artifact per completed Stripe payment transaction, no completed-payment receipt for an uncaptured or failed payment, no second receipt-triggering action caused by a retry or duplicate delivery, no false booking confirmation, and continued refund and compensation handling. Because Stripe sandbox/test mode does not prove live-mode receipt-email delivery, inspect the production Stripe receipt setting and Stripe's documentation for its live-mode delivery behavior without sending a live charge; record this separate evidence without claiming that the test receipt proves live email delivery.

   As the synthetic customer, open the receipt from the authenticated transaction view and attempt it as another user. Record the synthetic Stripe charge and receipt identity without storing a prohibited external identifier, the provider-supported staging method or fixture used to obtain an expired hosted link, the attempted access to that link, the reissue action, and the observed customer access or delivery result. When the selected staging environment supports safe expiry and reissue, exercise both through Stripe and confirm owner-only access, the correct charge and amount, a working reissue result, and no completed-payment receipt for an uncaptured or failed charge. When it cannot, inspect the named production setting and provider documentation and use Section 3.3's isolated-response procedure to verify Pickup Lane's expired-link and reissue handling; label that evidence as documented provider behavior and simulated application behavior rather than end-to-end Stripe expiry or email delivery, and record any result those methods still cannot establish as incomplete. Receipt email delivery alone does not satisfy customer access.

21. <a id="p20-v21"></a>**P20.V21 — Concurrent scheduler occurrences**<br>For Pass 11 requirements 8, 12, restart the web application and scheduler, run two schedulers for the same occurrence, and deploy each adjacent worker and scheduler combination with pending jobs of every version that can exist during overlap. Record schedule-occurrence identities, enqueued jobs, compatible handlers, and unsupported jobs. Confirm one obligation per occurrence, continued operation without a web process, compatible execution where promised, and visible unchanged handling where a version is unsupported. For each critical schedule type, suppress one scheduled invocation, fail one run after trigger, delay another beyond its completion deadline, and restart the scheduler while a previous occurrence is pending. Inspect durable due occurrence, last successful completion, alert delivery, and idempotent recovery or explicit unresolved state. Confirm that no required occurrence disappears, missed work is discovered before the affected business deadline, and backfill cannot create duplicate business effects. Confirm that backup detection can report stale copies without a successful scheduler run.

   Suppress Render Cron while leaving Grafana monitoring active, then produce no data and a monitor query error. Record last-success age and alert delivery; confirm each failure is detected before its business deadline without a Cron invocation.

22. <a id="p20-v22"></a>**P20.V22 — R2 upload and bucket qualification**<br>For Pass 14 requirements 3, 4, and 9; Pass 19 requirement 8, inspect the production bucket and token scopes without copying their values or changing objects. In isolated production-equivalent (non-production resources matching the relevant production build, settings, and limits) and non-production buckets, exercise an expired capability, wrong method, wrong key, wrong pending record, repeated use of one signed upload URL before expiry, confirmation replay, R2 timeout before a response, timeout after the object change, cross-environment credential, and each prohibited operation for every role. Inspect the private bucket's production browser-origin CORS rules without changing production objects; in non-production buckets with the matching rules, exercise the recorded administrator frontend origin, an unlisted origin, the required `PUT` and upload headers, and a disallowed method or header. Inspect PostgreSQL, R2, cleanup or reconciliation state, logs, telemetry, and Cloudflare settings. Confirm that the approved browser upload works, disallowed browser origins, methods, and headers do not receive CORS permission, CORS is not treated as authentication or single-use protection, and reuse before expiry does not bypass pending-record checks, publish unvalidated content, or create a duplicate business effect. Confirm no unauthorized publication, overwrite, cross-environment access, blind retry after an unknown outcome, or capability leakage and confirm that production settings match the recorded contract. For the selected two-bucket contract in each environment, verify that the public bucket serves only sanitized outputs and that all private staging, original, and retained-master objects are in the separate non-public bucket with independently restricted credentials. Wherever a public custom domain is configured, verify that it serves only sanitized outputs; in production, verify that published public outputs use only the recorded production custom domain and that `r2.dev` does not serve bucket objects. If an explicit future architecture revision selects a controlled private-delivery alternative, verify its approved private path and equivalent negative tests under Pass 14 requirement 7; that future branch does not satisfy the current two-bucket contract.

23. <a id="p20-v23"></a>**P20.V23 — Approved Terms and public presentation**<br>For Pass 02 requirement 6 and Pass 17 requirement 22, inspect the qualified legal review, approved version published at the isolated staging public address and retained earlier version, access without sign-in, and the terms shown before email and Google registration, community and official game hosting or joining, and official-game checkout. Compare the official-game and independent community-organizer descriptions, adult eligibility, conduct, soccer injury risks, cancellation, refund, dispute, governing-law, liability, any waiver or acknowledgment, and any indemnification treatment with the reviewed decision and implemented flows. Inspect a revision and the associated renewed-acceptance decision under Pass 07 requirement 14. Record the version and text reached at each point, legal decision and its jurisdiction and activity scope, and each mismatch or inaccessible document. Confirm that no placeholder or unreviewed protection is published as final, every required action can reach the applicable text beforehand, earlier accepted text remains reproducible, and a missing document, unresolved material legal question, or material mismatch blocks approval. Pass 21 verification 4 separately checks the deployed production address and version.

24. <a id="p20-v24"></a>**P20.V24 — Release artifact and migration reproducibility**<br>For Pass 03 requirements 7, 18; Pass 20 requirements 1 and 2, build two artifacts from the same revision, change one build input, and compare the recorded identities. Run base-to-head and adjacent-version migration checks at projected volume, interrupt and resume them, and exercise rollback or forward fix. Change a pinned Node, npm, Python, tool, manifest, or lock value. Confirm evidence names every existing artifact, changed artifacts invalidate prior evidence, migration results cover every listed condition, and version or lock mismatch fails.

25. <a id="p20-v25"></a>**P20.V25 — External-service qualification checks**<br>For Pass 03 requirement 13, run Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL-hosting, and application-hosting checks in each named emulator, fake, test, staging, and final production environment used by the release. Before deployment, a production-environment check here is limited to read-only inspection of an established provider or hosting setting or a production-safe check that does not require the deployed application; deployed-host behavior is checked in Pass 21. Compare the evidence labels and observations. Confirm that each production claim is supported by the exact production system or a production-safe check allowed by the requirement being proved and Section 3.5.

26. <a id="p20-v26"></a>**P20.V26 — Initial isolated attack-path assessment**<br>For Pass 20 requirement 3, assess the running production release build in an isolated deployment through the public, authenticated, and administrative browser and API paths and the named game, booking, payment, refund, and webhook attack paths. Reuse a mapped test only after confirming it observes the same runtime boundary and prohibited effect; introduce controlled cross-user, role, input, replay, and financial-state attacks where the mapped tests leave a gap. Exercise applicable input-to-interpreter sinks from the released code inventory with isolated malicious command characters, regex metacharacters, executable expressions, template delimiters, serialized type markers, email-header newlines, unexpected object prototype keys, and unapproved or redirected outbound URLs; record either a verified absent sink or its accepted safe result and the missing prohibited effect. Record each attempted path, expected and actual response, database and Stripe test-mode result, prohibited-effect check, finding and disposition, correction or mitigation, and retest.

    **Changed-route reassessment.** Change one security-relevant route or authorization rule and inspect the resulting reassessment and affected retests. Confirm that no production resource or user is affected, no volumetric test runs, every finding has a justified disposition, and an unresolved requirement-violating or high-impact exploitable finding blocks release.

    **Recurring and incident-triggered assessment.** Advance a controlled clock past the recurring revalidation interval without any source-code change, and trigger one material security incident or configuration-drift finding. Inspect scheduled affected-path tests, scope, results, and remaining findings; confirm the recurring and triggered checks execute in isolation and unresolved prohibited effects block acceptance.

   **Severity and release decision.** Inspect the recorded severity rubric and classify one controlled exploitable finding that violates a master requirement and one that meets its high-impact rule without a more specific requirement. Confirm that both block release until correction or verified mitigation prevents the effect and the affected path is retested.

    **Recurring sample coverage.** For the recurring run, compare the selected and omitted paths with the recorded eligible inventory, repeatable selection rule, minimum coverage in each named area, changed-path and prior-failure treatment, and high-impact mitigation treatment; confirm that no required area is empty and an interval without its required sample remains incomplete.

27. <a id="p20-v27"></a>**P20.V27 — End-to-end telemetry traces**<br>For Pass 09 requirements 1 and 2, 5, trace one successful and one failed authentication, booking and payment, refund, durable job, account deletion, image processing, and administrative action through every participating component. Supply a valid, malformed, oversized, and missing caller correlation value. Record the new internal request identifier, any separately retained caller correlation value, the caller error fields required by Pass 06 requirement 2, business and job links, environment, release, operation, outcome, and normalized error class. Inspect each participating log source's time-synchronization configuration or the selected provider's documentation for its managed time source. Normalize recorded timestamps for one multi-component test operation to UTC and confirm that every security-event timestamp has UTC or an explicit offset and that the correlated event order is consistent with synchronized clocks rather than untrusted client time. Force an isolated wrong-host backend TLS certificate and verify a sanitized failure event with the expected correlation, without a transmitted credential or sensitive endpoint value. Confirm that every request receives a new internal identifier; a valid caller value remains separate and linked; malformed, oversized, and missing caller values never replace the internal identifier; each flow can be followed end to end; and no prohibited value appears.

28. <a id="p20-v28"></a>**P20.V28 — Failure-state monitoring cases**<br>For Pass 09 requirements 3 and 4; Pass 18 requirement 5, produce every recorded request, dependency, database, financial, queue, reconciliation, image, deletion, deployment, backup, capacity, and external-service condition. For every external-service condition, confirm that the selected detection methods cover the complete condition and that any second method closes a recorded gap or supplies the required backup route. Attempt to emit user, object, external-service, request, variable URL, and free-form error values as metric labels and emit one unlisted fixed-vocabulary value. Record detection, delivery, metric acceptance or fallback, and all label values. Confirm coverage of each required condition, no unrelated provider notification, and a bounded vocabulary with no prohibited labels. Generate isolated ordinary failures and a repeated pattern of unauthorized, cross-user, concealed-resource, malformed-authentication, invalid-sensitive-input, and invalid-signature requests. Record the threshold, signal, notification, and affected request results; confirm the pattern is detected without personal content and isolated legitimate errors are not blocked by detection. For every security-critical external setting named in Pass 18 requirement 5, inspect its approved and live values and the chosen provider notification or scheduled comparison. Simulate an unapproved change in an isolated provider account or with a controlled notification, record the responsible-role delivery and disposition, and confirm that a changed repository value or an unaffected staging account cannot conceal production drift.

29. <a id="p20-v29"></a>**P20.V29 — Release-blocking indicator and deadline alerts**<br>For Pass 18 requirements 1–4, cross each release-blocking indicator and every processing deadline, leave each condition unresolved, then resolve it. Inspect thresholds, windows, the accountable Pass 01 role, notifications, procedures, queues, and dashboards. Confirm that alerts require action, repeated signals group, every stale operation remains visible with its next action, and dashboards keep each outcome separate.

30. <a id="p20-v30"></a>**P20.V30 — Safe representative log records**<br>For Pass 09 requirement 6, generate one safe representative record from each log source listed in the production architecture that supports such an event in the isolated staging release system, confirm arrival with its environment and release identity, interrupt delivery from each source whose delivery can be exercised safely, and inspect access and retention settings. For a source without a safe staging event or interruption method, record that limitation and use the provider-documentation and isolated-response procedure below without claiming observed delivery or interruption. Inspect the production log-delivery credential's scope and provider documentation; in a separate system using the same credential policy, attempt to read, modify, or delete an earlier test record and change access or retention settings. For an unavailable external-service log, exercise the recorded replacement signal. Confirm that an exercised delivery loss is detected, the workload credential cannot perform any of those actions, unauthorized access is denied, retention matches Pass 16, and every unavailable source has a working named detection path. Record application/worker OpenTelemetry delivery to the environment's Grafana stack separately from Render-native platform/deployment/runtime log availability in Render Pro. Inspect the named Render-native retention setting and current provider documentation for its 14-day history, and confirm that no automatic Render-to-Grafana forwarding or 30-day Render-log retention is claimed without observed delivery and a verified retention mechanism. Pass 21 verification 2 checks the deployed production sources and their required event types.

   Compare the source–event-type inventory with the exact requirements cited under Pass 09 requirement 6. For every required event type that can be generated safely in staging, generate that event and record its destination or alternate signal, environment, release identity, observed arrival, and detection of interrupted delivery. For a provider event that cannot be generated safely, record the provider's documented event behavior and inspect its named production setting read-only; exercise any assigned alternate signal in isolation. Where the final provider event or destination cannot be exercised safely, use Section 3.3's production-inspection, service-documentation, and isolated-response procedure to test Pickup Lane's handling of the documented event or failure. Label that result as provider-documentation and isolated-response evidence, not observed provider delivery. Record the evidence type and any remaining unverified delivery or absence-detection result for each pair; a required result without sufficient evidence remains incomplete. A successful record from one event type must not complete another type's check.

31. <a id="p20-v31"></a>**P20.V31 — Alert delivery and recovery**<br>For Pass 18 requirements 5 and 6, inject and recover each alert condition, leave the initial notification unacknowledged through escalation, repeat the condition, and activate each maintenance-suppression form through its expiry or removal event and latest time. Record primary and backup delivery, escalation, grouping, recovery, suppression, and underlying metric or queue state. Confirm each required condition reaches its recorded destination, suppression does not remove the application obligation, and suppression cannot remain active past its recorded end.

32. <a id="p20-v32"></a>**P20.V32 — Privileged and sensitive audit cases**<br>For Pass 09 requirements 8–10; Pass 18 requirement 8, exercise every listed privileged success, sensitive read, authorization denial, stale conflict, exact replay, changed replay, external-service failure, configuration change, and failed or outcome-unknown required audit write. Compare each persisted audit record with the source request, current actor and role, authoritative time, resource, action, result, stable failure code, correlation, environment, release, domain links, changed fields, and required structured reason. For each audit-write failure, confirm that an independent operational signal appears without depending on the failed write and that a linked failure record created after recovery describes the failure without claiming that the original write succeeded. Confirm complete coverage without unrestricted before-and-after snapshots.

   Inspect the explicit authentication-administration event inventory and event-specific schemas; exercise a planned provider-console change and an unavoidable emergency action with audit storage unavailable. Confirm operation identity, pre-action intent where possible, independent evidence and prompt reconciliation where not, and no routine sign-in mislabeled as an administration change.

33. <a id="p20-v33"></a>**P20.V33 — Audit failure and unknown-outcome cases**<br>For Pass 09 requirement 11, force audit failure and unknown audit-write outcome before each state-changing action and protected read, then retry external forwarding twice. Inspect protected state, returned fields, durable obligations, immutable audit IDs, and forwarded events. Confirm rollback or nondisclosure when the required record is not known to be committed and one forwarded event after duplicate delivery.

   For an external provider mutation, force pre-intent failure, post-effect audit failure, and unknown provider outcome separately. Record the original operation ID, provider evidence and local audit state, the API response or operator-facing result, reconciliation state, and any later retry. Confirm the first failure blocks dispatch; when the provider effect or post-action audit outcome is unknown, the caller or operator receives a recorded pending or unresolved result for the original operation, never unqualified success or a claim that the provider effect was rolled back. Confirm the later failures preserve provider evidence and raise the independent signal, reconciliation precedes retry or closure, and no case blindly repeats the mutation.

34. <a id="p20-v34"></a>**P20.V34 — Audit record access and immutability**<br>For Pass 09 requirements 12–14, attempt audit update, correction by overwrite, deletion, list, detail, wrong-parent read, excessive page request, unlisted filter, prohibited-field request, and access through a granular audit role using an ordinary application role, the administrator who created the event, and another active administrator. Inspect responses, stored records, and the audit event for each permitted read. Confirm immutable originals, linked corrections only, rejection of the prohibited role, consistent authorization, bounded fields and pages, no protected disclosure, and an audit record created before each permitted response.

35. <a id="p20-v35"></a>**P20.V35 — Audit alerts and review assignments**<br>For Pass 18 requirements 7 and 8, exercise every immediate alert and operational-review assignment, leave one queue item past its review deadline, and force an audit write, scheduled deletion, unauthorized access, and configured archive transfer to fail. Record routing, accountable role, timing, queue fields, response steps, and resolution. Confirm each required event remains visible without copying protected content.

36. <a id="p20-v36"></a>**P20.V36 — Restored deleted data and retention decisions**<br>For Pass 15 requirement 6, restore a backup taken before a completed deletion, a retention pause, and an approved hold, while workers, schedulers, and outbound processor changes remain disabled under Pass 20 requirement 21. Reapply each later decision and inspect every restored store before traffic is enabled. Confirm the deleted profile does not reappear, scoped retained records remain protected, and production traffic stays blocked until verification passes.

   Exercise deletion initiation with a valid Firebase ID token before and after the local inactive commit, and with Firebase disable/revocation delayed or unknown. Inspect the atomic local state and durable work, all protected API results, the pending external outcome, and the accepted response. Confirm immediate local denial, permitted status/recovery/support access, and no false claim that Firebase sign-in is already disabled.

   For each post-backup deletion, retention pause, and hold decision, exercise a successful protected journal write and protected read-back from the region required for restoration before acknowledging the decision. Separately make the journal write fail, the required-region read-back fail, the journal-write outcome unknown, and the read-back outcome unknown. In each case inspect the caller-visible response and whether the decision was reported as accepted, completed, or protected; the local decision and durable-operation state; journal persistence and required-region recoverability; and every required protective restriction. Confirm that no decision is reported as accepted, completed, or protected as a finalized decision without the required journal evidence. A deletion initiation that remains pending must retain the committed local inactive state and durable work, deny protected backend operations even with a valid Firebase ID token, and make no false claim that Firebase disablement or revocation has completed.

   Restore a backup predating a deletion, pause, and hold. Inspect protected S3 journal identities, order, required-region read-back, gap detection, replay, and final treatment. Confirm no traffic resumption while completeness or outcome is unknown.

37. <a id="p20-v37"></a>**P20.V37 — Privacy notice and collection order**<br>For Pass 16 requirement 7, map every privacy-notice and in-product statement to the inventory and implemented collection, disclosure, retention, and deletion behavior. Add one controlled mismatch and one undisclosed collection to the release check. In the isolated deployment of the production release build, open the approved public privacy notice without signing in and inspect each collection point identified by the recorded launch-state legal decision, using a synthetic account for a point that requires sign-in; inspect the notice content and the order of notice availability and collection for registration, payment, authentication persistence, and any enabled analytics or recording. Inspect the recorded analytics consent determination, read-only actual production processor settings, and the production release build's browser configuration without treating the isolated notice as proof that the production address serves it. In that isolated system, exercise analytics before disclosure and configuration verification, with consent absent, granted, and withdrawn where consent is required, and with an unapproved event or data field. Inspect browser storage, outbound requests, and processor-received events. Confirm the approved notice is publicly accessible in the isolated release deployment; each applicable pre-collection disclosure is available before its collection begins; analytics emits and persists nothing before its gate passes or after required consent is withdrawn; only approved events and data are received after activation; and each mismatch, missing required notice or consent, or unverified production setting blocks collection and release until corrected. Pass 21 verification 5 checks the actual deployed production notice and collection points before final acceptance.

38. <a id="p20-v38"></a>**P20.V38 — Nevada applicability verification**<br>For Pass 02 requirement 1 and Pass 16 requirement 9, inspect the recorded Nevada determination against the actual commercial service, Nevada-resident consumer use, covered information, Nevada connection, and each claimed statutory exclusion or notice exception; check any required Nevada notice under Pass 16 requirement 7. If covered, submit valid and invalid synthetic requests through the designated address, including an existing privacy email when selected; use a controlled clock to test commercially reasonable verification, a response within 60 days, a reasonably necessary extension with notice and response by day 90, and continued treatment of information collected after a verified request. Inspect current disclosures and any existing transfer that could meet NRS 603A.333; if no sale path exists, confirm that fact and that a later change could not be approved without honoring the verified request. Record receipt and response times, verification result, address delivery and monitoring, data-transfer result, and any claimed exception. Confirm that invalid requests do not receive another consumer's information or count as verified, every verified request meets its deadline, and no covered information about an opted-out consumer is sold after verification. If not covered, record which statutory element is absent or which exclusion applies; do not claim this scenario proves a Nevada request duty.

39. <a id="p20-v39"></a>**P20.V39 — Privacy-law applicability verification**<br>For Pass 16 requirement 7, inspect the prelaunch federal and state privacy-law determination against the current statutory text, effective dates, recorded launch scope, and facts used for every jurisdictional, entity, size, revenue, consumer-count, sensitive-data, sale or sharing, processing, and exclusion criterion. For each applicable law and affected processing activity, trace every identified launch duty to its exact master requirement, verification scenario, and observed evidence or to the justified amendment that makes it verifiable. Inspect each supported exclusion and future-conditional trigger; introduce a controlled missing current fact and changes that cross a threshold, add a served jurisdiction, change data processing or provider use, and change a governing legal rule. Record the resulting classification, reassessment date, duty mapping, and signoff result. Confirm that nationwide registration alone does not establish coverage, an unknown current fact cannot establish nonapplicability, verified unmet triggers do not create speculative duties, each applicable duty has a verified result before signoff, and a changed trigger or law reopens the affected determination and checks.

40. <a id="p20-v40"></a>**P20.V40 — Secret inventory and exposure scan**<br>For Pass 01 requirements 3, 6; Pass 03 requirements 1 and 2, inventory every secret and browser-public setting, plus every Pickup Lane-generated secret or unguessable capability and each selected first-party or provider cryptographic owner; inspect the creating code and documented provider behavior for approved random generation and the 128-bit threshold, then scan source history, build inputs and artifacts, runtime images, client bundles, source maps, logs, planning evidence, and browser output with controlled detectable values. Compare each deployed component's injection path and environment binding with the production architecture. Inspect the environment record for local development, automated test, CI, staging, and production and confirm that it contains no preview environment. Confirm no private value appears; each public value is classified and restricted for its exact type; local development uses only its local components and assigned non-production external resources; ordinary automated tests and CI use their dedicated test databases and simulated external responses without staging or production credentials; external-service verification uses the assigned staging resources; staging and production resources and credentials are separate; and no credential or resource assigned to one context can read or change another context.

41. <a id="p20-v41"></a>**P20.V41 — External account access inspection**<br>For Pass 03 requirement 3; Pass 19 requirements 3 and 4, review Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL, application hosting, DNS, repository, CI, monitoring, and backup permission inventories and attempt every operation each API, worker, migration, backup, monitoring, CI, deployment, diagnostic, and human role lists as permitted or prohibited. Inspect support for short-lived authentication for each CI and workload operation and inspect what credential application-default credential loading resolves to. Confirm exact least privilege, separate credentials for different grant sets, named MFA-protected human access, use of a provider-issued short-lived credential when supported, and treatment of application-default credentials backed by a stored private key as a long-lived key with every required control.

42. <a id="p20-v42"></a>**P20.V42 — Administrator privilege lifecycle**<br>For Pass 01 requirement 2; Pass 10 requirement 8; Pass 18 requirement 11; Pass 19 requirements 1 and 2, exercise initial account bootstrap, normal grant, temporary grant, emergency elevation, expiry, role reduction, revocation, offboarding, personnel reassignment, periodic review, and attempted use after each removal. Inspect requester, approver, recipient, scope, times, result, account attribution, MFA, review findings, audit, and last recoverable administrator path. Confirm no shared account, access outside recorded scope or time, missed offboarding deadline, or removal of the final tested recovery route.

   For a planned production provider-console access change, inspect the durable Pass 09 intent before the action and its linked provider evidence and result afterward. For an unavoidable emergency with audit storage unavailable, inspect independent incident/provider evidence, the later reconciled audit record, and the unresolved signal; confirm no fabricated pre-action intent or rollback claim.

43. <a id="p20-v43"></a>**P20.V43 — Backup actor execution**<br>For Pass 01 requirement 2; Pass 19 requirement 3, remove each primary accountable person from the exercise and use the recorded second person or vendor escalation route to recover administrative access to registrar and DNS, repository and CI, Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL hosting, application hosting, monitoring, backups, and incident command. Confirm each route remains usable without the unavailable primary and without a shared credential. For a database restore, a financial reconciliation, a credential compromise, and a production rollback, also require the named alternate or escalation route to perform the application-specific safe response in isolation without instructions or credentials held only by the missing primary operator. Record completion time against the applicable objectives and any action the route could not perform; account login alone does not pass.

   Rotate and revoke a synthetic provider credential with a normal pre-action audit intent, then force a post-action audit failure and an unavoidable emergency audit outage. Inspect provider state, operation IDs, independent failure signal, preserved evidence, and reconciliation before any repeat or closure.

44. <a id="p20-v44"></a>**P20.V44 — Secret rotation procedures**<br>For Pass 03 requirement 1; Pass 18 requirement 9, rotate each secret type through its recorded replacement and rollout sequence while its consumer is operating, then attempt use with the old value and simulate failed rollout to one consumer. Record consumer adoption, service availability, monitoring, rollback or recovery, and old-value rejection. Confirm no unplanned outage, no consumer left unknowingly on the old value, and successful revocation after adoption.

45. <a id="p20-v45"></a>**P20.V45 — Release reproducibility and dependency changes**<br>For Pass 20 requirements 6 and 7, 15, build the same revision twice through the release process, then change one dependency lock, build setting, migration head, and runtime input separately. Inspect every frontend, API, worker, scheduler, migration, and image identity, package and operating-system version, hosting deployment, prior rollback artifact, diagnostics, logs, and error report. Confirm traceability to the exact inputs, changed identity when an input changes, and the required software-bill or signing behavior only when its trigger exists.

46. <a id="p20-v46"></a>**P20.V46 — Adjacent-version deployment rehearsal**<br>For Pass 20 requirements 8–11, 14, deploy adjacent frontend, API, worker, scheduler, and database-schema versions through every step and coexistence combination in the recorded procedure. Force configuration, schema, migration, artifact, health, traffic, post-deploy, rollback, and forward-fix gates to fail one at a time. Force `/ready` to fail while `/live` succeeds and then force `/live` to fail. With Render's `/ready` health check configured in staging, send controlled PostgreSQL-dependent requests and record any routed to an already-serving instance before withdrawal after 15 consecutive seconds of failed checks, its restart after 60 consecutive seconds of failed checks even while `/live` succeeds, and safe handling without prohibited effects during that interval. When `/live` is forced to fail, record the `/ready` result and Render behavior without treating `/live` as a separate restart controller. Record step blocking, traffic removal, restart behavior, compatibility, rollback, and forward fix. Confirm no later release step starts until the failed requirement is corrected and the same gate passes, except the containment, rollback, or forward-fix action required by the failure. Confirm that authorization to deploy requires the pre-deployment gates but leaves production-safe post-deploy checks pending, and that final acceptance cannot occur until those checks pass. For the first public launch, verify that synthetic checks can run while ordinary user activity remains blocked. For later updates, record whether Render routed production requests to the new release after readiness and before synthetic checks completed.

   As pre-deployment qualification for Pass 21 requirement 1, prepare and inspect its approved procedure version and rehearse every listed production-safe operation and its artifact treatment in the isolated release deployment. Record each starting state, input, expected and actual API and external-service result, prohibited-effect check, safety control, cleanup result, and release identity. Confirm that a procedure or operation without a passing rehearsal and a demonstrably safe production method remains incomplete and blocks authorization to deploy; the deployed production results remain pending for Pass 21.

47. <a id="p20-v47"></a>**P20.V47 — Capacity and spending calculation**<br>For Pass 19 requirement 6; Pass 20 requirements 12 and 13, calculate every listed capacity, quota, autoscaling, and spending input and run the final request, booking, payment, webhook, job, image, and administrative-query mix to the first limit or correctness failure. Record service limits and sources, baseline, warning and hard thresholds, tested range, first exhausted resource, largest passing load, planned peak, operating difference, scaling action, cost role, and notifications. Confirm the planned peak remains inside every blocking limit and each alert arrives before interruption or uncontrolled spending. Produce one month-equivalent itemized estimate for local development, staging, and production from the selected actual plan sizes, allowances, and measured workload, and compare their combined forecast with the approved $195/month planning ceiling. Exercise a threshold-crossing projection for API capacity, authentication usage, log ingestion, R2 operations, outbound email, and backup storage. Confirm each increase is visible before commitment, and do not declare the $195 ceiling satisfied from an illustrative subtotal or from free-tier assumptions.

48. <a id="p20-v48"></a>**P20.V48 — Environment and credential isolation**<br>For Pass 01 requirement 4; Pass 06 requirement 1; Pass 19 requirement 5; Pass 21 requirement 3, compare every resource listed for local development, automated test, CI, staging, and production and every entry point, private dependency, region, process responsibility, command, worker count, concurrency, keep-alive, recycling, shutdown, instance, autoscaling, restart, and durable-state location with the configured production control-plane settings and production-equivalent staging release. Confirm that the environment record contains no preview environment. Inspect production account bindings to confirm that no non-production credential is trusted. In isolated resources with the same policies, attempt cross-environment access and attempt to store authoritative state in process memory. Inspect configured outbound service destinations, use one required allowed destination, then submit a client-controlled URL and an external redirect in isolation; confirm that neither changes the backend destination or causes a new unapproved external request. Confirm separate staging and production resources and credentials, no non-production production access, shared durable authority, and process settings that fit hosting and PostgreSQL limits through rolling overlap. Pass 21 verification 2 compares the deployed runtime with these settings.

   In the isolated staging release path, exercise DNS change, canonical-host behavior, certificate issuance and renewal, CDN or cache behavior, direct-origin access, selected region and data-residency settings, administrative-account recovery, and one failure for every recorded single point of failure. Exercise a measured direct-origin bypass and a provider limit that can defeat a required availability, webhook, financial, capacity, or deadline outcome. Record each result, the named platform/security materiality decision, monitoring, response, and unresolved blocker. Confirm that an assumed purchase or residual-risk note does not waive a failed master outcome. Pass 21 verification 2 inspects the named production settings and uses only production-safe requests against the deployed host.

49. <a id="p20-v49"></a>**P20.V49 — Runtime image security support**<br>For Pass 20 requirement 16, when a runtime image is used, inspect its base support status, build and runtime contents, user and privilege mode, mounts or sockets, CPU, memory, process, capability, and writable-path limits; exercise one limit and the security-update deadline. Confirm prohibited content and privilege are absent and limits are enforced. When no image is used, confirm the architecture record identifies the deployed runtime and states why the image-specific checks do not apply.

   Inspect the actual Render deployment mode and artifact owner for each component. Test image-specific controls only for an application-controlled Docker image and record the inapplicability basis for native or static deployment; an undecided mode leaves this evidence incomplete.

50. <a id="p20-v50"></a>**P20.V50 — Rollback evidence and results**<br>For Pass 20 requirements 6, 8, 11, 14, execute rollback using the prior artifact and configuration identities after traffic has begun and execute a forward fix for a non-reversible migration. Record database compatibility, component identities, health, post-deploy results, and user-visible state. Confirm the release returns to one documented compatible state without losing a required migration or business obligation.

51. <a id="p20-v51"></a>**P20.V51 — Approved tier targets**<br>Inspect the recovery-tier record for every required category's essential or replaceable classification, exact records and systems, approved target, and reason a replaceable category can be absent during safe resumption. For each essential category, compare the simulated failure time with the consistent verified restorable source-state time, record the numeric interval and missing committed changes, and confirm the interval is at most 60 minutes. Check each replaceable category against its separately approved treatment; do not infer recoverability from backup creation or replication time alone.

    **Oregon recovery-point selection.** Also select the newest verified restorable Oregon PostgreSQL backup copy and record its type, recoverable source-state time, creation or export and upload duration, completion time, Oregon replication delay, validation time and result, and age at the simulated regional loss. Inspect the production backup and replication settings read-only.

    **Isolated tier restoration and failure cases.** For Pass 01 requirement 1; Pass 15 requirements 1–3, restore each recovery tier into an isolated environment from the oldest recovery point (the dated state to which data can be restored) that must satisfy its objective. Simulate loss or corruption of PostgreSQL state, Stripe and Firebase continuity, worker state, audit access, and R2 objects. Record the selected backup or instant, achieved data-loss interval, elapsed recovery time, reconstructed external state, image fallback, and each tier invariant.

    **Isolated Oregon restore.** Restore that copy in isolation after simulated Ohio loss and record its missing data and elapsed time through the required safe-traffic checks.

    **Compatible point-in-time recovery when required.** If scheduled backup copies alone cannot meet the data-loss objective, separately restore to the selected instant using a compatible physical base backup and archived WAL or a documented provider-managed equivalent; record that recovery point, missing data, and elapsed time through the same checks.

    **Data-loss and safe-resumption results.** Confirm the one-hour loss and eight-hour resumption objectives for the oldest required tier point and the selected Oregon recovery path, record the backup-copy-only result separately when point-in-time recovery is required, and confirm the exact replaceable-image treatment.



   Record incident detection, credible restoration evidence, named formal declaration, and safe-resumption time separately. Confirm the eight-hour clock begins at declaration and the detection-to-declaration interval meets its own recorded boundary without backdating or postponement.

52. <a id="p20-v52"></a>**P20.V52 — Backup failure and corruption cases**<br>For Pass 15 requirements 3 and 4, force a missed and failed backup, corrupt one isolated copy, attempt read, alteration, and deletion against isolated copies in both Ohio and Oregon with API, worker, migration, ordinary administrator, backup, and restore credentials, and perform one authorized restore. Inspect both production regions read-only for encryption, versioning, Object Lock mode and retention, replication status, role permissions, monitoring, and restore readiness. In isolation, exercise both regional copy policies, their denied and permitted credential actions, a damaged or unavailable copy in each region, and authorized restoration from each remaining usable copy. Inspect encryption, role permissions, monitoring, retention, transaction-log window, and recovery dependencies. Confirm detection, rejection of every unauthorized action, and a usable authorized copy within the approved data-loss objective.

53. <a id="p20-v53"></a>**P20.V53 — Stale restored obligation safeguards**<br>For Pass 20 requirements 17 and 18, 20 and 21, restore a backup containing stale jobs, webhooks, schedules, idempotency records, a completed later deletion, a later retention pause or hold, and mismatched Stripe, Firebase, and R2 state. Keep traffic and all outbound mutation disabled, run every listed invariant and reconciliation check, then re-enable workers, schedulers, outbound operations, and traffic in the recorded order. Confirm the deletion and retention decisions are reapplied, external and local states agree, and no duplicate charge, refund, booking, message, deletion, or image operation occurs.

   Remove one decision-journal event and make one required-region read-back uncertain before isolated restore. Confirm the completeness check stops traffic, workers, and outbound mutation until the gap or uncertainty is resolved and all post-backup deletion, pause, and hold decisions have been replayed.

54. <a id="p20-v54"></a>**P20.V54 — Recovery tabletop and response deadlines**<br>For Pass 18 requirements 12, 14, 17; Pass 20 requirements 19, 22, conduct the full recovery tabletop with every primary and backup role, exceed one response and recovery objective, make both an external-notification and no-notification decision, expose one recovery gap, assign corrective work, and verify the correction in the repeated affected exercise. Record severity, detection, escalation, containment, supporting facts, decision deadlines and times, communications, recovery authority, closure criteria, and recurrence check. Confirm the incident remains open until every criterion is met. Repeat the restore, payment reconciliation, and credential-containment decisions with the primary operator unavailable; require the assigned alternate to perform the time-critical steps under the actual recovery instructions and record the observed deadline, access limitation, or blocked action, rather than treating notification delivery or login recovery as success.

   Exercise each recorded restoration-decision condition with its accepted evidence and named primary or backup authority. Record the detection event and time, evidence, decision, actor, declaration time when recovery is declared, and the numeric detection-to-declaration boundary. Then delay a declaration beyond that boundary; confirm the delay is reported as a missed response target, a no-restore decision requires recorded supporting evidence, and the eight-hour recovery clock is never backdated or extended by postponing declaration.

55. <a id="p20-v55"></a>**P20.V55 — Isolated system rebuild**<br>For Pass 15 requirement 5, rebuild an isolated environment using only the recovery material and the named secret-recreation paths. Compare application, DNS, Firebase, Stripe, R2, PostgreSQL, hosting, monitoring, and infrastructure settings with the production architecture record and scan the recovery material for secret values. Confirm that the instructions reconstruct every listed setting without containing a copied secret.

   Rebuild the protected S3 decision-journal access and replay path from recovery material, test required-region read-back and ordered completeness, and verify that no protected journal value or secret is copied into that material.

56. <a id="p20-v56"></a>**P20.V56 — External-service incident runbooks**<br>For Pass 18 requirements 15 and 16, trigger or simulate every external-service condition mapped by Pass 18 requirement 5 and exercise each runbook for API or database outage, connection exhaustion, failed deployment or migration, worker backlog or terminal failure, Stripe webhook or payment mismatch, R2 upload or credential failure, Firebase outage, secret compromise, certificate expiry, backup failure, restore, user-deletion failure, and domain, DNS, and external-account recovery. Record the Pass 18 detection method, primary and backup delivery, degraded application behavior, containment, escalation, and recovery result. Confirm every required condition and incident class has complete detection, a working route, and explicit application behavior without an unrelated or duplicate alert.

57. <a id="p20-v57"></a>**P20.V57 — Incident record inspection**<br>For Pass 18 requirement 13, inspect the incident record produced by each verification scenario for timeline, systems, users, data, decisions, evidence, containment, recovery validation, communications, and corrective work. Insert a controlled missing field and confirm closure is rejected until it is completed.

58. <a id="p20-v58"></a>**P20.V58 — Recurring exercise interval and triggers**<br>For Pass 20 requirements 19, 22, advance through one complete recurring exercise interval and separately trigger each change and incident condition listed in those requirements. Inspect the exercise schedule and completion records. Confirm every required path runs within the interval and every affected path repeats after its trigger rather than waiting for the next cycle.

59. <a id="p20-v59"></a>**P20.V59 — Stripe event and checkout cases**<br>For Pass 13 requirements 5, 6, 19, and 24; Pass 17 requirement 22; and Pass 19 requirement 7, send a valid subscribed Stripe event, duplicate event, out-of-order event, invalid signature, wrong endpoint secret, malformed body, unsupported event type, and event requiring work longer than the HTTP deadline. Record raw-body verification, acknowledgment time, durable event or job identity, transition result, and configured production account, mode, versions, endpoint, and subscribed event set. Without signing in, inspect the public business, service, currency, contact, Terms of Service, refund, dispute, and applicable cancellation disclosures, then follow a checkout to its confirmation point and compare the displayed terms and rules with the implemented official-game booking, payment, refund, credit, and dispute outcomes. Make one required disclosure inaccessible and separately omit required terms acceptance during isolated checkouts; inspect the payment request and Stripe state. Confirm that each required disclosure is accessible before payment confirmation and matches the actual result, no payment is requested when one is missing or required terms are unaccepted, only a valid event changes state once, acknowledgment occurs only after durable recording, long work continues durably, and every invalid or unsupported event creates no business change.

   Run the active webhook and checkout cases in Pass 20 verification 59 with the production release build and the dedicated staging Stripe general sandbox and the approved version at the isolated staging public address. Inspect the production Stripe account binding read-only. Pass 21 verification 4 checks the deployed production Terms address and version.

60. <a id="p20-v60"></a>**P20.V60 — Versioned Terms registration cases**<br>For Pass 07 requirement 14, use the production release build with the legally approved, versioned Terms content in an isolated system to exercise email and Google registration, a direct account-creation API request, hosting and joining each game type, and official-game booking and payment with the current terms accepted, acceptance absent, a claimed acceptance supplied only by the client, an unavailable document, and a superseded version. Publish one material participation or liability revision and one nonmaterial correction under the recorded legal decisions, then retry each affected operation through the browser and directly through the API.
