Record the text and version presented, affirmative action, authenticated account, server acceptance time, applicable activity and renewal decision, response, and database and Stripe effects. Confirm that a valid acceptance permits its covered operation once, every required missing or stale acceptance blocks the affected operation before a game, booking, or payment effect, a nonmaterial correction follows its recorded decision, prior acceptance evidence still identifies the text accepted, and legal, recovery, support, and deletion access remains available.

#### Completion condition

Every pre-deployment verification scenario and permitted read-only production-setting check required for the exact release has passing, release-linked evidence. An unresolved or failed predeployment gate blocks production deployment. Checks that require application code on the deployed production host, including final response headers, redirects, HSTS, TLS behavior, production Terms and privacy-notice publication and collection points, startup and log delivery, remain incomplete and close only in Pass 21 before final acceptance.

---

<a id="p21"></a>

### Pass 21 — Production Deployment and Final Acceptance

#### Purpose

Authorize and execute the production deployment, inspect final provider/DNS/security bindings, perform strictly production-safe post-deploy checks, and record final acceptance.

#### Required behavior

1. <a id="p21-r01"></a>**P21.R01 — Production-safe post-deploy checks**<br>After deployment readiness and required health conditions pass, production-safe post-deploy checks must run against the deployed release before final production acceptance. They must use a dedicated synthetic account and identifiable test records to exercise Firebase authentication, public read, protected read, PostgreSQL write, durable-job execution, Stripe connectivity without a customer charge, Cloudflare R2 connectivity without making an object public, and telemetry delivery.

   Before deployment, the Pass 01 release-assurance responsibility and the owner of each affected operation must approve one versioned procedure covering every required production-safe operation. For each operation, the procedure must identify its starting state, synthetic identity, input, existing Pickup Lane or provider operation, expected API and external-service results, prohibited effects and how each is checked, controls that prevent effects on real users or material consumption of limited production capacity, and the final treatment of every artifact it may create. The exact procedure version and production release build must pass an isolated rehearsal before that operation is authorized in production. A missing procedure, failed rehearsal, or operation without a demonstrably safe method remains incomplete and blocks deployment authorization until resolved; no test-only product workflow is implied.

   Before execution, the check record must list every account, database record, job, object, provider test artifact, audit record, and telemetry item it can create and assign each an allowed terminal treatment: deletion where permitted, retention under its required category with a test identity and access restriction, or a named reconciled state that prevents further business or provider effects. The check must verify and record each artifact's terminal state and report any failed or unknown treatment as an unresolved cleanup item; a retained audit or other required record must not be deleted merely to clean up a test. Any financial check must use a Stripe-supported method that cannot charge a customer. A failed or incomplete check, including unresolved cleanup, blocks final acceptance and invokes the failure response in Pass 20 requirement 8.

2. <a id="p21-r02"></a>**P21.R02 — Final external-system inspection**<br>Final external-system verification must cover hosting/edge, Firebase/Google Cloud, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL hosting, DNS/registrar, repository/CI, Postmark notifications, PostHog analytics, Grafana monitoring/logging, and AWS S3 Ohio/Oregon backups using Section 3.5. Postmark binding evidence must agree with the Pass 11 transactional-email checks, and PostHog binding evidence must agree with the Pass 16 privacy and analytics checks.

    For each production storage system holding personal, private-message, financial, moderation, audit, authentication, or secret data classified under Pass 02 requirement 2, including PostgreSQL, its managed recovery copies, applicable object storage, and backup storage, the record must identify whether encryption at rest is provider-enforced or configurable and verify that it protects the stored data.

    Provider-managed encryption satisfies this requirement; application-level field encryption is not required by it. A covered store without verified encryption at rest blocks production signoff.

    Backup encryption also follows Pass 15 requirement 4.

    Every backend connection carrying credentials or protected application data must use the selected provider's documented private, access-controlled path or TLS with normal certificate-chain and hostname verification to its recorded endpoint.

    A connection over the public network must not fall back to plaintext or accept an invalid or wrong-host certificate; certificate checks must not be disabled to make a connection succeed.

    For every public-network backend connection, the selected provider must support TLS 1.2 or 1.3 with an authenticated, forward-secret AEAD cipher suite. AEAD (authenticated encryption with associated data) detects tampering; forward secrecy protects earlier connections if a long-term key is later exposed. The client must not negotiate an older protocol or weaker suite.

    For Render internal PostgreSQL, the documented private-network path and database authentication may establish this boundary without treating its self-signed TLS certificate as a publicly trusted certificate.

    If the selected application architecture includes HTTP communication between internal services carrying credentials or protected data, that HTTP service-to-service path must use TLS with a verified internal certificate or a documented provider-managed equivalent transport-encryption mechanism; the Render private PostgreSQL exception applies only to its database protocol and does not excuse plaintext internal HTTP.

3. <a id="p21-r03"></a>**P21.R03 — Public routing and transport verification**<br>Before signoff, verification must cover DNS resolution and change, canonical-host behavior, certificate issuance and renewal, CDN or cache behavior, direct-origin protection (protection of a directly reachable hosting address that can bypass the canonical edge), selected region and data-residency setting, administrative-account recovery, and the recorded response to each single point of failure.

    Every public frontend, API, direct-origin, and alternate provider hostname must negotiate only TLS 1.2 or 1.3 with authenticated, forward-secret AEAD cipher suites; the record must identify the provider-controlled protocol and cipher policy and reject a hostname that still negotiates an older protocol or weaker suite.

    For each public frontend and API hostname and any directly reachable origin or alternate provider hostname, the architecture record must identify the actual upstream denial-of-service protection, the traffic and service layers it covers, documented limits and failure behavior, and whether any route bypasses that protection.

    Compare those facts with the selected hosting configuration, required service deliveries and health checks, Pass 18 requirement 18 traffic controls, and Pass 20 requirement 12 capacity evidence. Record the remaining availability, cost, and false-rejection risk and the decision that accepts it or names a correction before signoff.

    For a provider mitigation or traffic-exhaustion event, the operating response must identify its Pass 18 detection signal, the Pass 01 responsible role, provider escalation route, handling of required service deliveries, and observable recovery condition.

    An unresolved material protection gap or missing detection or response must block signoff; an undocumented protection claim cannot establish coverage.

    This requirement does not prescribe a new edge product.

    Public frontend, edge, and API file-serving paths must not expose directory listings of private or nonpublic asset roots, including through trailing slashes, encoded paths or fallback routes.

    Public frontend, edge, and API file-serving paths, including direct-origin and application-fallback paths, must not return the contents of `.env` files, `.git/config`, private keys, service-account files, internal configuration, backups, or any file outside the recorded public asset set.

    Every check must pass before production signoff.

   A direct-origin or other uncovered traffic path is a material availability or abuse gap when measured traffic or a provider limit shows that it can defeat required availability, financial or webhook delivery, capacity protection, or a recorded deadline. The named Pass 01 platform/security responsibility must record the path, measurement, provider control, remaining risk, and correction or unresolved release blocker. It may record residual-risk acceptance only when every master outcome still passes; a purchased control is not presumed, and a failed master outcome cannot be waived by that acceptance.

4. <a id="p21-r04"></a>**P21.R04 — Production Terms publication**<br>Before final production acceptance, Pickup Lane must publish the final, versioned Terms of Service implemented under Pass 17 requirement 22 at a public production address available without sign-in and reachable before account activation, game hosting or joining, and official-game booking or payment. The deployed text and incorporated policies must match the approved version and actual signup, hosting, game, booking, payment, refund, and dispute behavior. A missing, inaccessible, placeholder, superseded, or materially mismatched document must block final production acceptance and the affected account or payment operation. Earlier published versions must remain identifiable and reproducible for the acceptance evidence required by Pass 07 requirement 14.

#### Verification

1. <a id="p21-v01"></a>**P21.V01 — Approved production-safe check rehearsal**<br>For Pass 21 requirement 1, inspect the approved procedure version and its Pass 20 verification 46 isolated rehearsal for every production-safe operation before deployment; compare its release identity, starting state, synthetic identity, input, existing operation, expected API and external-service results, prohibited-effect checks, safety controls, and final artifact treatment with the operation about to run. An absent or failed procedure or rehearsal blocks authorization to deploy. After readiness and required health conditions pass, execute only those approved operations against the deployed release with the dedicated synthetic account and identifiable records. Before each operation, confirm its starting state and artifact inventory, including the assigned terminal treatment for each possible account, database record, job, object, provider test artifact, audit record, and telemetry item. For Firebase authentication, public and protected reads, PostgreSQL write, job completion, noncharging Stripe connectivity, private R2 connectivity, and telemetry delivery, record the actual API and provider result, release identity, prohibited-effect checks, and each created artifact's identity, final state, treatment check, and any unresolved cleanup item. In an isolated deployment using the production release build, force each cleanup step to fail once and exercise its visible failure, recovery, and final reconciliation; do not deliberately create those failures in production. Confirm each result matches its approved expected result, no customer charge or public object occurs, complete result linkage, no deletion of a required retained record, and final production acceptance is blocked by any failed, unknown, or incomplete operation or artifact treatment. Confirm that an incomplete or failed check triggers the required failure response.

2. <a id="p21-v02"></a>**P21.V02 — Production provider and routing inspection**<br>For Pass 21 requirements 2 and 3, inspect the final hosting edge, Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL, DNS or registrar, repository and CI, Postmark, PostHog, Grafana monitoring and logging, and Ohio/Oregon S3 backup settings under Section 3.5.

   **Storage encryption:** For each covered storage system, inspect the named production encryption setting when configurable or record the provider documentation that establishes enforced encryption for the selected service, and confirm that the inspected production resource uses that service or setting; include PostgreSQL data and recovery copies, applicable object storage, and backups.

   **Backend transport:** Inspect each protected backend connection's production endpoint, supported TLS versions and cipher suites, and transport configuration against the provider documentation.

   **Public TLS:** Inspect the provider's public-host TLS policy and make production-safe handshakes for each public and alternate hostname with supported and obsolete protocols and suites.

   **Deployed HTTP policy and headers:** For Pass 06 requirements 9–11; Pass 17 requirement 2, send each listed application HTML, static-file, API, download, and Stripe or Firebase return-response kind through the deployed final public request path using production-safe requests and inspect the response received by the caller. Record every redirect and effective `Content-Security-Policy`, `Strict-Transport-Security`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, frame restriction, `Permissions-Policy`, `Cache-Control`, browser-origin, and security-sensitive `Set-Cookie` header; inspect `Secure`, `SameSite`, applicable `HttpOnly`, `__Host-` or `__Secure-` prefix, `Path`, and `Domain`. Confirm that the result matches the recorded policy, contains no conflicting header fields, and does not redirect repeatedly. A wrongly scoped cookie must not satisfy the policy; exercise that rejection in the isolated Pass 20 verification 1.

   Confirm effective `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` on public frontend and API responses. For every Pickup Lane-controlled production hostname, inspect HTTPS HSTS `max-age` of at least `31536000` seconds and the recorded subdomain inventory; confirm that `includeSubDomains` protects only a domain whose affected subdomains can all serve valid HTTPS, or record the exact incompatibility and narrower-policy exception. Record the provider-managed HSTS result for every shared provider-owned hostname without imposing a policy on its parent domain.

   Send production-safe allowed and unlisted browser-origin, supported and unlisted host, and caller-supplied proxy-header requests through the deployed public path and any directly reachable origin. Record acceptance or rejection and confirm that forged information does not alter routing, authorization, redirect destinations, or request attribution; the active forged-header and protected-operation cases remain in isolated Pass 20 verification 1. For each public and alternate hostname, inspect the production certificate and provider-managed TLS behavior and make a production-safe HTTPS and HTTP request. Confirm that valid HTTPS succeeds and HTTP performs no protected operation or disclosure; the expired- and wrong-host-certificate cases remain in isolated Pass 20 verification 1.

   Send production-safe allowed and unlisted CORS preflights for a classified protected-read route through the deployed path, then use a dedicated synthetic account under the production-safety and cleanup conditions of Pass 21 verification 1 for the actual protected read with valid App Check proof. Record preflight and actual-request statuses and CORS headers separately. Confirm that the allowed preflight needs no proof, an unlisted origin receives no authorizing CORS headers, and the actual protected read still requires its valid user credential and authorization and follows the inspected production App Check mode; `enforced` mode must require valid proof. The active invalid-proof and combined-fault exercises remain in isolated Pass 20 verification 6 and Pass 06 verification 3.

   In isolation, attempt plaintext fallback and an invalid or wrong-host certificate on public-network connections, and verify that the Render internal PostgreSQL path stays private and requires its database credential. Inspect every internal HTTP connection, if present, for verified TLS or documented provider-managed transport encryption; verify that the private PostgreSQL transport exception does not authorize unencrypted internal HTTP.

   Record the negotiated version and suite or rejection for each TLS check and the connection or rejection and confirm that no protected data or credential is sent over an unapproved path.

   **Routing and recovery:** Inspect the named production DNS, canonical-host, certificate-renewal, CDN or cache, direct-origin, region or residency, administrative-account-recovery, and single-point-of-failure settings. Use production-safe requests to confirm the deployed routing and compare each setting with the isolated failure and change exercises in Pass 20 verification 48; no disruptive change or failure is exercised against production.

   **Protection:** Inspect each public and alternate hostname's production routing and protection setting and the provider's current documentation for protection scope, limits, mitigation behavior, and available signals; do not infer that a canonical-domain rule also covers an alternate hostname.

   **Deployed log sources:** Inspect the production application/worker OpenTelemetry destination in Grafana separately from Render's native platform/deployment/runtime log explorer. Use the production-safe synthetic operations in Pass 21 verification 1 to identify the deployed release's application and worker records and the corresponding Render-native service or deployment records; record their actual destinations, arrival or absence, access and retention settings, and delivery-gap signal. For every other required production source–event-type pair in Pass 09 requirement 6, inspect the named production destination, access, retention, and missing-delivery setting; observe an event and its arrival through the actual production pipeline only when a production-safe operation can generate it. Otherwise record why production generation is unsafe or unavailable, inspect the relevant provider documentation and production setting, and use the matching isolated event or response exercise from Pass 20 verification 30, including any assigned alternate detection signal. Label observed production delivery, observed staging delivery, exercised alternate detection, and documentation plus isolated-response evidence separately; none proves a stronger boundary than it exercised. Leave a required event or delivery-gap result incomplete if those methods do not establish it. Inspect the selected Render Pro native log-history setting and provider documentation for the current 14-day boundary. Do not claim automatic Render-to-Grafana forwarding or 30-day retention without observed delivery and a verified retention mechanism; apply the Pass 09 requirement 6 fallback when a required source cannot deliver a log.

   In an isolated deployment, use bounded requests and injected provider-documented mitigation or rate-limit responses to check the Pass 18 alert, responsible-role delivery, provider-escalation procedure, required-service handling, and recovery observation without generating denial-of-service traffic.

   Record the comparison with Pass 18 requirement 18 and capacity evidence, residual-risk decision, and every uncovered or unknown path; confirm that no undocumented protection claim or unresolved material protection, detection, or response gap passes signoff.

   **Public files:** Inspect production public asset roots, route mounts, and edge configuration without reading private file contents and confirm that no file outside the recorded public asset set is present in a public root; in an isolated deployment with the production release artifact and matching routing, place synthetic marker files outside the permitted public roots and request `.env`, `.git/config`, private-key, service-account, internal-configuration, and backup paths through frontend, edge, API, encoded-path, nested-path, direct-origin, and application-fallback forms.

   Request a synthetic private directory with a trailing slash and encoded path, and confirm that no directory contents or listing are returned. Record each file and directory response; confirm that no marker or protected file content is returned by the file requests and no listing or private directory content is returned by the directory requests.

   Confirm the observed production settings and response and confirm that every check passes.

   Inspect the measured direct-origin bypass and provider-limit results from isolated Pass 20 verification 48 against the deployed production settings. Inspect the named platform/security materiality decision, evidence, monitoring, response, and unresolved blocker; confirm an assumed purchase or residual-risk note does not waive a failed master outcome.

3. <a id="p21-v03"></a>**P21.V03 — Configuration startup failure cases**<br>For Pass 04 requirements 2–4, use the production release build in an isolated system to start each process once with a complete valid test configuration, then separately omit, corrupt, or replace each setting with a resource from the wrong environment. Record that the complete configuration starts, each incorrect configuration stops before accepting a request or job, and no failed attempt contacts or changes an external resource. For final production evidence, inspect the deployed setting names and environment bindings without copying their secret contents, then record one successful startup through the release procedure. Confirm that production exposes no debug route, test route, reload process, development server, or publicly accessible API documentation.

4. <a id="p21-v04"></a>**P21.V04 — Published Terms and acceptance check**<br>For Pass 21 requirement 4, request the deployed production Terms of Service without signing in and inspect the version and text reached from account activation, game hosting or joining, and official-game booking or payment. Compare the deployed version with the Pass 02 requirement 6 approval, the Pass 17 requirement 22 release build, and the implemented flows and Pass 13 requirement 19 disclosures. Record the response, version, access path, and any unavailable or mismatched content. Confirm that a missing, placeholder, superseded, inaccessible, or materially mismatched version blocks final acceptance.

   If an earlier approved Terms version was published in production, retrieve its retained text through an existing read-only, authorized production path and compare the text or its immutable digest with that version's approval and a corresponding acceptance reference under Pass 07 requirement 14; record only the version, comparison result, and a nonpersonal reference in evidence. Confirm the earlier accepted text remains identifiable and reproducible and no later edit changes its meaning. If no earlier version has been published in production, record that starting condition and retain the isolated prior-version and acceptance exercise in Pass 20 verification 23; do not claim an earlier production version was retrieved. In either case, do not expose personal acceptance information.

5. <a id="p21-v05"></a>**P21.V05 — Read-only or synthetic production inspection**<br>For Pass 16 requirement 7 and the isolated release checks in Pass 20 verification 37, request the actual deployed production privacy notice without signing in and inspect the notice and each collection point named by the recorded launch-state legal decision. Use only read-only page and configuration inspection or a dedicated synthetic account under the production-safety and cleanup conditions of Pass 21 verification 1 where a collection point requires sign-in; do not submit a real registration, payment, or other protected mutation. Record the public address, response, release and notice version, disclosure text and access path, the order of notice availability and collection for registration, payment, authentication persistence, and any enabled analytics or recording, and the inspected production processor and browser configuration.

    **Comparison with approved inventory and isolated release evidence.** Compare the deployed statements and collection order with the approved inventory, applicable legal decision, Pass 20 verification 37 results, and actual production processor settings inspected read-only. Confirm the production notice is publicly accessible without sign-in; each legally required pre-collection disclosure is accessible before that collection begins, including before sign-in where collection begins there; and production analytics remains disabled until disclosures, any required consent, and verified processor configuration permit it.

    **Isolated negative-test evidence and acceptance blockers.** Preserve the isolated negative results for absent or withdrawn consent, an undisclosed collection, and an unapproved event or field as the active behavior evidence; do not repeat those active failure exercises against real production. A missing, inaccessible, mismatched, or late notice, unverified production setting, or observed collection before its required disclosure blocks final acceptance and the affected collection until corrected.

#### Completion condition

Only safe post-deploy checks are run against real production. Production signoff additionally satisfies every item in Section 7; no exception may leave a master requirement or required verification incomplete.

<a id="section-6"></a>

## 6. Explicit Scope Exclusions

The current production-readiness program excludes:

- user-uploaded avatars or preset avatar libraries;
- player or community-host venue-image uploads;
- a new player-reporting workflow;
- granular application administrator roles beyond the current active-administrator policy;
- a generalized workflow engine for financial, moderation, or background-job state;
- a custom authentication service or a second application authentication authority;
- a custom identity or access-management platform spanning Firebase and the other production systems;
- a custom object-storage service in place of R2;
- a custom payment processor in place of Stripe;
- Kafka, a second durable queue, microservice decomposition, sharding, a service mesh, or event sourcing;
- a fairness scheduler for background jobs;
- permanent append-only history for every job heartbeat;
- a blanket rate limiter;
- distributed tracing;
- public API versioning;
- zero-loss recovery for replaceable venue images;
- exposing unrestricted API documentation in production;
- storing payment-card data, authentication secrets, or Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL, hosting, DNS, CI, monitoring, or backup credentials in Pickup Lane application records;
- a custom compliance, test-management, requirement-to-test, trusted-root, automated infrastructure-map scanning, or fault-injection platform;
- a generalized repair framework, storage-management dashboard, audit investigation product, or audit export product;
- a general personal-data export product or generic legal-hold platform;
- moderation assignment, reassignment, reopen, merge, formal note-correction, expanded history-explorer, or generalized notice-suppression workflows;
- duplicate application business-state machines implemented in PostgreSQL procedures; or
- an enterprise incident-management platform or giant alert catalog.

An exclusion prevents accidental expansion. It does not waive a required outcome elsewhere in this document. The blanket-rate-limiter exclusion does not waive the endpoint-family traffic protection in Pass 18 requirement 18; those families may have different controls and limits based on their recorded risk and use. Adding an excluded feature requires an amendment to this master before planning or implementation begins.

<a id="section-7"></a>

## 7. Production Signoff

One release record contains two distinct decisions: authorization to deploy before production deployment and final production acceptance after deployment. Authorization to deploy is not final production acceptance. An exception described as minor or immaterial cannot waive an incomplete master requirement, required verification, or any condition below. Pickup Lane is ready for production only when all of the following are true:

1. Every requirement in this master maps through the Pass 03 verification map to its owning parent pass record and completed evidence that verifies its required results and prohibited outcomes. The release record reconciles all of these links for the exact release. An explicit scope exclusion prevents unrelated work from being added; it does not count as completion of a requirement.
2. The linked pass records and release reconciliation contain no incomplete requirement from this master at final production acceptance. Checks requiring the deployed release may remain explicitly pending at authorization to deploy only as permitted by item 10.
3. Each verified requirement has evidence in the form required by Section 3.2 and points to the immutable release candidate or final production environment it proves.
4. Every setting governed by Section 3.3 has an exact production setting, supporting evidence, a recorded enforcement location, and recorded conditions that require it to be chosen or tested again.
5. Every deferred Firebase, Stripe, Cloudflare R2, PostgreSQL-hosting, application-hosting, DNS/CDN, repository/CI, monitoring, and backup check has been completed through the evidence method assigned by Sections 3.2, 3.3, and 3.5. The evidence must inspect the named production setting and final deployment configuration. An active boundary, failure, capacity, deletion, or recovery exercise that could change production data, create a charge, expose protected information, consume limited production capacity, disrupt service, or make a real request fail must run in an isolated system configured with the final production settings; it must not run against the production resource.
6. Required automated and manual checks pass for the same release candidate.
7. The operational responsibilities and account-recovery paths listed in Pass 01 have the required owners and alternate person or recovery route; application-administrator recovery, alert response, incident command, backups, and restore authority have named owners.
8. The isolated restore, recovery tabletop, capacity exercise, rolling deployment check, rollback or forward-fix check, and production-safe post-deploy checks have recorded successful results.
9. Unresolved failures that can cause unauthorized access, sensitive-data disclosure, duplicate financial or booking effects, lost durable obligations, corrupted capacity, lost audit records, operation without a required request, job, connection, upload, storage, or concurrency limit, or unverified recovery block launch.
10. Before production deployment begins, the release signoff record identifies the release identity, evidence set, required owners and recovery routes, review date, and person who will initiate the deployment. It records authorization to deploy only after all required pre-deployment checks, staging verification, security requirements, release evidence, and recovery preparations are complete for the exact release candidate. Production-safe checks requiring the deployed production release remain explicitly pending and do not prevent the deployment needed to perform them. This authorization cannot complete or override an unmet final-acceptance item above. The deployment result belongs in the execution record after deployment; it is not a prerequisite for authorization to deploy. The same release signoff record records final production acceptance only after the production-safe post-deploy checks and every other applicable item above pass for the deployed release. A failed or incomplete post-deploy check blocks final acceptance. Before the first public launch, ordinary user activity remains blocked until final acceptance.
